\hypertarget{how-to-read-this-document}{%
\section*{How to read this document}\label{how-to-read-this-document}}

This document is kept as a laboratory notebook rather than a specification. It has two parts and one rule.

\textbf{Part I --- the frozen baseline and the decision set as it stood at the freeze} (§1--§19). Part I was written before any Phase 3 implementation, against repository tip \texttt{476c2a0}. \textbf{Part I is never rewritten.} Its claims about package behavior are claims about \texttt{476c2a0} and remain true of \texttt{476c2a0} regardless of what the package does now. Its ``Target'' columns, proposed defaults, and open questions are preserved as the record of what was intended and what was undecided at that moment --- including the parts that were later decided differently.

\textbf{Part II --- the amendment record} (A-1 onward). Every change of fact, decision, or status after the freeze is a numbered, dated, commit-stamped amendment. Part II is \textbf{append-only in the following precise sense}: an entry is opened once, on a dated occasion, and thereafter may only be \emph{appended to} --- by a dated continuation inside the entry, or by a later entry that supersedes it and says so. Text already written is not deleted or rewritten. Several entries below carry more than one date because they were continued in this way; that is the intended operation, not an exception to it. A correction to an entry is itself an appended paragraph, marked as a correction, never an edit of the sentence that was wrong.

\textbf{The rule.} Part I text that a later amendment has overtaken is \emph{marked in place, not corrected in place}. Four marks are used:

\begin{longtable}[]{@{}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\columnwidth - 2\tabcolsep) * \real{0.2600}}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\columnwidth - 2\tabcolsep) * \real{0.7400}}@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
Mark & Meaning \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
\amdref{A-7} & The adjacent statement is still accurate as a statement about the freeze tip, but Part II amendment A-7 records what happened to it afterwards. Read both. \\
\amd{A-7} & The row or item carries text amended in place by A-7 --- the amending text follows the tag. \\
\amdnew{A-7} & The row or item did not exist at the freeze and was added by A-7. \\
\supsd{A-7}{superseded text} & The statement was operative at the freeze and is no longer operative. It is retained, struck, so that the reasoning that produced it stays legible. \\
\end{longtable}

Marked blocks in the amendment colour, set off by rules, are amendments inserted \emph{inside} a frozen section where an in-line tag would not carry enough text.

\textbf{Why this form.} The failure mode this document exists to prevent is a refactor that quietly changes numerical or statistical results. That protection depends on the pre-implementation record staying legible after the implementation exists --- a document silently updated to agree with the code cannot testify against the code. Consequently: no \emph{unmarked} statement of current status appears in Part I. Marked amendment blocks inside Part I do carry status, which is the point of marking them; what Part I contains nowhere is an unmarked sentence whose truth depends on today's date. Status statements in Part II are always dated and stamped with the commit they describe. Reading the document at any future date requires only reading further into Part II, never re-reading a revised Part I.

\textbf{Companion records.} Per-phase acceptance evidence lives in the repository under \texttt{refactor-patches/\allowbreak{}phase3\{a,c,d,e\}/\allowbreak{}rebaseline\_record.md} and the tip-verification and reaudit records named in Part II. Part II summarizes and points; it does not duplicate them.

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\hypertarget{phase-3-baseline-and-decisions}{%
\section{Phase 3 Baseline and Decisions}\label{phase-3-baseline-and-decisions}}

\textbf{Status at the time of writing (2026-07-18): baseline evidence assembled, freeze pending final discharge (§1.2).} The Stage 3 SBC completion record (R = 200, PASS) is archived and committed; the former freeze blocker is discharged. The remaining discharge items are listed in §1.2.\amdref{A-1}

This document freezes the pre-Phase-3 baseline of \texttt{yuxinlg/\allowbreak{}BSTPP-for-illegal-dumping} and records the settled decisions, proposed defaults, open questions, and deferred work governing Phases 3a--3g. It does \textbf{not} implement Phase 3, does not modify package behavior, does not rewrite the mathematical guide, and does not contain the individual commit prompts.

\textbf{Placeholder convention.} Evidence that must still be read off the live environment or produced by a run is marked \texttt{{[}{[}FILL:\ …{]}{]}}, each with its discharge procedure. Code claims verified against the tip in this document cite \texttt{file:line\ @\ 476c2a0}.

\textbf{Claim-status vocabulary} (used throughout §5--§6):

\begin{longtable}[]{@{}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\columnwidth - 2\tabcolsep) * \real{0.5000}}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\columnwidth - 2\tabcolsep) * \real{0.5000}}@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
\begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Label
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Meaning
\end{minipage} \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
\textbf{verified (tip)} & Confirmed against code at commit \texttt{476c2a0} (refactor tip, 2026-07-18), with file:line anchor, or against the committed run records \\
\textbf{verified (2c record)} & Verified at the Phase 2c tip per the consolidation document; not re-verified at the tip where so marked \\
\textbf{inferred} & Inferred from code reading or design records, not yet verified by execution \\
\textbf{known defect} & Confirmed deviation from intended behavior \\
\textbf{declared approximation} & Intentional, documented deviation of computation from model \\
\textbf{untested} & No test exercises this path \\
\end{longtable}

Design documents (the guide, the consolidation document, this document) are \textbf{not} proof of current implementation behavior; where this document asserts tip behavior it cites the code or the committed run records directly.

\hypertarget{working-agreement-note}{%
\subsubsection{1.3 Working-agreement note}\label{working-agreement-note}}

\texttt{CLAUDE.md}'s prime directive (``refactoring changes structure, never external behavior'') is retained for every commit classified \emph{behavior-preserving structural extraction}. Phase 3 also contains commits that intentionally change semantics; those are governed by the change classification of §7 and the sign-off protocol, and must never be described in behavior-preserving language. This is a scoped amendment to the working agreement, not a suspension of it.

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\hypertarget{scope-and-non-goals}{%
\subsection{2. Scope and non-goals}\label{scope-and-non-goals}}

\textbf{In scope (Phase 3):} the preparation freeze; 3a data contracts and unique membership; 3b domain/partition seam extraction; 3c background/covariate polygon support corrections; 3d polygon excitation support modes; 3e human-unit interface and computational cutoffs; 3f architecture rewrite completion; 3g verification and documentation. The approved 3a--3g sequence and each phase's scope boundary (§10) are preserved as given; scope creep across phase boundaries is prohibited.

\textbf{Non-goals:}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Whole-city high-resolution or multiresolution inference (architecture must not preclude it; no city-mode abstractions are built now).
\item
  \texttt{ReportingRegions} and \texttt{ComputationPartition} as classes (deferred vocabulary; §10.2).
\item
  Arbitrary user-supplied standardization weights (deferred to the covariate-pipeline phase).
\item
  Rewriting the mathematical guide (§15 specifies the amendment; a separate task edits it).
\item
  Decoder retraining and the missing seasonal-decoder provenance recovery (recorded, not solved).
\item
  Any change to the research pipeline repository beyond consuming the new interfaces.
\end{itemize}

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

C1 & \textbf{Truncated kernels as scientific model vs.~infinite-support target.} The guide \emph{defines} the kernels with truncation (eq. \texttt{tkernel}, \texttt{skernel-trunc}), treats the pair set and compensator as ``jointly the exact likelihood of the truncated-kernel model,'' and classifies truncation as taxonomy category (b) with ``nothing being approximated.'' Team decision: the scientific target uses infinite-support kernels; finite windows are computational cutoffs that remove regions contributing negligible offspring mass, purely to reduce cost. & Guide §Model, §Likelihood, §Taxonomy vs.~D-13/D-14 & \textbf{Resolved: intentional specification revision} (settled 2026-07-18). Affected guide sections inventoried in §15. \\
C2 & \textbf{Disc vs.~per-axis-square spatial cutoff.} The guide's spatial truncation is a radial disc, ‖Δs‖ ≤ w\_s, with tail mass Q(w\_s) = 1 − exp(−w\_s²/2σ²); its pair set and excitation compensator are disc-based. The implementation (verified at tip, §5.13) uses a per-axis real-unit square, max(\textbar Δx\textbar,\textbar Δy\textbar) ≤ w\_s, single-sourced across all three legs. & Guide eqs. \texttt{pairset}, \texttt{skernel-trunc}, \texttt{exc-compensator} vs.~\texttt{bstpp/utils.py:71} @ tip & \textbf{Resolved: per-axis square adopted (D-21, settled 2026-07-18).} Since the window is a computational cutoff excising near-zero offspring mass (C1), its exact shape is a convenience; the square is the only shape whose Gaussian mass intersected with a rectangle is closed-form, and it mirrors min(T − t, w) per axis. The guide's disc equations are revised as part of the §15 addendum. \\
C3 & \textbf{Prime directive vs.~intentional semantic changes.} & \texttt{CLAUDE.md} vs.~Phase 3 plan & \textbf{Resolved} by scoped amendment (§1.3) and change classification (§7). \\
C4 & \textbf{Guide's rectangle-mass classification vs.~dual-mode plan.} The guide classifies the bounding-rectangle excitation mass as a category-(c) approximation with known sign; Phase 3d elevates polygon-support mass to a co-equal supported mode. & Guide §Taxonomy vs.~3d & Consistent extension, but §Taxonomy text needs the per-mode framing (§15). \\
C5 & \textbf{Consolidation drift.} The consolidation document is compiled at the Phase 2c tip; the real-unit trigger patch series and the SBC-program commits landed after it. Its coordinate-system section (internal-unit σ\_x², aspect-ratio \emph{trigger} geometry) is superseded: the tip's spatial trigger is a real-unit object (§5.10), and the historical internal-draw rescale --- the aspect-ratio anisotropy defect --- is removed (\texttt{bstpp/main.py} offspring comment, tip). The \emph{background} decoder anisotropy remains (§5.10). & Consolidation §Coords vs.~tip & Superseded claims flagged in §5; remaining 2c-record claims marked as such. \\
C6 & \textbf{Guide version identity.} The source hierarchy names \texttt{bstpp\_guide\_v4.pdf}; the project working copy is \texttt{bstpp\_guide.tex}. & --- & \texttt{{[}{[}FILL:\ confirm\ bstpp\_guide.tex\ compiles\ to\ /\ is\ content-identical\ with\ bstpp\_guide\_v4.pdf;\ record\ the\ guide\textquotesingle{}s\ commit{]}{]}} \\
\end{longtable}

3 & full Cox--Hawkes, unit gain & \textbf{200} & \textbf{PASS} & \texttt{results/sbc\_stage3/} & \texttt{9d8fbbb9…} & \texttt{5da5784} & \texttt{51bc297}, \texttt{476c2a0} \\
\end{longtable}

Notes:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Stages 1--2 carry \textbf{pre-run deviation notes}: R = 600 is a deliberate upward deviation from the pre-registered 100--200 range, decided before each run, for power only. Stage 3's R = 200 is exactly the top of the pre-registered range; no deviation note applies.
\item
  Stage 3 detail: L = 127; 12 primary functionals; decision rule ``every primary mc\_p ≥ 0.004'' (Bonferroni family-wise ≤ 4.8\%); minimum primary p = 0.092 (\texttt{z\_temporal\_0}, no U/dome shape); zero ties; zero divergent transitions over all 200 fits (divergent replicates are never dropped, pre-registered); zero ESS retries (max raw draws 508); minimum quantile ESS 141.15 (tightest primary, \texttt{alpha}) vs.~the 120.65 gate.
\item
  \textbf{Scope of the SBC evidence}: unit gain (\texttt{UNIT\_\allowbreak{}GAIN\_\allowbreak{}SP\_\allowbreak{}VAR\_\allowbreak{}MU\ =\ 0.0}), \textbf{rectangular unit-box domain, \texttt{spatial\_window=None}}, NUTS. PASS means no calibration deviation was detected at this resolution; it is not proof of correctness, \textbf{not} evidence of polygon-mode calibration, and not evidence about the production-gain prior regime (recorded as a modeling finding at stage 2).
\item
  The SBC-supported regime at the baseline is therefore: rectangular domains, unit gain, with the finite-w\_s legs covered structurally by the identity suite (I4-w\_s, I11-w\_s) rather than by a dedicated SBC run.
\item
  Mirrors of all three completed records live beside the pins in \texttt{refactor-patches/\allowbreak{}baselines-2026-07/\allowbreak{}}.
\item
  \textbf{Exit-criterion clarification}: the 2026-07-18 Stage 3 record is the \emph{baseline} record. The Phase 3 \textbf{exit} criterion (D-20, §12) requires a \emph{new} Stage 3 R = 200 run at the Phase 3 tip; the baseline record is what it will be compared against for regressions in diagnostics (divergences, ESS, budget behavior).
\end{itemize}

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

Meanwhile the excitation compensator charges bounding-rectangle mass (\texttt{rectangular\_\allowbreak{}excitation\_\allowbreak{}compensator}, \texttt{likelihood.py:120–168}). In-code rationale: ``per-axis box semantics are the only choice the excitation compensator can charge exactly (per-axis erf limits clipped at w\_s --- the Gaussian mass of disc-intersect-rectangle has no closed form), exactly mirroring min(T − t, w) temporally.'' & \textbf{verified (tip)} & \texttt{bstpp/utils.py:71–86}; \texttt{bstpp/\allowbreak{}likelihood.py:143–152}; \texttt{bstpp/main.py:1977–1983} \\
Tail-mass formula for the square cutoff (adopted with D-21): ε\_s = 1 − erf(w\_s/(√2 σ))². The disc formula ε\_s = exp(−w\_s²/(2σ²)) applies only to the guide's superseded disc equations and must not be mixed in. & settled (D-21) & --- \\
\end{longtable}

\hypertarget{known-defects-approximations-and-evidence-gaps}{%
\subsection{6. Known defects, approximations, and evidence gaps}\label{known-defects-approximations-and-evidence-gaps}}

\textbf{Known defects (fail-fast gaps and misbehavior):}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Silent NaN-row dropping} at ingestion (\texttt{main.py:603}) --- verified at tip; 3a converts this to loud rejection (a \emph{stricter} IV change than the draft assumed: current behavior silently alters the event set).\amdref{A-2} \amd{A-2} the \texttt{main.py:603} anchor is the \textbf{held-out scoring} path; the \emph{constructor} never dropped NaN rows, it crashed downstream in the membership \texttt{sjoin}. Both are closed by 3a.
\item
  Grid-line ingestion crash with misleading message (§5.1) --- fixed by 3a.\amdref{A-2}
\item
  Silent out-of-polygon event acceptance: likelihood credit without compensator debit (§5.1) --- fixed by 3a.\amdref{A-2}
\item
  No malformed-geometry/CRS contracts at the boundary (§5.2) --- fixed by 3a.\amdref{A-2, A-16}
\item
  Covariate gaps silently become zero-valued covariates (§5.6) --- fixed by 3c.\amdref{A-5}
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{Declared approximations at the baseline (each documented at its site):}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\setcounter{enumi}{5}
\tightlist
\item
  Excitation compensator mass over A\_□, not A (§5.12) --- becomes an explicit \emph{mode} in 3d.\amdref{A-9, A-10}
\item
  Full-area boundary cells in the no-covariate Cox background (§5.5) --- corrected in 3c.\amdref{A-5}
\item
  Covariate support = covariate polygons, not C\_c ∩ A\_m ∩ A (§5.6) --- corrected in 3c.\amdref{A-5}
\item
  Background PriorVAE real-space anisotropy (§5.10) --- documented, not patched in Phase 3.
\item
  PriorVAE variance shrinkage (prior-side overconfidence) --- documented; out of Phase 3 scope. The unit-gain SBC scope (§4.5) additionally means production-gain calibration is an open scientific question, not a Phase 3 deliverable.
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{Evidence gaps:}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\setcounter{enumi}{10}
\tightlist

  \st{Stage 3 R = 200 record} --- \textbf{discharged} (§1.2).
\item
  \st{Cutoff geometry at tip} --- \textbf{discharged}: per-axis square, verified (§5.13).
\item
  \st{Nonrectangular parenting/compensator asymmetry} --- \textbf{discharged}: verified structurally at tip (§5.11).
\item
  \st{\mbox{\texttt{aligned\_\allowbreak{}difference\_\allowbreak{}pairs}} global-pair-set property} --- \textbf{discharged}: verified (§5.8).
\item
  Fresh suite + pin run at \texttt{476c2a0}; pipeline snapshot; env export (§1.2 items 1--4).\amdref{A-1}
\item
  Remaining §5 \texttt{{[}{[}FILL{]}{]}} anchors (2c-record rows).\amdref{A-1}
\item
  Seasonal decoder provenance UNKNOWN (constrains 3f decoder contract; not invented).
\item
  District geometry availability for the 3d polygon-mass diagnostic --- external dependency; does not block this document. \texttt{{[}{[}FILL:\ status{]}{]}} \amd{A-8} discharged: 18 planning districts (127,590 events) and 6 park polygons obtained and exercised; three park geometries were invalid and required \texttt{make\_valid} --- recorded as an upstream data defect.
\end{enumerate}

\hypertarget{change-classifications}{%
\subsection{7. Change classifications}\label{change-classifications}}

\hypertarget{computational-devices}{%
\subsubsection{7.1 Computational devices}\label{computational-devices}}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{(a) Exact reformulation} --- same output, different computation.
\item
  \textbf{(b) Declared model substitution} --- a different, fully declared model.
\item
  \textbf{(c) Genuine numerical approximation} --- computed quantity ≠ model quantity, gap characterized.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Specification-revision consequence (C1 + C2):} under D-13/D-14/D-21, the finite windows move from the guide's category-(b) framing (``the model itself'') to \textbf{declared computational cutoffs of category (c)}: computed quantity ≠ scientific-model quantity, with the omitted tail mass --- temporal ε\_t = e\^{}\{−w/β\}, spatial ε\_s = 1 − erf(w\_s/(√2 σ))² --- as the characterized gap, applied consistently to event term, compensator, and simulator. §15 records the guide edits.

D-20 & Stage 3 SBC at R = 200 (full Cox--Hawkes) is the mandatory statistical Phase 3 exit criterion; policy verbatim in §12.1 & validation & team \\
\textbf{D-21} & \textbf{Spatial cutoff geometry is the per-axis square in real units} (max(\textbar Δx\textbar,\textbar Δy\textbar) ≤ w\_s), in all three legs. Rationale: the window is a computational cutoff (D-14) excising near-zero offspring mass, so its shape is a convenience choice; the square is the only shape with a closed-form Gaussian mass against rectangle limits, and it mirrors the temporal min(T − t, w) per axis. Tail formula ε\_s = 1 − erf(w\_s/(√2 σ))²; the disc formula is retired with the guide's disc equations (§15). Matches the verified tip implementation (§5.13) and the SBC-validated legs. & spec revision (C2) & team, 2026-07-18 \\
\textbf{D-22} & \textbf{Half-open convention: left-closed/right-open per axis, {[}e\_k, e\_\{k+1\}), with the outermost right/top edge closed} so the partition covers the domain exactly. Temporal axis: {[}t\_k, t\_\{k+1\}) with t = T̃ closed into the last cell; seasonal axis: seamless on the circle; spatial axes: {[}x\_k, x\_\{k+1\}) per axis with the domain's max-x/max-y edges closed. Direction delegated to implementation by the reviewer (2026-07-18); chosen to match \texttt{searchsorted(...,\ side=\textquotesingle{}right\textquotesingle{})}-style semantics and the existing temporal-pair convention (Δt = w included, Δt = 0 excluded). Content of the 3a micro-rebaseline (D-5). & contract (MR content) & team delegation, 2026-07-18 \\
\amdnew{A-9} \textbf{D-23} & \textbf{On a nonrectangular domain, the excitation support mode must be stated explicitly}; there is no default. Resolves OP-2 as proposed. Rationale: the mode is a modeling commitment, and a silent default reproduces exactly the undeclared-approximation problem Phase 3 exists to remove. Axis-aligned array domains default to \texttt{rectangle}, where the two modes coincide. & architecture / contract & team; implemented \texttt{edfce53} \\
\amdnew{A-13} \textbf{D-24} & \textbf{Cutoffs are selected from a tolerance on omitted mass, with a physical override, and both are recorded in provenance.} Resolves OP-6 as proposed. Package default tolerances reproduce the historical rules of thumb: ε\_t = e\^{}\{−5\} (the w ≥ 5β rule) and ε\_s = 1 − erf(4/√2)² (the w\_s ≥ 4σ rule). Every supplied tolerance is validated as finite and in (0,1) \emph{before} precedence is applied, so a malformed tolerance cannot be silently retained behind a winning physical cutoff. & interface / spec (C1) & team; implemented \texttt{2ce665c}, \texttt{2a7c558} \\
\amdnew{A-9} \textbf{D-25} & \textbf{Polygon excitation mass is evaluated from a precomputed C¹ cubic Hermite table in log σ} (K = 64 log-uniform knots over the declared σ range), built offline by fixed-node boundary quadrature in host float64, with a loud no-extrapolation contract. Resolves OP-9. Accuracy budget and its derivation are in A-9. The alternatives were rejected on measured grounds, not preference: direct JAX boundary quadrature is oracle-exact but ≈ 28.5 s per warm gradient at n = 16{,}479 and fails in float32; oracle-built PCHIP tables pass on values but fail the derivative gate at every K. & numerical method & team; \texttt{62bde1f}, \texttt{edfce53} \\
\amdnew{A-10} \textbf{D-26} & \textbf{Polygon mass tables are prepared explicitly and hard-required at construction.} The package never builds one silently, never rebuilds one on incompatibility, and never mutates global JAX precision to do so. A spatial-window change that invalidates the installed table requires a compatible replacement passed to \texttt{set\_window}. Rationale: a silently rebuilt table hides both a multi-minute cost and a change of the numerical object the likelihood is evaluated against. & API / contract & team; \texttt{ae27947}, \texttt{8580364} \\
\amdnew{A-11} \textbf{D-27} & \textbf{Equal event counts are not compatibility.} A supplied mass table is valid only against the recorded canonical domain-union geometry, event coordinates \emph{and row order}, event count, realized spatial window, σ range and knot grid, interpolation convention, panel height, quadrature order, and backend schema version, with all duplicated metadata consistent between artifact and sidecar. Event identity is a hash of little-endian float64 coordinates; decimal-formatted identities are rejected because distinct float64 events collided under them. & contract & team; \texttt{1adeb00}, \texttt{ce5508f}, \texttt{90d45bb} \\
\amdnew{A-12} \textbf{D-28} & \textbf{Truncating the \texttt{sigmax\_2} prior to the table's σ interval is a declared scientific change (SC), not a numerical device.} It alters prior and posterior support and is required by the no-extrapolation contract of D-25. Truncation is exact (normalized, samplable, batched, JIT-safe) for the named prior families only; other families raise rather than being approximated. Where the supplied prior is already supported inside the interval, truncation is behaviorally inert. Bounds must appear in model provenance, and the change requires semantic tests and a rebaseline record. & model / spec & team; \texttt{edfce53} \\
\amdnew{A-12} \textbf{D-29} & \textbf{σ bounds are use-case quantities: they must be determined by the analyst for the phenomenon and domain at hand, and whatever value is in force must appear in model provenance identified as user-supplied or package-supplied.} A plausible range for an excitation length scale is a property of the phenomenon, not of the software. \textbf{What is settled here is the principle and the disclosure requirement.} Whether the package may nevertheless carry a convenience default --- \texttt{max\_sigma} presently falls back to 5\,km in polygon mode --- is \emph{not} settled and is registered as OP-10. D-29 does not by itself authorize or forbid that default. & interface & team, 2026-07-27 \\
\amdnew{A-6} \textbf{D-30} & \textbf{One canonical domain geometry.} \texttt{PreparedDomain.union\_geometry} is the single set-union of the supplied domain rows, and every downstream consumer (partitions, covariate refinement, excitation support, mass-table identity) uses that object rather than recomputing a union. Domain area is the area of that union, never the sum of row areas. & invariant & team; \texttt{05da465}, \texttt{2a67962} \\
\amdnew{A-2} \textbf{D-31} & \textbf{Data-contract enforcement is on by default}; \texttt{data\_contracts='report'} is a migration instrument that preserves legacy behavior bit-for-bit for dry runs and is to be removed once the pipeline has migrated. It is not a permanent public mode. & contract & team, 2026-07-20 \\
\amdnew{A-15} \textbf{D-32} & \textbf{Held-out data are a complete standalone realization} over the model domain and horizon --- for example a different year at the same location --- not a subset of a split, which would sever the parent structure the Hawkes term depends on. All state derived from parent events is therefore rebuilt from that realization: excitation pairs, and in polygon mode the rows M\_A(s\_j, σ) of the excitation-mass table. Event term and compensator both use the held-out realization's own times and locations; no training-event-indexed row is reused. This is a correctness requirement of the existing held-out likelihood, not a change to its scientific target. & model / contract & team; \texttt{8c4a702}, \texttt{8580364} \\
\amdnew{A-14} \textbf{D-33} & \textbf{Public state mutation is transactional.} Validation, pair construction, cutoff resolution, support construction, provenance, and mass-table installation are prepared in local state and committed once; a failure at any step leaves every observable field unchanged. Rationale: a half-applied window change is the silent-corruption mode this refactor was convened to eliminate --- the object would report one cutoff and integrate another. & invariant & team; \texttt{798f551}, \texttt{8580364} \\
\amdnew{A-16} \textbf{D-34} & \textbf{CRS is never adopted, overridden, or inferred silently.} A CRS-bearing domain requires covariates to declare a matching CRS; tabular (CSV / plain \texttt{DataFrame}) covariates require an explicit \texttt{spatial\_cov\_crs}; a mismatch reaching internal attachment raises rather than being rewritten. Rationale: the real-unit trigger contract (D-15, §5.10) is meaningless if coordinates can be reinterpreted without the user's knowledge. & contract & team; \texttt{c22aa51}, \texttt{adea3d3}, \texttt{2cf326d} \\
\amdnew{A-22} \textbf{D-35} & \textbf{Every freeze ships with its enforcement, in the same commit.} A configuration object that does not validate its own invariants at construction is not done: validation lives in \texttt{\_\_post\_init\_\_} behind a single factory per object, not at scattered call sites. Applies to the Phase~3f frozen configs (starting with \texttt{NumericalConfig}: measured mass-table budget policy, $\sigma$-bound coherence, support-mode compatibility). & invariant / process & team; Phase 3f opening \\
\amdnew{A-24} \textbf{D-36} & \textbf{Phase blocker test.} An issue blocks opening the next phase if and only if it can change the likelihood event term, the compensator, the simulator, the accepted configuration space, a public state transition, provenance content or persistence, the validity of a verification gate, or the surface the next phase will freeze (clauses B1--B6 of \texttt{refactor-patches/pre-3f-stabilization/phase3e\_closeout\_and\_3f\_readiness.tex} §3.1, generalized from ``3f'' to ``the next phase''). \emph{Corollary, stated because it is easily lost}: a defect on a path over which a pin or trace baseline of that next phase is taken is a blocker regardless of severity, because deterministic trace equivalence would certify its preservation. Severity is not the criterion; position relative to the pins and the freeze surface is. & invariant / process & team; A-24 \\
\amdnew{A-24} \textbf{D-37} & \textbf{Finite readiness rule (principle).} A phase opening is governed by a pre-registered closed readiness rule evaluated on one frozen candidate tip; no condition of the form ``and no further defect is known'' may appear in it; termination is bounded by a stated iteration cap; the instance is recorded in the amendment that opens the phase. The Phase~3f instance (R1--R9) is recorded in A-24, not in this decision. & process & team; A-24 \\
\amdnew{A-24} \textbf{D-38} & \textbf{Compatibility and state-transition matrix as a standing gate.} The executable Lane~B gate --- \texttt{tests/test\_lane\_b\_config\_matrix.py} together with \texttt{refactor-patches/pre-3f-stabilization/covering\_array\_rows.json} --- is added to the §12 acceptance matrix as a mandatory gate (\textbf{M}) for every commit that changes the constructor signature, \texttt{set\_window}, the trigger or support selection logic, the cutoff interface, or provenance construction; and as a mandatory exit gate (\textbf{E}) at the Phase~3 tip alongside D-20's Stage~3 SBC run. It does not replace the pins, the SBC gates, or the polygon conservation tests; its sensitivity is near-disjoint from all three. & validation / process & team; A-24 \\
\amdnew{A-24} \textbf{D-39} & \textbf{Coverage is recorded; absence of findings is not evidence.} Two artifacts are part of the development record and are maintained thereafter: a register-to-test traceability matrix (\texttt{refactor-patches/pre-3f-stabilization/traceability\_matrix.md}; closeout §7 design origin) and an audit coverage map (\texttt{refactor-patches/pre-3f-stabilization/audit\_coverage\_map.md}; closeout §8 design origin). ``Unaudited'' is a recorded state rather than an implicit claim, and a clean audit is interpretable only against the coverage it declares. \emph{Enforcement:} every commit that moves a production site updates its traceability rows in the same commit; a row whose check no longer exists after a move is a finding, not a formatting issue. & process / contract & team; A-24 \\
\amdnew{A-26} \textbf{D-40} & \textbf{One invariant, one error identity, one message, independent of entry path.} A configuration object owns the invariants it declares; every other call site \emph{delegates} to it rather than restating the check. The error type and the canonical invariant clause are produced by exactly one function and are byte-identical wherever the violation is detected; a site may append a context-specific \emph{remediation} clause after that shared text, because build time (``I will not build this'') and install time (``this table does not match this model'') are different situations with different useful advice, but it may not restate the invariant. Ordering must stop mattering: whichever check runs first, the same error surfaces. \emph{Corollary on encoding:} raised message strings are ASCII. A non-ASCII character can raise \texttt{UnicodeEncodeError} while the traceback carrying it is being printed to a legacy-codepage console --- an error path that fails while failing. Docstrings, comments, and this register keep σ and set notation; only strings that reach a console are constrained. Written to govern all five Phase~3f configuration objects, not only \texttt{NumericalConfig}: WP2--WP5 multiply the entry paths, and a split pattern in place when they land is replicated four more times. \amdnew{A-27} \emph{Refinement (A-27), not a new decision number.} \supsd{A-27}{Every other call site \emph{delegates} to it.} An invariant has one identity and one canonical clause, but it may have more than one \emph{owner}, and the test of whether a second owner is legitimate is whether the owners validate the same \emph{quantity}. Where a value is defaulted, the pre-default and post-default quantities differ, and an invariant expressible on one can be inexpressible on the other. The sigma/mode family is the worked case: rectangle both-or-neither and polygon-requires-\texttt{min\_sigma} are \emph{argument} invariants testing which argument was omitted, and defaulting erases that distinction, so only \texttt{resolve\_sigma\_bounds} can express them; \texttt{min\_sigma} positivity and \texttt{min\_sigma} $<$ \texttt{max\_sigma} are \emph{resolved-bound} invariants that \texttt{NumericalConfig} already validates on the right quantity. Delegating everything to the config \emph{after} resolution --- the reading D-40 invited --- would have silently dropped the first two: a \textbf{reject$\rightarrow$accept} change on two of the five invariants. The obligation is therefore one identity and one clause per invariant, produced by one function; more than one call site is always permitted, and more than one \emph{owner} is permitted only where the quantities genuinely differ, which must be stated where it is claimed. & invariant / API & team; A-26, refined A-27 \\
\amdnew{A-31} \textbf{D-41} & \textbf{Evidence is demonstrated, and a commit is verified against a pre-stated file list.} \emph{Enforcement.} Every enforcement row is demonstrated \textsc{red} against the pre-change state before it is claimed to enforce anything, and the capture is committed. The revert is \emph{minimal} --- revert only what the row is meant to detect; reverting shared API alongside it yields an \texttt{ImportError} at collection, a red that proves nothing. Rows that pass on both sides are recorded as \emph{non-discriminating by construction} and are never offered as evidence. \emph{Provenance.} Every capture records the checked process's own exit status, not a pipeline's; a capture showing success beside a failure is worse than no capture. After committing, \texttt{git status -{}-porcelain} is empty \emph{and} \texttt{git show -{}-stat HEAD} is checked against the file list stated before the commit was made --- a commit whose contents were not compared to a pre-stated list is \textbf{ungated regardless of what the gates said}. \texttt{stderr} is never routed to \texttt{/dev/null} on a git command. \emph{Corollary (relabeling).} Relabeling in place is permitted where no assertion changes and the head note carries the full old$\rightarrow$new mapping; anything that changes an assertion uses \texttt{\textbackslash supsd} per occurrence. & process / evidence & team; A-31
\\
\end{longtable}

\textbf{Superseded statements (do not carry forward unqualified):}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  ``Every offspring leaving A is discarded before parenting'' --- applies \textbf{only} to polygon-support mode; rectangle-approximation mode may allow R ~A cascade participation as its declared approximation.
\item
  ``The diagnostic decides whether polygon mass is built'' --- \textbf{obsolete} (D-17).
\item
  ``SBC scope will be selected after seeing which paths changed'' --- \textbf{obsolete}; replaced by §12.1.
\item
  The guide's disc-based w\_s equations (pair set, Q(w\_s), disc compensator factor) --- \textbf{superseded by D-21}; revision in §15.
\item
  \amdnew{A-9} ``The §13 diagnostic decides the accuracy target'' --- \textbf{refined}: the accuracy target is derived from the D-21 cutoff budget (A-9), not from the diagnostic. The diagnostic decides \emph{when rectangle mode is defensible}, and its answer is regime-dependent (A-8).
\item
  \amdnew{A-12} ``Spatial quantities pass through 3e unchanged'' (§10.e) --- \textbf{qualified}: true of units, but polygon mode constrains the σ \emph{prior} (D-28), which is a spatial-side change 3d introduced and 3e did not.
\item
  \amdnew{A-10} ``Rectangle mode retained, numerics within the mode unchanged'' --- \textbf{holds}, and is the load-bearing claim behind every ``pins bit-identical'' gate recorded in Part II.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Boundary convention (settled, D-22):} left-closed {[}e\_k, e\_\{k+1\}) per axis; outermost right/top edge closed; t = T̃ closed into the last temporal cell; seasonal circle seamless. Complete coverage: no valid event can fall through the last edge.

\textbf{Classification (recorded):} the half-open convention and unique-membership rule are an \textbf{intentional micro-rebaseline} for otherwise valid events exactly on grid or cell boundaries --- not merely improved invalid-input handling. Interior events remain bit-unchanged.

\textbf{Required evidence:} adversarial tests for polygon-boundary points, grid-line points (each axis; corner = both), holes, multipolygons, duplicate-join constructions; a no-change test for interior events; a rejection test for each contract; a regression test replacing the silent \texttt{dropna} with loud rejection.

\begin{amendment}{A-2 --- 10.a as implemented}{2026-07-20 -- 2026-07-27}
Implemented as staged enforcement rather than a single flip: the validation layer landed report-only, the §14 dry run ran against the project data (A-3), and the default moved to \texttt{reject} only on sign-off of that report. Two facts differ from the frozen text. First, the ``NaN rows silently dropped'' row describes the \emph{held-out} path; the constructor crashed rather than dropping (A-2). Second, unique membership was obtained by deduplicating the existing \texttt{sjoin} by maximum cell id rather than by switching to floor arithmetic --- chosen precisely so that every strictly-interior event passes through bit-identically and no ULP-scale reassignment near an edge is possible. Later contract additions: finite positive horizon, polygonal positive-area domain, and the CRS contract (A-16). \texttt{report} mode is a migration instrument only (D-31).
\end{amendment}

\hypertarget{b-extract-the-domainpartition-seam}{%
\subsubsection{10.b Extract the domain/partition seam}\label{b-extract-the-domainpartition-seam}}

Narrow, behavior-preserving extraction (BP) \textbf{before} polygon calculations change. Exactly three data-bearing objects:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{ModelData} --- events, domain input, dates, covariate sources.
\item
  \textbf{PreparedDomain} --- validated model geometry, bounding rectangle, CRS/unit information, unit transforms.
\item
  \textbf{PreparedPartitions} --- field cells, clipped/intersection geometries, common refinements, areas, index maps, seasonal overlap matrix W.
\end{itemize}

Do \textbf{not} introduce six unused classes. Deferred design vocabulary (contracts, not classes): \emph{ModelDomain} (contract statement about the scientific/observed domain); \emph{ReportingRegions} (deferred until city/reporting functionality consumes them); \emph{ComputationPartition} (deferred until computational tiling has a consumer).

\textbf{Invariants recorded now (binding on all future work):}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  An independent district fit uses the district as its model domain.
\item
  A citywide fit uses the city as its model domain.
\item
  Districts within a citywide fit are reporting regions, not process boundaries.
\item
  Independent district fits exclude cross-district excitation by choosing separate model domains.
\item
  A citywide fit includes cross-district excitation.
\item
  Future computational tiling must not change model semantics.
\item
  Excitation pairs are a function of the global event set and cutoff/window only --- never restricted by computation tiles. \textbf{Verified satisfied at the baseline} (\texttt{bstpp/utils.py:89}, §5.8).
\end{enumerate}

The legacy \texttt{args} dictionary may remain temporarily behind an adapter; it must not be the final canonical state. Its removal commit is open until 3f is designed (OP-8).

\begin{amendment}{A-4 --- 10.b as implemented}{2026-07-20}
Three objects, no more, extracted verbatim; \texttt{args} entries are assigned the seam objects' fields \emph{themselves} rather than copies, so the adapter cannot drift from the seam. Invariant 10.b.7 was given a negative-constraint test: the signature of \texttt{aligned\_difference\_pairs} is pinned, so any future attempt to pass a tile or partition argument fails a test and forces a recorded decision rather than silently restricting the pair set. The deferred vocabulary (\emph{ModelDomain}, \emph{ReportingRegions}, \emph{ComputationPartition}) was not introduced.
\end{amendment}

\textbf{Standardization --- settled principles (D-10..D-12):} primarily preprocessing outside BSTPP; pre-standardized values accepted unchanged; BSTPP reports whether/how it standardized; if an in-package convenience remains, area weighting is the implementable one (exact intersection areas exist) but not scientifically mandatory; exposure weighting normally upstream; population standardization on original census units pre-subdivision.

\textbf{Standardization API --- proposed, not settled (OP-3/OP-4/OP-5):} default off; explicit method string (\texttt{"domain\_area"}); arbitrary user weights deferred. \textbf{These proposals do not gate 3c}: 3c can land preserving explicit input values with a narrow explicit convenience interface; the API question resolves before 3f freezes configuration objects.

\begin{amendment}{A-5 --- 10.c as implemented}{2026-07-20}
Landed as five commits split by class, with the one-support-object pattern honored: the clipped C\_c ∩ A geometries feed likelihood integration, background sampling, \emph{and} event-to-cell membership, so the three legs cannot disagree about where domain mass lives. The coverage contract exports the offending geometry, not only the failing index. One transitional window was accepted and closed deliberately: between the refinement change and the coverage contract --- exactly one commit --- a covariate gap inside A silently left the refinement. On standardization, only the reporting requirement (D-10) and the explicit \texttt{'domain\_area'} convenience (D-11) landed; \textbf{OP-3 and OP-4 remain open}, so the default is still the legacy count-weighted z-score. The API question is deferred to 3f as the frozen text anticipates.
\end{amendment}

\hypertarget{d-polygon-excitation-support-modes}{%
\subsubsection{10.d Polygon excitation support modes}\label{d-polygon-excitation-support-modes}}

\textbf{Scientific contract (D-2/D-3, D-13/D-14):} A is the scientific and observed domain; observed events outside A rejected; final reported/simulated events lie in A; infinite-support kernels are the scientific target.

\textbf{Two supported computational modes on nonrectangular domains (D-17):}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Bounding-rectangle excitation approximation.} May allow offspring in R ~A to participate in the cascade before final observation-domain filtering, \emph{if declared}; must charge the matching rectangle-support mass; labeled explicitly approximate for nonrectangular A; mode recorded in fit and simulation provenance. (This is the tip's verified behavior, §5.11--5.12, made explicit and opt-in.)
\item
  \textbf{Polygon-domain excitation mass/support.} Offspring leaving A are discarded before parenting; compensator charges mass over A, subject to the declared cutoff approximation and numerical accuracy; numerical method, tolerance, interpolation behavior, and error control documented and tested.
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{Per-mode consistency invariant (D-18):} within a mode, the offspring parenting-eligibility region ≡ the compensator charge region. Mixing a rectangle simulator leg with a polygon compensator leg (or vice versa) must be \textbf{structurally impossible} --- one support object feeds both legs, following the tip's single-sourced-predicate pattern for w\_s (§5.9), which is the template.

\textbf{Rectangle degeneracy (mandatory acceptance test):} when A is an axis-aligned rectangle, the two modes coincide (within declared tolerance; bit-identical where operation ordering permits).

\textbf{Default selection --- proposed, not settled (OP-2):} nonrectangular Hawkes/Cox--Hawkes domain with no support mode specified → \textbf{loud error requiring an explicit choice}. Not silently adopted without reviewer acceptance.

\textbf{Polygon-mass diagnostic (specification in §13):} informs the accuracy target, precomputation/interpolation vs.~direct quadrature, runtime expectations, and guidance on when rectangle mode is adequate. It does \textbf{not} determine whether polygon mode exists. District geometry availability is an external dependency (§6.18).

\begin{amendment}{A-10 --- 10.d as implemented}{2026-07-22 -- 2026-07-27}
Both modes exist, OP-2 resolved as proposed (D-23), and D-18 is enforced structurally by a single \texttt{ExcitationSupport} object feeding parenting and compensator. Three things the frozen text did not anticipate. \textbf{(i)} Polygon mass is restricted to the \emph{exact} built-in Gaussian spatial trigger, by type identity rather than \texttt{isinstance}, because the Hermite table integrates that kernel and a subclass overriding the event kernel would produce a likelihood whose event term and compensator describe different processes. This is a placeholder, not a permanent restriction (OP-11). \textbf{(ii)} The table must be prepared explicitly and is hard-required at construction (D-26) --- an API change, not behavior preservation. \textbf{(iii)} Polygon mode constrains the σ prior by truncation (D-28), which is an SC change that the frozen §7.3 had no row for.
\end{amendment}

\hypertarget{e-human-unit-interface-and-computational-cutoffs}{%
\subsubsection{10.e Human-unit interface and computational cutoffs}\label{e-human-unit-interface-and-computational-cutoffs}}

\textbf{Present state --- verified; do not reconvert what is already correct:}

\begin{longtable}[]{@{}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\columnwidth - 4\tabcolsep) * \real{0.3333}}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\columnwidth - 4\tabcolsep) * \real{0.3333}}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\columnwidth - 4\tabcolsep) * \real{0.3333}}@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
\begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Quantity
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Current state
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Status
\end{minipage} \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
\texttt{sigmax\_2} & squared \textbf{real} units of the input X/Y columns; user-supplied prior & \textbf{verified (tip)} --- \texttt{main.py:1582} \\
spatial cutoff \texttt{spatial\_window} & \textbf{real} coordinate length; real-space square & \textbf{verified (tip)} --- \texttt{utils.py:71–75} \\
\texttt{beta} & \textbf{internal} time units & \textbf{verified (tip)} --- \texttt{main.py:1582} \\
temporal cutoff \texttt{window} & \textbf{internal} time units & \textbf{verified (tip)} --- \texttt{main.py:1975} usage; docstring \\
\end{longtable}

3e's conversion layer therefore covers the \textbf{temporal} quantities (β, w) and reporting; the spatial quantities pass through unchanged.

\textbf{Exponential kernel semantics (D-16):} f(t; β) = β⁻¹ exp(−t/β); β is the temporal scale and \textbf{mean lag}, not a rate. Preferred public name: \textbf{\texttt{mean\_lag\_days}}; \texttt{temporal\_scale\_days} recorded as the alternative considered. Derived reporting: half-life (β ln 2), decay rate (1/β), time to 1\% (β ln 100), configured cutoff, omitted tail mass.

\textbf{Specification revision (C1/C2, D-13/D-14/D-21):} temporal and spatial windows are computational cutoffs for infinite-support kernels --- devices that remove time/area contributing negligible offspring mass, purely to reduce cost. Any finite cutoff applies consistently to (i) pair construction/event term, (ii) simulation/thinning, (iii) compensator. Omitted mass quantified: \textbf{temporal ε\_t = e\^{}\{−w/β\}; spatial (per-axis square, D-21) ε\_s = 1 − erf(w\_s/(√2 σ))²}. The disc formula exp(−w\_s²/(2σ²)) belongs to the retired disc equations and must never be mixed with the square geometry.

\textbf{Preferred interface (proposed, subject to final approval --- OP-6):} tolerance-based automatic cutoff selection (user supplies ε; code derives w, w\_s, e.g.~w = β ln(1/ε\_t), w\_s = √2 σ · erfinv(√(1−ε\_s))); direct physical cutoff override; explicit provenance for both the tolerance and the realized cutoff. The existing rule of thumb w\_s ≥ 4√σ\_x² (\texttt{main.py:1614}) is subsumed as a default tolerance.

\textbf{Guide revisions required:} specified in §15 (now definite on the square geometry); the baseline document specifies, a separate DOC task edits.

\textbf{Open configuration question (OP-7, non-blocking for 3a):} whether cutoff settings live in inference configuration or a separate computational/numerical configuration.

\begin{amendment}{A-13 --- 10.e as implemented}{2026-07-22 -- 2026-07-24}
\texttt{mean\_lag\_days} is accepted as a prior in days and converted onto the internal \texttt{beta} sample site; supplying both raises. Cutoff resolution follows D-24, and the tail formulas are the D-21 square ones. Two corrections arrived by audit after the feature: tolerances are validated \emph{before} precedence, so a malformed shared tolerance cannot hide behind two winning axis-specific overrides; and \texttt{set\_window} became transactional and provenance-atomic (A-14, D-33). \texttt{Temporal\_Power\_Law} is deliberately out of scope --- its \texttt{beta} is a shape parameter, not a mean lag, so no tolerance interface is defined for it and an explicit physical window remains the compatible path. OP-7 is untouched.
\end{amendment}

\hypertarget{f-complete-the-architecture-rewrite}{%
\subsubsection{10.f Complete the architecture rewrite}\label{f-complete-the-architecture-rewrite}}

Target responsibilities (BP overall, API where signatures change):

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  frozen typed model configuration; separate prior configuration; separate partition/decoder configuration; separate inference/run configuration;
\item
  prepared data and geometry \textbf{outside} frozen configuration;
\item
  a grid/partition module owning maps, intersections, areas, refinements, W;
\item
  likelihood atoms separated from NumPyro orchestration;
\item
  the simulator consuming the \textbf{same} mass/support atoms;
\item
  plotting separated, \texttt{model.plot\_*} preserved through delegation;
\item
  thin Hawkes/LGCP/Cox--Hawkes wrappers preserving the familiar fit interface;
\item
  RNG keys passed explicitly to fitting and simulation (the fit path already accepts an optional \texttt{rng\_key} since \texttt{0563510}; the default fixed-key path is byte-identical legacy behavior);
\item
  removal of \texttt{args} as canonical internal state (commit = OP-8).
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Decoder contract (load-bearing --- retraining is expected):} records and validates latent shape; output shape; compatible temporal/seasonal/spatial partition dimensions; decoder file/artifact references; gain/calibration conventions (\texttt{sp\_var\_mu}, incl.~the \texttt{UNIT\_GAIN\_SP\_VAR\_MU} identity used by the SBC program); training/provenance metadata; \textbf{explicit UNKNOWN where provenance cannot be recovered} (the seasonal substitute); compatibility between declared partition configuration and decoder output. Do not invent missing provenance. Partition sizes (50, 24, 25) become configuration but are constrained by pretrained decoder output shapes.

City-scale note (D-19): architecture must merely avoid precluding later city-scale work --- no hard-coded single-domain assumptions in the seam objects --- without building unused city-mode abstractions.

\hypertarget{g-verification-and-documentation}{%
\subsubsection{10.g Verification and documentation}\label{g-verification-and-documentation}}

Acceptance criteria are class-appropriate (detail in §12):

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{BP commits:} all existing tests; deterministic pin/trace comparison (four configs); rectangular pins unchanged where operation ordering permits, otherwise preregistered tolerance-based equality with a declared expression-change record (the \texttt{spatial\_\allowbreak{}refinement\_\allowbreak{}integral} docstring shows the required form); smoke fit + simulation for all families.
\item
  \textbf{MR/SC commits:} explicit rebaseline record; old and new behavior explained; focused semantic/property tests; behavior-preserving language forbidden.
\item
  \textbf{Polygon semantics tests:} rectangle; triangle; concave polygon; multipolygon; polygon with a hole; exact total intersection area; unique boundary/grid-line membership; refinement invariance (I9); simulator/compensator support consistency (D-18); conservation (I11) on every regime claimed exact or internally coherent; explicit nonrectangular rebaseline; cross-tile-pair preservation (10.b.7).
\item
  \textbf{Polygon numerical mass:} agreement with rectangle closed forms; comparison with a higher-accuracy polygon reference; convergence as resolution increases; gradient checks where gradients are used; NUTS runtime/memory benchmarks (baseline profile: per-fit cost \textasciitilde quadratic in n via pairs; pilots 21 s / 226 s / 967 s at n = 288 / 850 / 1642, machine-local --- recorded at \texttt{5da5784}); conservation on polygon domains.
\end{itemize}

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\hypertarget{cross-cutting-invariants}{%
\subsection{11. Cross-cutting invariants}\label{cross-cutting-invariants}}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  Event term, compensator, and simulator are three views of one intensity; any device (cutoff, discretization, windowing, support restriction) applies to all three (generalizes I4/I11; strengthened per-mode by D-18). The tip's single-sourced \texttt{within\_\allowbreak{}real\_\allowbreak{}box\_\allowbreak{}window} is the reference pattern.
\item
  Single-factor posterior (I5) survives every restructuring.
\item
  Special-case reduction (I8): LGCP and plain Hawkes remain exact restrictions; \textbf{revised target} per C1 --- the untruncated model is the scientific target, and finite cutoffs converge to it as ε → 0.
\item
  Refinement invariance (I9) holds on the new clipped/refined partitions.
\item
  Unit invariance (I10): internal rescaling changes \(\ell\) only by the deterministic Jacobian offset; unit-free functionals unchanged. Every real/internal interface is a declared, tested conversion --- the event-term unit boundary (\texttt{likelihood.py:100–118}) is the model.
\item
  Bounding-rectangle invariance (I12): real-unit trigger and window semantics are invariant to the choice of bounding rectangle; preserved through every 3b/3f restructuring.
\item
  Conservation (I11): E{[}n{]} = compensator, per model family and per support mode.
\item
  Excitation pairs are a function of the global event set and cutoffs only (10.b.7).
\item
  Fail loud at the boundary; never mask inside the likelihood (one NaN atom kills the scalar likelihood and all gradients).
\item
  Mode, cutoff, tolerance, decoder identity/gain, and standardization treatment are recorded in fit/simulation provenance.
\end{enumerate}

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\hypertarget{sbc-acceptance-policy-settled-verbatim}{%
\subsubsection{12.1 SBC acceptance policy (settled, verbatim)}\label{sbc-acceptance-policy-settled-verbatim}}

\begin{quote}
``Every behavior-preserving Phase 3 commit is gated by deterministic trace equivalence and rectangular pins for plain Hawkes, LGCP, and full Cox--Hawkes. At the Phase 3 tip, all three modes must complete end-to-end smoke or confirmation runs, and full Cox--Hawkes Stage 3 SBC at R=200 is the mandatory statistical exit criterion. A Stage 1- or Stage 2-specific SBC rerun is mandatory if deterministic equivalence fails, a model-specific path changes intentionally, or its confirmation run produces an anomaly. Polygon semantics have separate geometric and conservation acceptance tests; rectangular SBC is not evidence of polygon calibration.''
\end{quote}

The Stage 3 exit run is a \textbf{new} R = 200 run at the Phase 3 tip under the same pre-registered design (or an explicitly amended one), compared against the 2026-07-18 baseline record for diagnostic regressions.

\hypertarget{polygon-diagnostic-specification}{%
\subsection{13. Polygon diagnostic specification}\label{polygon-diagnostic-specification}}

\textbf{Purpose:} quantify the rectangle-vs-polygon excitation-mass discrepancy on the Philadelphia district geometries to set the polygon-mode accuracy target and advise when rectangle mode is adequate. It does not decide whether polygon mode exists (D-17).

\textbf{Inputs:} district polygons (external dependency, §6.18); event locations per district; σ grid spanning representative values ≈ 10--500 m (log-spaced, e.g., 10, 20, 50, 100, 200, 500 m).

\textbf{Computation, per district and per σ:} for each event location s\_j (or a boundary-stratified subsample), the rectangle spatial mass M\_rect(s\_j; σ) --- the per-axis erf product of §5.12, optionally window-clipped --- and a high-accuracy polygon reference M\_poly(s\_j; σ) (adaptive quadrature or dense-grid summation with convergence check). Report as a \textbf{function of σ}, not at a single posterior estimate --- α and most of the temporal factor scale out of the relative comparison.

\textbf{Per-district outputs:}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  median and maximum spatial-mass difference M\_rect − M\_poly;
\item
  relative excitation-compensator overcharge Σ(M\_rect − M\_poly)/ΣM\_poly;
\item
  fraction of events within 1σ, 2σ, 3σ of ∂A;
\item
  variation across districts (identifies districts that fill their bounding boxes poorly);
\item
  cost of the high-accuracy polygon reference (wall time, memory);
\item
  the accuracy required of a production polygon-mass method (tolerance such that the induced compensator error is dominated by the declared cutoff ε and float tolerance).\amdref{A-9}
\end{itemize}

\begin{amendment}{A-9 --- the accuracy target this bullet asks for}{2026-07-21}
Discharged \emph{analytically}, not from the diagnostic. Under D-21 the omitted spatial mass is \(\epsilon_s = 1 - \operatorname{erf}\!\left(w_s / (\sqrt{2}\sigma)\right)^{2}\); at the preregistered reference \(w_s = 3\sigma\), \(\epsilon_s = 5.3923032 \times 10^{-3}\). Reserving a tenth of that already-accepted error for the numerical mass evaluation gives \(\tau_{\mathrm{abs}} = 5.39 \times 10^{-4}\), which is exactly this bullet's criterion --- numerical error dominated by the declared cutoff --- made numerical. The gate was fixed \emph{before} the backends were compared. Full derivation, the finite-cutoff form \(\min(\tau_{\mathrm{abs}}, 0.1\,\epsilon_s(\sigma))\), and the separate provisional derivative gate are in A-9. What the \emph{diagnostic} decided is a different question: when rectangle mode is defensible at all (A-8).
\end{amendment}

\textbf{Consumers:} OP-9 (numerical method + accuracy target); NUTS runtime expectations (baseline cost profile in §10.g); user guidance for mode selection; 3d test tolerances.

\begin{amendment}{A-8 --- §13 executed; results}{2026-07-21, \texttt{906b30b}}
Run on 18 Philadelphia planning districts (127{,}590 events) and 6 park polygons. \(M_{\mathrm{rect}}\) is the per-axis erf product of §5.12; the reference \(M_{\mathrm{poly}}\) is a divergence-theorem line integral (Gauss--Legendre order 20, edge step ≤ 0.5σ, 8σ clip with neglected mass < 2.5×10⁻¹⁵), self-tested against the analytic rectangle case and convergence-checked to ≤ 10⁻¹¹ under step halving and order 32. Events were boundary-stratified with inclusion weights; events at least 8σ from ∂A were handled analytically.

\textbf{Headline result --- the answer is regime-dependent, and that is the finding.} Relative excitation-compensator overcharge on \emph{planning districts} is ≈ 1--7\% at σ ≤ 50 m, rising to 8--39\% at σ = 500 m (Lower South worst). On \emph{ribbon parks} it reaches 50--650\%: rectangle mode is not defensible there at any σ in the tested range. The pre-freeze expectation --- that districts are miles across while excitation is tens of metres, so the rectangle approximation is probably adequate --- is confirmed \emph{for districts at small σ} and refuted for elongated geometries, which fill their bounding boxes poorly. Mode selection is therefore a per-geometry judgement, which is the substantive argument for D-23 (no default).

\textbf{Data defect found upstream:} three of the six park geometries were invalid and required \texttt{make\_valid} to process; flagged upstream, not repaired in the package. This discharges the §6.18 external dependency.

\textbf{Artifact caveat.} Per-region/σ report tables are written under \texttt{output/}, which is \texttt{.gitignore}d; the numbers above are therefore recorded \emph{here} as the durable statement, with input hashes recorded in the OP-9 shootout \texttt{run\_config}. The diagnostic module and its import-time self-test are tracked and rerunnable.
\end{amendment}

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\hypertarget{covariate-contract-real-data-dry-run}{%
\subsection{14. Covariate-contract real-data dry run}\label{covariate-contract-real-data-dry-run}}

Before 3a/3c enforcement flips from report to reject, run the new validation layer in \textbf{report-only mode} against the actual project data:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  the Philadelphia event sets (per district and pooled): count of NaN rows \textbf{currently silently dropped} (§6.1 --- these will begin to fail loudly), out-of-polygon events, boundary/grid-line ties, CRS metadata state;
\item
  every covariate layer used in the pipeline: coverage of A, exported gap/overlap/sliver geometries, area statistics of defects;
\item
  the substitute seasonal decoder and all decoder artifacts against the 3f contract fields (shapes, gains incl.~\texttt{sp\_var\_mu}, provenance → UNKNOWN where applicable).
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Output:} a committed report \texttt{{[}{[}FILL:\ path{]}{]}} \amd{A-3} \texttt{refactor-patches/\allowbreak{}phase3a/\allowbreak{}dry\_run\_report.md} (+ \texttt{dry\_run\_findings.csv}, \texttt{dry\_run\_report.py}), commit \texttt{ff1aef5}. --- enumerating every datum that will begin to fail under the new contracts, so enforcement does not surprise the pipeline and genuine data defects are fixed upstream rather than accommodated in the package. Any contract adjustment motivated by this dry run is a recorded decision, not a silent relaxation.

\begin{amendment}{A-3 --- §14 executed; result and its consequence}{2026-07-20, \texttt{ff1aef5} / \texttt{f0b4375}}
Run in report semantics on the package-boundary inputs the production pipeline actually passes to BSTPP (per-park \texttt{locs\_s} frames for the four park boxes and the pooled in-park aggregate, prepared verbatim per the analysis notebooks; inputs identified by size, mtime, and sha256 in the report). No fits, no data modification, no enforcement.

\textbf{Finding.} Across 22{,}713 in-park events the \emph{only} violation class is \texttt{event\_time\_out\_of\_range}: five events on 2024-12-31 with T ∈ (1460.30, 1460.64{]}, because the pipeline computes \texttt{total\_days = 365*4} and 2024 is a leap year (true span 1461 days). Everything else is clean --- no nonfinite coordinates or covariates, no out-of-domain events, no grid-line or covariate-edge ties, no CRS mismatches, all geometries valid.

\textbf{Why this matters more than five rows suggest.} Under the frozen tip those events are accepted silently, and the excitation compensator's \(\min(T-t, w)\) goes \emph{negative} for them --- the same class of silent corruption the held-out path already rejected. The contract surfaced a live defect rather than a nuisance.

\textbf{Disposition.} Recorded as an \emph{upstream data/configuration defect} (fix: \texttt{total\_days = 1461}, or compute the span from the calendar), explicitly \textbf{not} a reason to loosen the contract. No contract was adjusted. On this basis the default was flipped to \texttt{reject}.

\textbf{Outstanding at the date of this entry (2026-07-27):} the upstream fix has \emph{not} landed in \texttt{yuxinlg/Illegal-Dumping}. Until it does, those park fits will raise under the default contract. A second upstream item, flagged during the OP-9 work and likewise outstanding: \texttt{batch\_park\_fits.py} passes \texttt{spatial\_window=0.1}, which under the real-unit contract (§5.9--5.10) is 0.1 \emph{metre} in EPSG:26918 --- a cutoff four orders of magnitude below any plausible excitation scale, i.e.\ an effectively empty pair set.

\textbf{Not covered.} The decoder-artifact leg of the §14 specification (shapes, gains, provenance → UNKNOWN) was not exercised; it belongs to the 3f decoder contract and remains outstanding.
\end{amendment}

\hypertarget{provenance-and-compatibility-requirements}{%
\subsection{16. Provenance and compatibility requirements}\label{provenance-and-compatibility-requirements}}

Every fit and simulation records, machine-readably:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  package version + git commit; model family; domain type (rectangle/polygon) and CRS;
\item
  excitation support mode (rectangle-approximation / polygon-mass) --- D-17/D-18;
\item
  cutoff provenance: tolerance requested, realized w and w\_s, omitted mass ε\_t, ε\_s, cutoff geometry (per-axis square, D-21);
\item
  unit contract: public real-unit parameter values and the internal conversion constants used;
\item
  decoder identity: artifact reference/hash, shapes, gain (\texttt{sp\_var\_mu}), provenance metadata or UNKNOWN;
\item
  standardization treatment: none / method string / upstream-declared;
\item
  RNG: explicit key/seed;
\item
  for simulations: mode-consistent support object identity.
\end{itemize}

The SBC harness's \texttt{implementation\_\allowbreak{}identity()} (commit, tracked-diff SHA-256, script SHA-256, library versions; refuses to pool across drift --- \texttt{0563510}) is the in-repo precedent and should be generalized rather than reinvented.

Compatibility: \texttt{model.plot\_*} and the familiar \texttt{fit} interfaces survive 3f through delegation/thin wrappers; \texttt{args} survives behind an adapter until OP-8; deprecations announced, not silent.

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\begin{itemize}

  PriorVAE variance-shrinkage remediation; \textbf{production-gain (\texttt{sp\_var\_mu\ =\ 2.0}) calibration} --- the prior-predictive diffuseness finding recorded at stage 2 stands as a modeling question outside Phase 3.\amdref{A-17} \amd{A-17} Confirmed unchanged by Phase 3, and now scoped: \texttt{sp\_var\_mu} becomes a \emph{quantity to estimate} after the refactor rather than a fixed multiplier to calibrate by hand.

  Seasonal decoder provenance recovery / clean retraining under the 3f contract.
\item
  Polygon-regime SBC (requires 3d completion; explicitly not claimed by rectangular SBC) and any SBC beyond the Stage 3 exit rerun.\amdref{A-7} \amd{A-7} Partially addressed: stage 2p validates the polygon LGCP \emph{background} path. Polygon \emph{excitation} SBC remains deferred and is not claimed by stage 2p.

  \amdnew{A-17} Per-kernel polygon mass for custom spatial triggers (OP-11).

  \amdnew{A-17} Reinterpreting covariate or reporting layers as the model-domain union.
\item
  \amdnew{A-17} Tolerance / \texttt{mean\_lag\_days} interface for \texttt{Temporal\_Power\_Law}, whose \texttt{beta} is a shape parameter rather than a mean lag.
\item
  Guide addendum execution (§15) --- a DOC task, no longer decision-gated.
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{reviewer-decisions-required-before-3a}{%
\subsection{Reviewer decisions required before 3a}\label{reviewer-decisions-required-before-3a}}

\textbf{Blockers (must resolve before the first 3a commit):}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Execute the freeze runs} --- fresh full suite + pin verification at \texttt{476c2a0} on the analysis machine, commit the environment export, and snapshot the pipeline repo (§1.2.1--4). Mechanical, but the freeze is not declared without them.
\item
  \textbf{Discharge or demote the remaining §5 \texttt{{[}{[}FILL{]}{]}} anchors} (§1.2.5) --- the ingestion/partition/background rows still resting on the 2c record. (The formerly blocking verifications --- cutoff geometry, pair-set property, nonrectangular asymmetry, Stage 3 record --- are all discharged in this revision.)
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{No open \emph{decisions} block 3a.} D-21 and D-22 resolved the two that did.

\begin{amendment}{A-1 --- disposition of the pre-3a blockers}{2026-07-19}
Both blockers were discharged before the first 3a commit: the freeze runs were executed at \texttt{476c2a0} (fresh suite 68/68; pins bit-identical, SHA-256 recorded) and the remaining §5 \texttt{{[}{[}FILL{]}{]}} anchors were discharged or demoted, in \texttt{5816b3d} / \texttt{68f2add}. The pipeline repository had no clone on the analysis machine; the non-git analysis trees were left untouched, and that gap is recorded rather than papered over.

This amendment also records the change of \emph{document form} answered here: from a pre-implementation specification to an append-only laboratory notebook (see ``How to read this document''). Part I is closed to revision as of this entry.
\end{amendment}

\textbf{Non-blocking (resolve at the indicated phase):}

\item
  \supsd{A-9}{OP-9 (polygon mass method) --- before 3d implementation; needs district geometry (§6.18) for the §13 diagnostic.}

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\hypertarget{part-ii}{%
\part*{Part II --- Amendment record (append-only)}\label{part-ii}}

\hypertarget{amendment-conventions}{%
\subsection{Conventions for this Part}\label{amendment-conventions}}

Entries are numbered \texttt{A-\emph{n}} in the order opened and are never edited or removed. Each entry states the date it was opened, the governing commits, the change class of §7.2, and which frozen sections it amends. Where an amendment changes the \emph{content} of a Part I section, the amending text is placed in that section as a marked block and the entry here records the reasoning, the evidence, and what the amendment leaves open; the two are not duplicates and both are part of the record.

Three disciplines carry over from Part I and are not restated in every entry. \textbf{Verification provenance:} a claim that something was verified names the artifact or the command that verified it, and a claim that something was \emph{not} verified is written as plainly as one that was. \textbf{Class honesty:} a behavior-changing correction is never described in behavior-preserving language, and an entry that reconstructs an acceptance trail after the fact says so rather than implying a contemporaneous run. \textbf{Machine locality:} all pin and rank comparisons are machine-local to the analysis machine of §4.2 and are meaningless across machines.
