\hypertarget{a-1}{%
\subsection{A-1 --- Freeze discharge and change of document form}\label{a-1}}

\textbf{Opened} 2026-07-19. \textbf{Commits} \texttt{5816b3d}, \texttt{68f2add}; form change 2026-07-27. \textbf{Class} process / DOC. \textbf{Amends} §1, §6.15--6.16, and the closing reviewer-decisions section.

The two pre-3a blockers were discharged. The freeze runs were executed in a detached worktree at \texttt{476c2a0} on the analysis machine: full suite 68/68, exit 0, and pin re-verification bit-identical against the committed \texttt{pins.json} with the output SHA-256 recorded. The environment export was committed, with \texttt{jax\_enable\_x64=False} noted explicitly because the polygon work later depends on that being true. The remaining §5 \texttt{[[FILL]]} anchors were discharged or demoted.

One item was not discharged and is recorded as a gap rather than closed: the pipeline repository \texttt{yuxinlg/Illegal-Dumping} had no clone on the analysis machine, so no pipeline snapshot exists for the freeze. The non-git analysis trees were deliberately left untouched.

\textbf{Form change.} Part I was written as a pre-implementation specification with ``Target'' columns and proposed defaults. Once implementation began, that form put the document under pressure to be revised into agreement with the code --- which would destroy the only property that makes it useful, namely that it is independent testimony about what was intended before the code existed. The document is therefore reconstituted as a laboratory notebook: Part I frozen and marked, Part II append-only. No statement of current status appears in Part I from this entry forward.

\hypertarget{a-2}{%
\subsection{A-2 --- Phase 3a: data contracts and unique membership}\label{a-2}}

\textbf{Opened} 2026-07-20; extended 2026-07-24 and 2026-07-27. \textbf{Commits} \texttt{3e69836}, \texttt{48f7d10}, \texttt{70c8677}, \texttt{f0b4375}, \texttt{f372e93}, \texttt{6e63efc}, \texttt{511ca5b}, \texttt{c22aa51}, \texttt{adea3d3}, \texttt{2cf326d}. \textbf{Class} IV + MR. \textbf{Amends} §5.1, §6.1--6.4, §10.a, §19.

\textbf{Conceptual basis.} A point-process likelihood has no defence against a malformed event: the event term credits it and the compensator debits a region, and if the two disagree about where the event is --- or whether it exists --- the disagreement is absorbed silently into the parameter estimates. The contract layer exists to make the boundary of the model explicit and to fail there, before any grid, membership join, or integral has been constructed. Placing it \emph{before} grid construction is the whole point: a violation named at the boundary is a data statement, whereas the same violation surfacing downstream is a symptom (\texttt{sjoin} produces the wrong number of rows) whose cause has to be reconstructed.

\textbf{Anchor correction to §5.1/§6.1.} The frozen text records ``NaN rows silently dropped at ingestion, \texttt{main.py:603}''. Reading the code establishes that \texttt{main.py:603} is the \emph{held-out scoring} path; the constructor never dropped NaN rows --- it crashed downstream in the membership \texttt{sjoin} with the misleading message about the computational grid. Both behaviors are closed, but the frozen characterization of which was which is corrected here rather than in place.

\textbf{Membership: why deduplication rather than arithmetic.} D-22 fixes the convention (left-closed, right-open per axis, outermost edges closed). The obvious implementation is to replace the spatial join with floor arithmetic on cell edges. That was rejected: floor arithmetic is a different computation, and near a cell edge it can differ from the join at ULP scale for events that are not on the edge at all. Instead the existing join is retained and its result deduplicated by maximum cell id. Because the field grid's ids are row-major from the bottom-left, the maximum id of an adjacent-cell tie \emph{is} the left-closed cell on an x-edge (+1), a y-edge (+25), or a corner (both), and events on the outermost right or top edge already join a single cell. Every strictly-interior event therefore passes through bit-identically, which is what the four golden pins confirm. On the covariate layer the same max-id rule is applied; on a row-major raster layer this coincides with left-closed, and on an arbitrary polygon layer it is recorded honestly as a deterministic convention rather than a geometric statement.

The temporal and seasonal axes were already D-22-compliant via \texttt{searchsorted(side='right')-1}; that is now pinned as contract rather than left as incidental behavior.

\textbf{Rebaseline scope.} No previously-working input changes result. The affected events --- on internal grid lines, on shared covariate edges --- could not be ingested at all before, or (on the held-out path) produced two \texttt{cov\_ind} rows with no length check, shifting every subsequent event's covariate assignment. The MR classification covers the transition from crash-or-corruption to deterministic membership, not a reassignment of valid events.

\textbf{Staged enforcement.} The layer landed report-only, preserving every legacy numerical path and legacy failure mode bit-for-bit, so that the §14 dry run (A-3) could inventory what would begin to fail. The default moved to \texttt{reject} only after that report was reviewed and signed off. \texttt{report} remains available as the dry-run instrument and is scheduled for removal (D-31).

\textbf{Later additions.} Finite positive horizon; polygonal, finite, positive-area domain geometry (previously a \texttt{LineString} domain produced empty domain checks and failed later as \texttt{event\_outside\_domain}, naming the wrong thing); and the CRS contract of A-16. A latent defect was found and fixed in the diagnostics themselves: the polygon-domain branch built its bounding rectangle without the transpose, so the internal-grid-line diagnostics were vacuous or wrong for every \texttt{GeoDataFrame} domain --- diagnostic-only, no rejection behavior changed.

\hypertarget{a-3}{%
\subsection{A-3 --- §14 dry run executed; leap-day finding}\label{a-3}}

\textbf{Opened} 2026-07-20. \textbf{Commits} \texttt{ff1aef5}, \texttt{f0b4375}. \textbf{Class} DOC + IV. \textbf{Amends} §14, §19. \textbf{Content amendment} in §14.

The finding, its disposition, and the two outstanding upstream items are recorded in the §14 block. What belongs here is the principle the exercise was designed to test and did: a contract that fires on real project data is either finding a defect or is wrong, and the decision of which must be made explicitly. It found a defect --- five events whose recorded time exceeds the declared horizon because the pipeline computes a four-year span as \(365 \times 4\) days --- and no contract was loosened to accommodate it. Under the frozen tip those events are accepted and the excitation compensator's \(\min(T-t,\,w)\) is negative for them.

\hypertarget{a-4}{%
\subsection{A-4 --- Phase 3b: the three-object seam}\label{a-4}}

\textbf{Opened} 2026-07-20. \textbf{Commits} \texttt{8084655}, \texttt{c6607ee}. \textbf{Class} BP. \textbf{Amends} §10.b. \textbf{Content amendment} in §10.b.

Every expression was moved verbatim and the operation order (grid → membership → covariates) preserved, so no numerical change is possible for any input; the pins confirm it. Two design points are worth recording because they constrain later phases.

First, the adapter is an \emph{identity} adapter: each \texttt{args} entry the seam owns is assigned the seam object's field itself, not a copy. A copying adapter would have permitted the two to drift while every test still passed. Second, the previously inline grid constants (internal horizon 50, seasonal length 24, seasonal period 365 days, and the 50/24/25 partition sizes) became named module constants documented as \emph{decoder-pinned} --- they are not free configuration, because the pretrained decoders emit fixed shapes. Making them named and annotated is the honest intermediate state; making them configurable is 3f work gated by the decoder contract.

Covariate event membership deliberately stayed event-side in the constructor rather than moving into the seam, purely to preserve the legacy operation order exactly. That is a temporary asymmetry, recorded so it is not mistaken for a design intention.

\hypertarget{a-5}{%
\subsection{A-5 --- Phase 3c: support on A, and the coverage contract}\label{a-5}}

\textbf{Opened} 2026-07-20. \textbf{Commits} \texttt{985c771}, \texttt{3120268}, \texttt{6d89b6e}, \texttt{1465e79}, \texttt{62ea1c1}, \texttt{e470cc0}. \textbf{Class} SC + IV + API. \textbf{Amends} §5.5--5.6, §6.5, §6.7--6.8, §10.c. \textbf{Content amendment} in §10.c.

\textbf{What was wrong and why it mattered.} On a polygon domain the frozen tip charged every in-domain field cell its full internal area \(1/625\) in the no-covariate background compensator. Cells were selected by intersection, so a cell that merely \emph{touched} \(A\) was charged a full cell, and boundary cells were charged their outside-\(A\) fraction. Separately, the background sampler drew locations over the full cell and relied on a downstream filter to discard points outside \(A\). The charged mass and the sampled support therefore described different regions, which is visible as overdispersion of the retained background count relative to its own compensator. The covariate leg had the same defect one level up: the refinement was built as \(C_c \cap A_m\) with no intersection against \(A\), and the covariate footprint --- not the declared domain --- decided the support.

\textbf{What replaced it.} One support object holds the clipped geometries \(C_c \cap A\) with their exact normalized intersection areas, and that same object feeds the likelihood integration arrays, the background location draw, and event-to-field-cell membership. For covariates the refinement is the exact triple intersection \(C_c \cap A_m \cap A\), verified against an independent shapely recomputation with total mass \(|A|\). Sub-tolerance slivers leave the support at the long-standing threshold, now named rather than inline.

\textbf{Numerical direction of the change.} On polygon domains \(I_{xy}\), \supsd{corrected 2026-07-28}{the log-likelihood,} and the background count rate \emph{decrease} by the previously overcharged outside-\(A\) mass, and simulated background counts now match the compensator instead of being filtered down after the draw.

\textbf{Correction appended 2026-07-28 (direction of the log-likelihood).} The clause struck above had the sign backwards, and it was inherited from the commit message of \texttt{985c771}, which states that ``\texttt{Itot\_xy} (and thus \texttt{loglik} and the background count rate) DECREASES''. The code composes \(\ell = \ell_1 - I_{\mathrm{tot},txy}\) (\texttt{bstpp/\allowbreak{}inference\_\allowbreak{}functions.py:202}, and the accumulating form at \texttt{:282}), so \emph{removing} compensator mass \textbf{raises} the log-likelihood. The correct statement: at fixed parameters the background compensator, \(I_{xy}\), and the simulated background count rate all decrease by the previously overcharged outside-\(A\) mass; since \(\ell = \sum_i \log \lambda_i - \Lambda\), the log-likelihood \emph{increases} by the removed overcharge wherever the event term is unchanged. The qualification matters here rather than being pedantic: the event term is \emph{not} unchanged for the measure-zero set of boundary events that 3c-2 remapped to a supported cell, so the two effects are separable only away from those events. \textbf{Scope of the error (checked 2026-07-28, not assumed).} The defect is confined to \emph{prose}. The code is correct and was verified on four points. \textbf{(i) Composition and sign:} \(\ell = \ell_1 - I_{\mathrm{tot},txy}\) with \(\ell_1 = \sum_i \log \lambda_i\) (\texttt{inference\_\allowbreak{}functions.py:175--177, 202}; LGCP accumulating form at \texttt{:271--282}), and \texttt{numpyro.factor} receives \(+\ell\) --- higher log-likelihood raises the joint density, as it must. \textbf{(ii) Monotonicity of the 3c change:} areas enter the background compensator only through \(\sum_k \exp(\text{log\_rate}_k)\,\mathrm{areas}_k\) (\texttt{likelihood.py:238--250}), a sum of nonnegative terms, so clipping a cell can only reduce \(I_{xy}\) --- the code moves the quantity in the direction the commit message's \emph{first} clause claims. Of the message's three assertions, ``\(I_{xy}\) decreases'' and ``the background count rate decreases'' are true; only ``and thus \texttt{loglik}'' is false. \textbf{(iii) No leakage into machine-checked artifacts:} no test, docstring, or in-code comment asserts a direction for \texttt{loglik}, and both consumers treat it as higher-is-better --- \texttt{expected\_AIC} computes \(-2\ell + 2k\) (\texttt{main.py:738}) and \texttt{log\_expected\_likelihood} takes a \texttt{logsumexp} of \texttt{loglik\_factor} (\texttt{main.py:727}). A wrong belief written into an assertion would have been the serious case; it was not.

\textbf{(iv) What would and would not have caught it.} The four golden pins are structurally blind here: they cover rectangle configurations, which 3c leaves bit-identical, so no pin could ever have tested the polygon-side direction. The evidence that does bear on it is the seeded Monte Carlo conservation test \texttt{tests/\allowbreak{}test\_clipped\_\allowbreak{}support.py::\allowbreak{}test\_sim\_cox\_\allowbreak{}count\_rate\_\allowbreak{}matches\_clipped\_\allowbreak{}compensator}, which compares the simulated background count mean against the clipped compensator and moved from \(z = 16.7\) to \(|z| < 5\) across the fix. That test is direction-sensitive and it passes. The lesson to carry into 3g: a prose claim about the \emph{direction} of a derived quantity is not covered by any gate in §12, and this one survived a rebaseline record, an acceptance record, and a document revision before being caught by review.

\textbf{Conflict recorded, not silently resolved:} the \texttt{985c771} commit message remains wrong in the repository history; this entry, not that message, is the operative statement. Correcting the message would require a history rewrite and is not proposed; \texttt{refactor-patches/\allowbreak{}phase3c/\allowbreak{}rebaseline\_\allowbreak{}record.md} should carry a pointer to this entry as a 3g documentation task. Rectangle domains are unchanged by construction: array domains keep the full grid with the exact uniform double \(1/n_{xy}^2\) stored on the support object rather than computed geometrically, so the float32 cast reproduces the legacy array bit-identically.

\textbf{One refinement of D-22, declared.} An event lying on a stretch of \(\partial A\) that runs along an internal grid line --- valid, since the boundary is inside --- whose left-closed cell has zero support now maps to the adjacent cell that actually carries its domain mass. This is a measure-zero set and no integral changes, but previously the event term and the compensator disagreed about which cell the event belonged to.

\textbf{A transitional window, accepted and closed.} For exactly one commit, a covariate layer with a gap inside \(A\) silently lost that region from the refinement. The coverage contract in the following commit made gaps and positive-area overlaps loud, with the offending geometry exported rather than only the failing index. Splitting the SC and IV halves across two commits was deliberate under §7.2; the cost was this one-commit window, and it is recorded rather than glossed.

\textbf{Standardization.} Only the reporting requirement and the explicit area-weighted convenience landed, together with rejection of unknown method strings --- the legacy boolean coercion had silently treated any non-empty string as \texttt{True}, so \texttt{standardize\_cov='area'} quietly produced count standardization. OP-3 and OP-4 remain open; the default is still the legacy count-weighted z-score.

\hypertarget{a-6}{%
\subsection{A-6 --- Canonical domain union; area from set-union}\label{a-6}}

\textbf{Opened} 2026-07-24. \textbf{Commits} \texttt{26a4c3c}, \texttt{05da465}, \texttt{2c7264a}, \texttt{2a67962}. \textbf{Class} BP for disjoint and single-row domains; \textbf{SC} for positively overlapping rows. \textbf{Amends} §7.3; establishes D-30.

An audit found the domain area computed as \(\sum_i \mathrm{area}(row_i)\) while support clipping, parenting, and polygon mass had already been intersecting against the geometric union. For a domain supplied as overlapping rows the two disagree, and the disagreement propagates into \texttt{area\_ratio} and hence into the normalization of every internal area. Disjoint and single-row domains --- every domain used to date --- are unaffected, which is why the defect survived: it is latent in the regime actually exercised.

The fix computes \texttt{union\_geometry} once on \texttt{PreparedDomain} and derives area from it. A separate characterization commit first \emph{pinned} that independent downstream recomputations of the union agreed with the prepared one (geometric equality, WKB, and geometry hash) before the plumbing commit made them consume the prepared object; that ordering is what allows the plumbing to be classified BP. The canonical object subsequently becomes load-bearing for mass-table identity (D-27), where a recomputed-but-equal geometry would still hash differently.

\hypertarget{a-7}{%
\subsection{A-7 --- SBC stage 2p: polygon-domain LGCP background}\label{a-7}}

\textbf{Opened} 2026-07-20. \textbf{Commits} \texttt{3bf6fb4} (preregistration), \texttt{677c9b7} (harness), \texttt{1a9dd22} (archive). \textbf{Class} validation. \textbf{Amends} §4.5, §12, §18, §19.

Phase 3c changed the LGCP background path, which fires the conditional escalation of §12.1. The covering archived stage is stage 2, whose configuration is rectangular --- and on rectangles the 3c integrands are bit-identical, so a rerun would re-measure an unchanged computation. Reruning it anyway would have produced evidence of the wrong thing; discharging the trigger on bit-identity alone would have left the changed regime unvalidated.

The decision, registered before any implementation, was to retain the archived rectangular stage 2 on bit-identity evidence and add \textbf{stage 2p}: stage 2's design verbatim --- priors, unit gain, \(R = 600\), adaptive ESS ladder, nine primary functionals, the \(p \geq 0.005\) rule, budget band --- on a pre-registered generic convex octagon (unit square with four asymmetric corner cuts, exact \(|A| = 0.928950\)) chosen so that the 3c clipped support, clipped sampling, and support membership are all live while the five archived pointwise probe cells stay strictly interior. Statistical components are reused \emph{by reference}, not copied, so the two stages cannot drift.

The pre-registered prior-predictive check at the distinct check seed reproduced the stage-2 count distribution scaled by \(|A|\) (median 122 against 124), inside the band. The run passed: every primary Monte Carlo ECDF \(p \geq 0.005\), minimum 0.134, supplementary \(a_0\) at 0.913, zero divergences and zero ESS retries across all 600 replicates.

\textbf{Scope, stated narrowly.} Stage 2p validates the polygon \emph{background} path at unit gain. It is not evidence about polygon excitation, about production gain, or about covariates. The standing rule that rectangular SBC is not evidence of polygon calibration now has a companion: background-path polygon SBC is not evidence of excitation-path polygon calibration.

\hypertarget{a-8}{%
\subsection{A-8 --- §13 diagnostic executed}\label{a-8}}

\textbf{Opened} 2026-07-21. \textbf{Commit} \texttt{906b30b}. \textbf{Class} DOC. \textbf{Amends} §6.18, §13. \textbf{Content amendment} in §13.

Results, the reference method, and the upstream geometry defect are recorded in the §13 block. The point to carry forward is that the diagnostic answered a question the frozen document had assumed away. The pre-freeze reasoning --- districts are miles across, excitation is tens of metres, so the bounding-rectangle approximation is presumably adequate --- holds for planning districts at small \(\sigma\) and fails badly for elongated geometries: ribbon parks show 50--650\% relative compensator overcharge, because such a region occupies a small fraction of its bounding box and the rectangle mass integrates mostly outside \(A\). Since the package is intended to serve both district-level and park-level analysis, no single default mode is defensible, and D-23 follows from measurement rather than from taste.

\hypertarget{a-9}{%
\subsection{A-9 --- OP-9 resolved: backend and accuracy budget}\label{a-9}}

\textbf{Opened} 2026-07-21. \textbf{Commit} \texttt{62bde1f}. \textbf{Class} numerical method. \textbf{Amends} §13, §17; establishes D-23 and D-25.

\textbf{The quantity.} Polygon mode requires, per event \(j\) and per \(\sigma\),
\[
  M_j(\sigma) \;=\; \int_{A \,\cap\, C_j} \mathcal{N}\!\left(s - s_j;\, \sigma^2 I\right)\, ds ,
\]
evaluated inside NUTS, differentiably in \(\sigma\), for every event at every leapfrog step.

\textbf{Accuracy-budget derivation (settled).} Under the D-21 per-axis square cutoff the omitted spatial mass is
\[
  \epsilon_s \;=\; 1 - \operatorname{erf}\!\left(\frac{w_s}{\sqrt{2}\,\sigma}\right)^{\!2}.
\]
At the preregistered reference \(w_s = 3\sigma\) this gives \(\epsilon_s = 5.3923032 \times 10^{-3}\). Reserving 10\% of that cutoff-error budget for the numerical polygon-mass approximation gives
\[
  \tau_{\mathrm{abs}} \;=\; 5.3923032 \times 10^{-4} \;\approx\; 5.39 \times 10^{-4},
\]
which is the gate carried in the code. The logic is that the numerical error of the mass evaluation should be an order of magnitude below the error already knowingly accepted by truncating the kernel; a numerical method more accurate than that buys nothing, and one less accurate than that silently dominates a budget the user was told was the cutoff's. \textbf{This gate was fixed before the backends were compared}, and the comparison then selected a backend satisfying it --- not the reverse. For an actual finite cutoff the shootout used \(\min\!\left(\tau_{\mathrm{abs}},\, 0.1\,\epsilon_s(\sigma)\right)\), subject to the explicitly recorded numerical floor.

\textbf{The derivative gate is provisional and differently motivated.} \(\tau_{\mathrm{deriv}}\) was set equal to \(\tau_{\mathrm{abs}}\) as a separate numerical-stability criterion. It is \emph{not} derived from compensator truncation error, and it should not be read as if it were. Registered as OP-12.

\textbf{The comparison.} Three candidates were measured against the §13 oracle on five real geometries (up to 128 events each, deterministic boundary and interior selection, 49 \(\sigma\) values including off-grid and knot-adjacent, with a finite-difference derivative oracle whose own uncertainty is \(1.3 \times 10^{-7}\)).

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Fixed-node JAX boundary quadrature} is oracle-exact (\(\leq 2.1 \times 10^{-11}\); derivatives at the finite-difference floor) at every panel configuration --- and costs 28.5\,s per warm gradient on the largest polygon fit (\(n = 16{,}479\)), roughly \(4000\times\) the rectangle baseline, and fails outright in float32 through cancellation. Not viable as an in-sampler evaluator, but it is the right \emph{builder}.
\item
  \textbf{Oracle-built PCHIP tables} pass on values for \(K \geq 40\) and \textbf{fail the derivative gate at every \(K\)} (\(1.8 \times 10^{-2}\) at \(K = 64\)). This is the informative negative result: value accuracy and slope accuracy are separate properties of an interpolant, and a table that looks excellent by values alone would have fed corrupted gradients to NUTS.
\item
  \textbf{Quad-built tables} --- values and slopes taken from the independently validated quadrature backend, gated against the shapely oracle --- pass both gates on every geometry (at \(K = 64\): \(\leq 5.7 \times 10^{-7}\) on values, \(1.6 \times 10^{-5}\) on derivatives, zero bound violations), evaluate in 1.3\,ms at \(n = 16{,}479\) (about \(5\times\) \emph{faster} than the incumbent rectangle erf product), and cost roughly 23 minutes to build once per fit.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Decision.} One production path: C\textsuperscript{1} cubic Hermite lookup in \(\log \sigma\), \(K = 64\) log-uniform knots over the declared \(\sigma\) range, built offline by the fixed-node quadrature kernel in float64, with a loud no-extrapolation contract. The no-extrapolation contract is what makes the \(\sigma\) range a modeling commitment rather than an implementation detail, and hence what forces D-28 and D-29.

\hypertarget{a-10}{%
\subsection{A-10 --- Phase 3d: polygon excitation support modes}\label{a-10}}

\textbf{Opened} 2026-07-22; extended through 2026-07-25. \textbf{Commits} \texttt{edfce53}, \texttt{8c4a702}, \texttt{850a4b9}, \texttt{ae27947}, \texttt{8580364}, \texttt{238fbd3}, \texttt{0eeaebd}. \textbf{Class} CA + IV + \textbf{API}. \textbf{Amends} §10.d, §7.3; establishes D-26. \textbf{Content amendment} in §10.d.

\textbf{D-18 enforced structurally.} One \texttt{ExcitationSupport} object feeds offspring parenting eligibility and the compensator charge region, following the tip's single-sourced window predicate as the template. Mixing a rectangle simulator leg with a polygon compensator leg is not a configuration that can be expressed.

\textbf{The exact-type restriction.} Polygon mass integrates the built-in symmetric Gaussian spatial kernel and nothing else. A subclass overriding the event kernel would therefore produce a likelihood whose event term describes one process and whose compensator describes another --- not an approximation but an inconsistency. The gate is exact type identity rather than \texttt{isinstance} precisely because a subclass is the dangerous case. Rectangle mode keeps the custom-trigger interface unchanged. \textbf{This is a placeholder} pending a per-kernel polygon-mass backend (OP-11), not a decision that custom spatial kernels are unsupported in principle.

\textbf{Hard-require, and why it is an API change.} Polygon construction previously could build a table synchronously, and an older path mutated process-global JAX precision to do it. Both are now forbidden: the table is prepared by an explicit public call in host NumPy/SciPy float64, and a polygon constructor without a compatible table raises, naming the preparation function. The constructor validates and installs a supplied table; it never rebuilds on incompatibility. This is not behavior-preserving and is not described as such. The reasoning is that a silent rebuild hides two things at once --- a build cost of minutes, and a substitution of the numerical object the likelihood is evaluated against --- and that a global precision toggle is an action at a distance on every other computation in the process.

\textbf{Held-out.} See A-15.

\textbf{Preflight ordering.} An explicit polygon request with no table now fails before the base constructor performs data and decoder preparation, so the error arrives in milliseconds rather than after the expensive path.

\textbf{Honesty note on the acceptance trail.} No contemporaneous acceptance record was written when the original 3d feature commit landed; the per-phase record is a post-hoc reconstruction from commit messages and subsequent RED→GREEN repairs, and it does not claim historical RED runs that were not observed. The repairs themselves --- exact-type gate, table validation, preparation API, transactional replacement --- were each written test-first in the ordinary way.

\hypertarget{a-11}{%
\subsection{A-11 --- Mass-table compatibility identity}\label{a-11}}

\textbf{Opened} 2026-07-24; extended 2026-07-25 and 2026-07-27. \textbf{Commits} \texttt{1adeb00}, \texttt{ce5508f}, \texttt{90d45bb}, \texttt{513cb1a}. \textbf{Class} IV. \textbf{Establishes} D-27.

A mass table is an event-indexed numerical artifact: row \(j\) is \(M_A(s_j, \cdot)\) for one specific event at one specific location under one specific domain and window. Reusing it against a different realization produces a likelihood that is silently wrong rather than loudly broken --- and if the event counts happen to match, nothing fails at all. Equal counts are therefore explicitly not treated as compatibility.

Validation covers the canonical domain-union geometry hash, event coordinates and row order, event count, realized spatial window, \(\sigma\) range and knot grid, interpolation convention, panel height after unit conversion, quadrature order, and backend schema version, plus array shapes and finiteness. Three strengthenings followed from reaudit:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Event identity is binary.} The original identity formatted coordinates with \texttt{.9g}, under which distinct float64 events collide. Identity is now a hash of little-endian float64 coordinates. Legacy artifacts and decimal-formatted identities are \textbf{intentionally rejected}; that incompatibility is deliberate and recorded, not a migration oversight.
\item
  \textbf{Provenance cannot be falsified.} Descriptive user metadata is nested under its own key and cannot overwrite reserved compatibility fields.
\item
  \textbf{Sidecar and artifact must agree.} Duplicated numerical and identity metadata are compared field by field against the archive payload before the table object is constructed, so a tampered or stale sidecar cannot disagree with the artifact and still load. \texttt{table\_id} remains descriptive and is explicitly \emph{not} treated as artifact-integrity verification --- stated so that nobody later mistakes it for one.
\end{itemize}

A related fix keeps ingestion honest about coordinates: real event coordinates are retained in float64 at ingestion, because the float32 affine round-trip through the internal axis scales can perturb a coordinate enough to change its hash, which would make a correctly prepared table appear incompatible with the model that prepared it.

\hypertarget{a-12}{%
\subsection{A-12 --- σ bounds and prior truncation}\label{a-12}}

\textbf{Opened} 2026-07-22; scope settled 2026-07-27. \textbf{Commit} \texttt{edfce53}. \textbf{Class} \textbf{SC}. \textbf{Amends} §7.3, §10.d; establishes D-28 and D-29; opens OP-10.

\textbf{What happens.} In polygon mode the user's \(\sigma^2\) prior is replaced by its exact truncation onto \(\left[\texttt{min\_sigma}^2,\, \texttt{max\_sigma}^2\right]\). Truncation is exact --- correctly normalized, samplable, batched, JIT-safe --- for the named families (\texttt{HalfNormal}, \texttt{LogNormal}, and their truncated forms); any other family raises rather than being silently approximated.

\textbf{Why it is SC and not a numerical device.} The Hermite table exists only over the declared \(\sigma\) range and refuses to extrapolate (D-25). Something must guarantee the sampler stays inside that range, and the only honest mechanism is to state that the prior has no support outside it. That changes prior support and therefore posterior support: it is a declared change to the model, required by the numerical method but not \emph{reducible} to it. Consequently the bounds must appear in model provenance, and the change carries semantic tests and a rebaseline record rather than a tolerance argument. \supsd{corrected 2026-07-28}{Where the supplied prior already has negligible or no mass outside the interval, truncation is behaviorally inert} --- but ``inert in this case'' is a property of the supplied prior, not of the mechanism.

\textbf{Correction appended 2026-07-28 (inertness requires containment, not smallness).} Truncation renormalizes: if the supplied prior places mass \(\delta > 0\) outside the interval, every retained density value is multiplied by \(1/(1-\delta)\) and the tails are cut. Exact behavioral inertness therefore requires that the supplied prior have \textbf{no support} outside \(\left[\texttt{min\_sigma}^2, \texttt{max\_sigma}^2\right]\), not merely little mass there. A prior with negligible outside mass makes truncation \emph{approximately} nonbinding, which is a claim about a particular prior and a particular posterior and is owed a sensitivity check --- not a general property that can be asserted at the interface. The distinction is exactly the one D-28 exists to protect: an SC change that looks inert is still an SC change.

\textbf{Bounds are use-case quantities (D-29).} A plausible range for an excitation length scale is a property of the phenomenon and the domain, not of the software. \texttt{min\_sigma} is accordingly required in polygon mode with no default. \texttt{max\_sigma} currently falls back to 5\,km converted through the projected CRS when omitted --- a convenience chosen for the expected Philadelphia district and park use cases, recorded in provenance, and \textbf{not} a scientific statement. Since that default silently sets the truncation interval of a prior, whether it should exist at all is registered as OP-10, and the present position is that both bounds should be required to be stated.

\hypertarget{a-13}{%
\subsection{A-13 --- Phase 3e: human units and computational cutoffs}\label{a-13}}

\textbf{Opened} 2026-07-22; extended 2026-07-24. \textbf{Commits} \texttt{2ce665c}, \texttt{5f5bb8d}, \texttt{a46c960}, \texttt{2a7c558}, \texttt{798f551}. \textbf{Class} API + CA + IV. \textbf{Amends} §10.e, §17; establishes D-24. \textbf{Content amendment} in §10.e.

\textbf{Interface.} The public temporal scale is \texttt{mean\_lag\_days}, supplied as a distribution in days and converted onto the internal \texttt{beta} sample site by \(\beta_{\text{internal}} = \texttt{mean\_lag\_days} \times (T_{\text{internal}} / T_{\text{days}})\); supplying both raises rather than resolving a precedence. Spatial quantities were already real-unit and were not reconverted, per the frozen §10.e --- with the qualification of A-12 that polygon mode nonetheless constrains the \(\sigma\) prior.

\textbf{Cutoffs as declared approximations.} Finite windows are computational cutoffs of infinite-support kernels, with omitted mass
\[
  \epsilon_t = e^{-w/\beta},
  \qquad
  \epsilon_s = 1 - \operatorname{erf}\!\left(\frac{w_s}{\sqrt{2}\,\sigma}\right)^{\!2}
  \quad \text{(per-axis square, D-21),}
\]
inverted for tolerance-based selection as \(w = \beta \ln(1/\epsilon_t)\) and \(w_s = \sqrt{2}\,\sigma \operatorname{erf}^{-1}\!\big(\sqrt{1-\epsilon_s}\big)\). Because \(\beta\) and \(\sigma\) are latent, selection uses declared \emph{design} values supplied by the user, and both the tolerance and the realized cutoff are recorded in \texttt{cutoff\_provenance}. The package defaults reproduce the historical rules of thumb exactly --- \(\epsilon_t = e^{-5}\) is \(w \geq 5\beta\), and \(\epsilon_s = 1 - \operatorname{erf}(4/\sqrt{2})^2\) is \(w_s \geq 4\sigma\) --- so the change is one of accounting, not of defaults: what was a rule of thumb is now a quantified omitted mass.

\textbf{Two audit corrections.} Tolerances are validated \emph{before} precedence is applied. Previously only the effective per-axis tolerances were checked, so a malformed shared tolerance was accepted whenever both axis-specific values overrode it, and an invalid tolerance behind a winning physical cutoff was retained in provenance instead of being rejected --- provenance recording a value the code never honored is worse than no provenance. And \texttt{set\_window} was made provenance-atomic and then fully transactional (A-14).

\textbf{Scope limit, deliberate.} \texttt{Temporal\_Power\_Law} has no tolerance or \texttt{mean\_lag\_days} interface, because its \texttt{beta} is a shape parameter and not a mean lag; inventing a conversion for it would be a modeling claim. An explicit physical window remains the compatible path for unsupported triggers.

\hypertarget{a-14}{%
\subsection{A-14 --- Transactional public state mutation}\label{a-14}}

\textbf{Opened} 2026-07-24. \textbf{Commits} \texttt{a46c960}, \texttt{798f551}, \texttt{8580364}. \textbf{Class} \supsd{A-19}{SC on the public API surface} → \textbf{CF + API} (reclassified 2026-07-28; see A-19). \textbf{Establishes} D-33.

\texttt{set\_window} mutated the window arguments and rebuilt the excitation pairs \emph{before} cutoff validation and support rebuilding completed. A rejected input or a failed rebuild therefore left an object whose windows and pairs had changed while its provenance and support had not --- an object that reports one cutoff and integrates another. That is precisely the silent-corruption mode Phase 3 exists to eliminate, and it lived in the public mutator.

The method now validates inputs, prepares pairs, cutoff provenance, excitation support, and (in polygon mode) the replacement mass table entirely in local state, and assigns only after the whole replacement succeeds. Provenance is re-resolved as a physical override of the realized windows, with the constructor restoring the original tolerance-selection record after initial installation so that how the first cutoff was chosen is not lost the first time a window is changed.

In polygon mode a temporal-only change reuses the installed table --- the table is indexed by events and spatial window, not by the temporal one --- while a spatial-window change requires a compatible replacement supplied by the caller, validated against the \emph{prospective} window before any mutation. Silent rebuild is forbidden here for the same reason as at construction (D-26).

\hypertarget{a-15}{%
\subsection{A-15 --- Held-out data as a standalone realization}\label{a-15}}

\textbf{Opened} 2026-07-23. \textbf{Commits} \texttt{8c4a702}, \texttt{8580364}. \textbf{Class} \textbf{CF} (see A-19). \textbf{Establishes} D-32.

\textbf{The scientific reading, stated so it is not misread as a train/test split.} Held-out data are evaluated as a \emph{complete standalone realization} over the model domain and horizon --- for example a different year at the same location. They are not a random subset of the fitted events, because removing a subset severs the parent structure the Hawkes term is defined by: an offspring whose parent was withheld is not a held-out observation of the same process, it is an observation of a different one.

\textbf{The reset qualification (appended 2026-07-28).} ``A different year at the same location'' qualifies as a standalone realization \textbf{only if the process is taken to reset at the year boundary.} In a continuing Hawkes process it does not: events late in the training year would excite events early in the held-out year, and that excitation is precisely what this score omits. State plainly what the implemented quantity is and is not. It \emph{is} the likelihood of the held-out realization treated as a fresh realization of the same process with the same parameters, self-exciting only within itself. It \emph{is not} the future-conditional likelihood \(p(Y_{\text{future}} \mid Y_{\text{train}}, \theta)\): it carries no training-to-test excitation and does not condition on the training event history. Where the held-out window genuinely abuts the training window, the reset is an assumption the analyst is making, and it should be stated with the score rather than inferred from it.

\textbf{The consequence.} All state derived from parent events must be rebuilt from the held-out realization: the excitation pairs, and in polygon mode the rows \(M_A(s_j, \sigma)\) of the excitation-mass table. Both the excitation event term and the compensator then use the held-out realization's own times and locations, and no training-event-indexed row is reused.

\textbf{What was wrong.} The held-out path rebuilt the pairs but reused the training-event mass table. With unequal train and test counts this crashed on shape; with equal counts it silently scored the held-out events against another realization's geometry-indexed masses. The fix builds a test-specific excitation support without mutating the training support or arguments. Rectangle mode is unaffected, since its mass is a closed-form function of the event coordinates rather than a stored per-event table.

This is a correctness requirement of the existing held-out likelihood. It does not change what that likelihood is intended to compute.

\hypertarget{a-16}{%
\subsection{A-16 --- CRS contract completed}\label{a-16}}

\textbf{Opened} 2026-07-24; completed 2026-07-27. \textbf{Commits} \texttt{c22aa51}, \texttt{adea3d3}, \texttt{e706107}, \texttt{2cf326d}. \textbf{Class} IV. \textbf{Amends} §5.2, §6.4; establishes D-34.

The real-unit trigger contract (D-15, §5.9--5.10) means that \(\sigma\), the spatial cutoff, and every distance in the excitation term are lengths in the coordinate system of the supplied data. A coordinate reference system that can be adopted, inferred, or overwritten without the user's knowledge therefore silently changes what those lengths mean. Four gaps were closed:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  a CRS-bearing domain with CRS-less covariates previously constructed, potentially adopting the domain CRS by assumption; it is now a named violation distinct from a mismatch;
\item
  tabular (CSV or plain \texttt{DataFrame}) covariates inherited the domain CRS \emph{before} validation, which made a missing source CRS undetectable in principle; an explicit \texttt{spatial\_cov\_crs} is now required when the domain declares a CRS, parsed through the standard user-input parser and assigned before validation;
\item
  deprecated attribute-level CRS writes were replaced with the supported setter (or skipped where already matching);
\item
  that replacement initially retained permissive override on mismatch at internal attachment, which could rewrite a CRS after validation should have rejected the pair; mismatch there now raises. The permissive setter is retained only for the CRS-less case adopting a declared CRS.
\end{itemize}

Array domains carry no CRS and are unaffected throughout.

\hypertarget{a-17}{%
\subsection{A-17 --- Ancillary fixes and deferred-work rescoping}\label{a-17}}

\textbf{Opened} 2026-07-20; extended 2026-07-27. \textbf{Commits} \texttt{65b586e}, \texttt{f372e93}. \textbf{Class} fix / DEF. \textbf{Amends} §18.

\textbf{Unconditional plotting in the SVI path.} \texttt{run\_svi} called pyplot unconditionally after every fit; the \texttt{plot\_loss} argument gated nothing. Any non-interactive caller passing \texttt{plot\_loss=False} still created a figure and called \texttt{show}, which on a machine with a broken default backend failed intermittently depending on which backend earlier imports had selected. It surfaced as an unrelated-looking flaky test during Phase 3c. Recorded here because the diagnosis matters more than the fix: an intermittent failure in a numerical suite is worth chasing to its cause rather than quarantining, and in this case the cause was a dead argument rather than anything numerical. The MCMC path was checked and does no plotting.

\textbf{Deferred work rescoped.} Three items are added to §18 as newly identified rather than newly deferred: per-kernel polygon mass for custom spatial triggers (OP-11); reinterpretation of covariate or reporting layers as the model-domain union; and a tolerance interface for the power-law temporal trigger. One item is rescoped: \texttt{sp\_var\_mu} remains at its production value and untouched by Phase 3, and becomes a \emph{quantity to estimate} after the refactor rather than a fixed multiplier to be calibrated by hand.

\textbf{Correction appended 2026-07-28 (do not overstate what estimation buys).} An earlier draft of this entry said that estimating the amplitude ``removes the reason the fixed value had to be chosen''. It does not. Estimation replaces a fixed gain with a \emph{prior on} the gain, and thereby converts a calibration problem into an inference problem carrying its own obligations. Three of them are already visible. \textbf{(i) Identifiability:} the field amplitude trades off against the intercept \(a_0\) and against the latent field's own scale --- \(\exp(a_0 + \texttt{sp\_var\_mu}\cdot f_s)\) is invariant to compensating shifts --- and, through the excitation share, against \(\alpha\); a prior that does not break these ties will produce a ridge rather than an estimate. \textbf{(ii) The budget problem persists:} the unit-gain SBC scope was adopted because prior-predictive counts at production gain span roughly seven orders of magnitude and cannot be budget-bounded, and a \emph{prior} spanning that range has the same property. Estimation relocates the problem into the prior specification; it does not dissolve it. \textbf{(iii) Interaction with the known PriorVAE variance shrinkage} (§6.10) --- an estimated amplitude will partly absorb the decoder's understated second moment, which makes the estimate hard to interpret as a property of the field rather than of the decoder. This is therefore a \textbf{post-Phase-3 modeling decision requiring its own SBC stage}, not a settled item of deferred work, and it is recorded here as such rather than as a solved question.

\hypertarget{a-19}{%
\subsection{A-19 --- Change class CF added; A-14 and A-15 reclassified}\label{a-19}}

\textbf{Opened} 2026-07-28. \textbf{Class} DOC (taxonomy). \textbf{Amends} §7.2; reclassifies A-14, A-15.

\textbf{The gap.} §7.2's taxonomy has no class for the most common thing Phase 3's audit passes actually produced: a repair that makes the code do what it was already specified and believed to do. Such a repair is not BP --- something observable changes --- but it is not SC either, because SC is defined as a scientific/model-semantics change, and the successful-call posterior is untouched. Forcing these into SC inflates them and, worse, dilutes SC precisely where it must stay sharp: D-28's prior truncation is a genuine SC change, and it should not sit in the same class as a fix to a failure path.

\textbf{The class.} \textbf{CF --- correctness repair implementing the already-declared target.} Gate profile: a rebaseline record wherever any observable changes, plus semantic tests; behavior-preserving language remains forbidden; SC's sign-off requirement does not attach.

\textbf{Reclassifications.} \textbf{A-14} (transactional \texttt{set\_window}) becomes \textbf{CF + API}. On a successful call the resulting state is what the pre-existing contract already promised; what changed is the failure path, which previously left windows and pairs mutated while provenance and support stayed stale --- plus one success-path observable, \texttt{cutoff\_provenance}, which changed from stale to correct. \textbf{A-15} (held-out realization) becomes \textbf{CF}: D-32 states the scientific target the held-out score always had, and the repair makes the computation match it.

\textbf{Conflict recorded.} \texttt{refactor-patches/\allowbreak{}phase3e/\allowbreak{}rebaseline\_record.md} classifies the \texttt{set\_window} provenance atomicity as \textbf{SC}. This document now classifies it CF + API. The in-repo record is not edited by this entry; the disagreement is recorded here so that a reader who finds both does not have to guess which is deliberate. Reconciling the in-repo record is a 3g documentation task.

\hypertarget{a-20}{%
\subsection{A-20 --- OP-13: placement of post-freeze configuration}\label{a-20}}

\textbf{Opened} 2026-07-28. \textbf{Class} DOC (proposal registration). \textbf{Amends} §17; marks OP-7.

OP-7 asks where \emph{cutoff} settings should live, and was scoped that way because at the freeze the cutoffs were the only contested configuration. Phase 3 has since added, as constructor keywords: the excitation support mode, \(\sigma\) bounds, a mass-table handle, five cutoff-related arguments plus two design scales, the data-contract mode, and the covariate CRS. These are not one kind of thing. The support mode and the \(\sigma\) bounds are model commitments (D-23, D-28). The cutoffs and the mass table are numerical devices (D-14, D-25). The contract mode and the CRS are input contracts. \texttt{standardize\_cov} is a preprocessing choice (D-10). They currently share one namespace and one provenance surface.

The proposal registered as OP-13 is that 3f separate them into distinct configuration objects along those lines, so that provenance can answer ``was this a modeling choice or a computational one?'' without the reader knowing the argument list by heart. It subsumes OP-7 rather than replacing it: OP-7's answer for cutoffs (a numerical configuration) is the same answer, now applied to a larger set. Recorded as a proposal, not a decision --- the frozen §10.f target names four configuration objects, and whether the post-freeze arguments partition cleanly into them has not been worked through.

\hypertarget{a-21}{%
\subsection{A-21 --- Pre-3f audit, decision freeze, and local correctness repairs}\label{a-21}}

\textbf{Opened} 2026-07-29. \textbf{Class} DOC + CF + API + SC (mixed; classified per item below). \textbf{Amends} A-9, A-14, A-15, A-17, A-18, A-20; settles OP-3, OP-4, OP-10, OP-13 configuration boundary; registers mandatory 3f acceptance requirements. \textbf{Does not begin} the broad 3f architectural refactor.

\paragraph{Editorial honesty (status-line layout).}
An earlier frozen-status-line editorial rewrite that re-laid Part~I status presentation is recorded here as \textbf{editorial/layout normalization}, not as a content amendment and not as an event that did not occur. Layout is not content (§ document rules); this sentence exists so a later reader does not have to invent a denial.

\paragraph{Configuration boundary for 3f (settles OP-13; binding).}
The five user-authored configurations will be strict, frozen Pydantic models:
\texttt{ModelConfig}, \texttt{PriorConfig}, \texttt{PartitionDecoderConfig}, \texttt{NumericalConfig}, \texttt{InferenceRunConfig}.
Prepared/runtime objects and provenance records remain dataclasses.
The actual event-indexed polygon mass table is prepared/runtime state outside configuration; its builder specification and numerical settings belong in \texttt{NumericalConfig}.
\texttt{spatial\_cov\_crs} is input metadata, not user-authored config.
Temporary \texttt{data\_contracts="report"} migration behavior must not become permanent frozen API (D-31 remains: migration instrument only).
These five models are \textbf{not} created in this pre-3f pass; the boundary is a binding 3f acceptance requirement.

\paragraph{OP-3 / OP-4 settled (SC / API; deliberate rebaseline).}
\texttt{standardize\_cov} defaults to off (\texttt{None}).
Accepted values are \texttt{None} and \texttt{"domain\_area"} only.
Legacy Booleans are rejected explicitly --- never silently reinterpreted.
Arbitrary user-supplied weights remain deferred.
This is a deliberate behavioral/API change relative to the legacy count-weighted default.

\paragraph{Polygon builder settings (binding 3f).}
\texttt{panel\_h\_m} and \texttt{gl\_order} are genuine \texttt{NumericalConfig} fields in 3f and must be plumbed consistently through preparation, validation, provenance, serialization, and table compatibility checks.
There must be no hidden-default validation that rejects a table built with the model's declared nondefault values.
\texttt{panel\_h\_m} is physically in metres only when the domain has a suitable projected CRS; do not describe arbitrary CRS-less domain units as metres.
No temporary competing configuration mechanism is introduced in this pre-3f pass.

\paragraph{Independent oracle accuracy gate (supersedes A-9's 10\%-of-cutoff claim).}
\supsd{A-9}{The claim that \(\tau_{\mathrm{abs}} = 5.3923\times 10^{-4}\) is universally ``10\% of the active default cutoff budget'' is retained as history and is no longer operative.}
The polygon-table oracle accuracy gate is set independently:
\[
  \tau_{\mathrm{abs}} \;=\; 10^{-5}.
\]
Under the default (\(4\sigma\)) design this remains below 10\% of that cutoff error, but numerical approximation and cutoff omission are \textbf{separate budgets} for nondefault settings.
Stronger existing empirical checks are not loosened merely because the declared gate is \(10^{-5}\).
Code names: \texttt{PRODUCTION\_TAU\_ABS} / \texttt{TAU\_ABS} \(= 10^{-5}\) for the adopted quad-built \(K{=}64\) path; \texttt{LEGACY\_SHOOTOUT\_TAU\_ABS} \(= 5.39\times 10^{-4}\) retained only for retrospective characterization of rejected oracle-built/PCHIP candidates (e.g.\ \(K{=}40\)). The derivative gate remains separately provisional under OP-12.

\paragraph{A-17 decoder relationship (correction).}
\supsd{A-17}{Overly strong invariance claims about \texttt{sp\_var\_mu} are retained as history where written and superseded here.}
The actual decoder relationship is
\[
  f_{xy} \;=\; e^{\texttt{sp\_var\_mu}}\, D(z),
  \qquad
  \lambda \;\propto\; e^{a_0 + f_{xy}}.
\]
Therefore \texttt{sp\_var\_mu}=0 is unit gain.
\texttt{sp\_var\_mu} can be strongly confounded with the intercept, latent decoder scale, and excitation share; estimation/calibration remains deferred beyond Phase~3.

\paragraph{Held-out acquisition vs D-26 / D-32 (CF + API; supersedes silent rebuild).}
D-32's scientific reading is preserved: held-out data are a complete standalone realization; event-indexed state is rebuilt from that realization.
\supsd{A-15}{Silent held-out mass-table construction as the acquisition path is retained as history and is no longer operative.}
Reconcile with D-26: polygon \texttt{log\_expected\_likelihood} hard-requires \texttt{mass\_table=} prepared for the held-out events; no table may be built implicitly; a training-event table must not be accepted for a different held-out realization; failure is loud and precedes scoring.
Rectangle mode is unaffected.
Nonfinite held-out inputs are rejected under the default contract mode; \texttt{cov\_ind} length is validated explicitly against the held-out event count.

\paragraph{Transactional \texttt{set\_window} sentinel semantics (CF + API).}
A private \texttt{\_UNSET} sentinel distinguishes omission from explicit \texttt{None}.
Required behavior: change only temporal; change only spatial; explicit \texttt{None} clears/restores untruncated defaults; zero-argument call is a true no-op; omitted \texttt{mass\_table} retains a compatible installed table and never builds silently; spatial design changes require an explicit replacement; validation failure rolls back every affected field; cutoff design scales persist so realized omission recomputes honestly.
For the untruncated temporal default, computational omitted mass is \texttt{None}/N/A --- do not report \(e^{-T/\beta}\), which confuses observation-horizon truncation with additional computational omission.
A-14/D-33 remain CF+API as reclassified in A-19.

\paragraph{Plain-Hawkes background union sampling (CF; D-30 downstream).}
\supsd{D-30}{The claim that every downstream consumer already used \texttt{PreparedDomain.union\_geometry} is retained as the invariant target; the plain-Hawkes no-covariate sampler was a remaining violator.}
After this repair, overlapping domain rows no longer duplicate physical area in the no-covariate background sampler; sampling uses the authoritative union geometry and \texttt{A\_area}.
D-30's downstream-union invariant is stated as satisfied only after this fix lands.

\paragraph{TruncatedLogNormal sampler (CF vs SC distinction).}
Choosing to truncate a user prior (D-28) changes the posterior and is an \textbf{SC}.
Fixing \texttt{TruncatedLogNormal.sample} so the already-declared truncated LogNormal is actually sampled on the positive interval (exponentiated draw; dtype-aware log-space normalization replacing the float32-inert \(10^{-300}\) guard) is a \textbf{CF}.

\paragraph{Saved-result provenance (binding 3f).}
For 3f, \texttt{save\_rslts} must persist: all resolved configurations; cutoff and numerical provenance; software/decoder/implementation identities; prepared-data/domain/event fingerprints; polygon mass-table identity, builder specification, integrity hash, and external reference --- without duplicating the large mass-table payload in every result file.
\texttt{load\_rslts} must hard-fail on incompatibility; any bypass must be explicitly named unsafe; warning-only or silent loading is not acceptable.
Not implemented as a stopgap serialization format in this pre-3f pass.

\paragraph{Mandatory 3f acceptance requirements (register; do not claim already done).}
\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item Five strict frozen Pydantic configs named above; prepared/runtime/provenance dataclasses.
\item Complete saved-result provenance and strict load compatibility.
\item Persistent cutoff design scales (landed in pre-3f for \texttt{set\_window}; must survive the 3f config split).
\item Sentinel mutation semantics (landed in pre-3f; must survive the 3f config split).
\item Usable \texttt{panel\_h\_m}/\texttt{gl\_order} numerical fields end-to-end.
\item Actual mass table outside configuration.
\item Decoder-contract dry run (§14 leg).
\item Final legacy-\texttt{args} removal only after all consumers migrate.
\end{itemize}

\paragraph{What does \emph{not} block starting 3f.}
Guide rewrite, conflict C6, polygon-excitation SBC, and the final Stage-3 \(R{=}200\) exit rerun remain later obligations as already assigned; they do not block the start of substantive 3f.

\paragraph{Pin wording repair.}
Replace the informal claim ``no rectangle-regime numerical result changed'' with the precise claim: \textbf{no result changed in the four pinned rectangle configurations} (machine-local golden pins).

\paragraph{OP-10.}
Resolved consistently with the existing principle (D-29): \(\sigma\) bounds are use-case quantities with provenance disclosure; the projected-CRS package default \(\texttt{max\_sigma}=5\,\mathrm{km}\) is retained as a disclosed convenience default, not as a claim that bounds are software properties. Explicit lower-\(\sigma\) policy preserved; no extrapolation or heuristic mass-table extension.

Warn when inferred/evaluated \(\sigma\) approaches either declared bound; remove dead \texttt{\_install\_excitation\_support}; correct the stale \texttt{\_sim\_cox} docstring; refresh agent guidance tip/path to the living \texttt{phase3\_record.tex}.

\subparagraph{Public-simulator overlap duplication (CF; D-30).}
Internal plain-Hawkes no-covariate background sampling already used \texttt{PreparedDomain.union\_geometry}, but both public \texttt{Hawkes\_Model.simulate} and \texttt{LGCP\_Model.simulate} still filtered via \texttt{points.sjoin(self.A)}, which duplicated a physical point once per overlapping input row.

Public-path tests cover controlled overlap points (Hawkes and LGCP), boundary membership, single and disjoint polygons, unchanged array-rectangle behavior, in-union membership, and one returned row per in-domain generated event.

\subparagraph{Production-path polygon-table value gate (DOC + test; retains central FD).}
New tests construct exclusively via \texttt{bstpp.polygon\_mass.prepare\_polygon\_mass\_table} and evaluate via the production online Hermite path (\texttt{make\_table\_eval} / \texttt{hermite\_polygon\_masses}), with \(K{=}64\), \texttt{panel\_h\_m}=20, \texttt{gl\_order}=16, uncut and finite \(w_s{=}400\).
Measured maxima on the analysis machine (process \texttt{jax\_enable\_x64} left \texttt{False}):
uncut \(\max|\Delta M| = 5.95\times 10^{-7}\); finite-\(w_s\) \(\max|\Delta M| = 6.14\times 10^{-7}\); both \(\le \texttt{PRODUCTION\_TAU\_ABS}=10^{-5}\).
Float32 online evaluation: same order (\(5.95\times 10^{-7}\) / \(6.14\times 10^{-7}\)); no hidden reliance on global x64.
Derivative maxima (reported separately under OP-12, not conflated with the value gate): uncut \(1.24\times 10^{-6}\); finite-\(w_s\) \(1.07\times 10^{-6}\) (both \(\ll\) provisional \texttt{TAU\_DERIV}).
Central finite-difference knot slopes are therefore retained.
Historical shootout gates remain historical (\texttt{LEGACY\_SHOOTOUT\_TAU\_ABS}); experiment-only confirmation tests may continue characterizing rejected candidates under local historical tolerances and must not be rewritten as if \(10^{-5}\) were their original preregistered gate.
OP-12 derivative policy remains explicitly unresolved.

\subparagraph{Held-out nonfinite rejection (CF + API).}
Held-out scoring now rejects NaN, \(+\infty\), and \(-\infty\) in every \texttt{data\_contracts} mode, including \texttt{"report"}, before \texttt{\_scale\_xyt}, mass-table compatibility checks, or scoring.
Errors name affected field(s) and counts without dumping arrays.
\texttt{cov\_ind} length continues to be validated against the held-out event count.
The explicit held-out polygon \texttt{mass\_table=} requirement is unchanged.
No silent drop/clip/repair of invalid held-out rows (supersedes the legacy report-mode \texttt{dropna} path).

\subparagraph{Stable TruncatedLogNormal normalization (CF).}
Choosing to truncate a user prior remains an \textbf{SC} (D-28).
Completing \texttt{\_log\_z} with a stable \(\log(1-e^{x})\) branch (\texttt{log(-expm1)} near zero; \texttt{log1p(-exp)} otherwise), while preserving CDF-versus-survival selection, is a \textbf{CF} of the already-declared distribution (alongside the earlier exponentiated-sample repair).
Also fixed: re-truncation of NumPyro \texttt{TruncatedNormal} wrappers now reads \texttt{base\_dist.loc}/\texttt{scale} (was \texttt{AttributeError} on \texttt{TwoSidedTruncatedDistribution}).

\paragraph{Continuation (2026-07-29) --- corrections on audited tip \texttt{04799d9}.}
\textbf{Audited base tip:} \texttt{04799d9} (A-21 continuation documentation tip; includes the \texttt{938e32b}+follow-up series).
\textbf{Implementation commits:} \texttt{e37ef50} (RED one-sided/non-Normal prior), \texttt{ac6101c} (GREEN prior adapter), \texttt{c4b38ff} (RED rectangle grid-edge simulate), \texttt{58d4e07} (GREEN rectangle bounds membership); documentation/annotation in this series tip.

\subparagraph{One-sided pre-truncated Normal prior handling (CF of declared truncation).}
Under pinned NumPyro 0.15, \texttt{LeftTruncatedDistribution} exposes \texttt{.low} but not \texttt{.high}, and \texttt{RightTruncatedDistribution} exposes \texttt{.high} but not \texttt{.low}.
The adapter previously assumed both attributes and raised \texttt{AttributeError} on one-sided wrappers.
Correction: treat a missing lower bound as \(-\infty\) and a missing upper bound as \(+\infty\); intersect with the newly declared \([\texttt{min\_sigma}^2,\texttt{max\_sigma}^2]\); reject empty, equal, reversed, or nonfinite-invalid intersections with the existing \texttt{ValueError} convention; preserve Normal \texttt{loc}/\texttt{scale}.
Imposing truncation on a user prior remains \textbf{SC} (D-28); correcting the already-declared truncation implementation is \textbf{CF}.

\subparagraph{Explicit rejection of truncated non-Normal bases (CF; no API broadening).}
Generic truncated wrappers also wrap Cauchy, Student-t, Logistic, and other families that expose \texttt{loc}/\texttt{scale}.
The prior adapter now accepts a pre-truncated wrapper through the TruncatedNormal branch \emph{only} when \texttt{base\_dist} is genuinely \texttt{numpyro.distributions.Normal}; truncated Cauchy / Student-t fail loudly with \texttt{TypeError} rather than silently becoming TruncatedNormal.
Supported families remain HalfNormal, LogNormal, TruncatedNormal (Normal base), and TruncatedLogNormal --- no new prior family.

\subparagraph{Rectangle computational-grid simulation duplication (CF; D-30 adjacent).}
Polygon public simulate already filtered via \texttt{PreparedDomain.union\_geometry}, but rectangle/array domains still used \texttt{points.sjoin(self.A)} where \texttt{self.A} is the \(25\times 25\) computational grid.
A physical point on an internal grid edge matched two cells (two output rows); a vertex matched four.
Correction: shared \texttt{PreparedDomain.covers\_xy} --- closed interval membership against authoritative \texttt{prepared\_domain.bounds} for rectangle/array domains; \texttt{union.covers} for polygons.
Public-path tests cover internal edge/vertex, each outer edge and corners, outside exclusion, interior points, Hawkes and LGCP rectangle modes, one retained row per generated in-domain event, and no regression for polygon / single-row / disjoint cases.
No \texttt{drop\_duplicates}; public output schema unchanged.
Adjacent audit (not fixed here): rectangle no-covariate background sampling still draws per computational cell (\texttt{geo\_df = self.A}) by design; field/covariate membership continues to use max-id dedup (D-22); \texttt{\_plot\_grid} overlay remains plotting-only.

\subparagraph{Production slope-description correction (DOC).}
Production \texttt{bstpp.polygon\_mass} builds knot slopes by central finite differences (\texttt{SLOPE\_METHOD = central\_fd\_log\_sigma}); the module docstring incorrectly attributed forward-mode AD to production.
Corrected to distinguish production (host NumPy/SciPy + central FD) from historical shootout/experimental JAX forward-mode AD builders.
No change to \texttt{PRODUCTION\_TAU\_ABS}\(=10^{-5}\), \texttt{TAU\_DERIV}, \(K{=}64\), \texttt{panel\_h\_m}=20, \texttt{gl\_order}=16, or the production builder.
Historical shootout records are left as historical.

\subparagraph{Kernel-specific cutoff and scale API gates (CF + API).}
Exact-type gates: \texttt{Temporal\_Exponential} owns \texttt{mean\_lag\_days}, temporal tolerance/design-scale, and exponential omitted mass; \texttt{Spatial\_Symmetric\_Gaussian} owns mandatory \texttt{sigmax\_2}, \texttt{min\_sigma}/\texttt{max\_sigma} truncation, spatial tolerance/design-scale, and Gaussian omitted mass.
\texttt{Temporal\_Power\_Law} and custom rectangle spatial triggers keep explicit physical cutoffs and \texttt{get\_par\_names()} priors; shared \texttt{cutoff\_tol} requires both axes.
Polygon exact-type Gaussian gate unchanged.
Implements the already-recorded kernel-specific contract --- not a new scientific-model decision.

\subparagraph{Untouched-axis cutoff provenance (CF + API).}
\texttt{Hawkes\_Model.set\_window} keeps an \texttt{\_UNSET} axis \texttt{TemporalCutoffRecord}/\texttt{SpatialCutoffRecord} verbatim and re-resolves only explicitly supplied axes into prospective per-axis records before a transactional commit.

\texttt{LGCP\_Model.set\_window} raises \texttt{NotImplementedError} for all calls (including zero-argument), without populating unused Hawkes window state.

\subparagraph{CRS-less / effective-panel resolution guard (CF + IV/API).}
Named \texttt{MAX\_PANEL\_TO\_MIN\_SIGMA\_RATIO}${}={}8$ rejects \texttt{prepare\_polygon\_mass\_table} when \texttt{effective\_panel\_h}/\texttt{min\_sigma} exceeds the limit after converting \texttt{panel\_h\_m} into domain-coordinate units.
Reports effective panel, \texttt{min\_sigma}, ratio, allowed ratio, and how to pass a smaller \texttt{panel\_h\_m}.
\texttt{PRODUCTION\_TAU\_ABS}${}={}10^{-5}$ unchanged; \texttt{jax\_enable\_x64} unchanged.

\subparagraph{Grid helper, kernel helpers, and short-MCMC repairs (CF / CF+API).}
\texttt{get\_grid\_post\_mean} for plain Hawkes covariates derives \texttt{b\_0} or \texttt{w}@\texttt{X\_s} locally with intersection-area weighting and no plot-side mutation.
\texttt{dist\_euclid} reshapes 1-D \texttt{z} from \texttt{z}; \texttt{exp\_sq\_kernel} adds diagonal noise only when \texttt{x is z}.
\texttt{run\_mcmc} skips \texttt{print\_summary} with a clear warning when retained draws are below NumPyro's diagnostic minimum of 4; samples are preserved.

\subparagraph{Dependency metadata (API + reproducibility).}
Canonical \texttt{requirements-runtime.txt} feeds \texttt{setup.py} \texttt{install\_requires}; \texttt{requirements.txt} includes it and retains notebook/dev extras separately; \texttt{MANIFEST.in} ships the runtime file in sdists.
Critical pins: \texttt{geopandas>=1.0}, \texttt{jax}/\texttt{jaxlib==0.4.23}, \texttt{numpy>=1.24.1,<2}, \texttt{numpyro==0.15.0}, \texttt{scipy>=1.9.0,<1.13}.

Does not claim resolution of guide-identity conflict C6.

\subparagraph{Deferred observations (recorded; not fixed here).}
\textit{Membership predicate consolidation.} \texttt{validate\_events} and simulation filtering still express canonical domain membership through separate vectorized/scalar paths; results are correct but the simulation path is slower and duplicate expressions could drift.
Register consolidation into one vectorized \texttt{PreparedDomain.covers\_xy(array\_x, array\_y)} as Phase 3f work.

\textit{Extreme float32 TruncatedLogNormal behavior.} \texttt{\_log\_z} is mathematically correct and approximately normalized to about $10^{-5}$ relative interval width; extremely narrow float32 intervals exhibit cancellation, but \texttt{\_log\_z} is value-independent so this is an additive constant and does not change the posterior in current BSTPP uses; \texttt{low < high} validation is inactive when NumPyro leaves \texttt{validate\_args=None}; sufficiently extreme-tail \texttt{TruncatedNormal} saturation can produce an out-of-support exponentiated sample.
No ad hoc clipping; numerical hardening is later work unless a principled correction with independent evidence becomes necessary for supported sigma ranges.
