\hypertarget{a-22}{%
\subsection{A-22 --- Phase 3f opened at \texttt{fe44de4}}\label{a-22}}

\textbf{Opened} 2026-08-04. \textbf{Class} DOC. \textbf{Establishes} D-35. \textbf{Amends} A-21's ``does not begin 3f'' framing; records the pre-3f closeout verdict.

\paragraph{Opening.}
Phase 3f opens at tip \texttt{fe44de4} against the pre-registered §9 criterion in \texttt{pre3f\_stabilization\_audit\_protocol.md}. Closeout artifacts live under \texttt{refactor-patches/pre-3f-stabilization/}. The phase is many commits; each work package gets its own brief. This entry is not a mandate for an open-ended rewrite.

\paragraph{Declared changes owned by 3f (from the closeout ledger).}
\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item \textbf{G1} --- membership predicate consolidated to a single \texttt{covers\_xy} used by validation and simulation. Structural; no numerical change expected.
\item \textbf{G2} --- \texttt{save\_rslts} implements the A-21 save/load contract with provenance and hard-fail incompatibility. Declared \textbf{API} change; Lane~B strict xfail flips to a passing assertion in the same commit.
\end{itemize}

\paragraph{Ordering.}
Bottom-up, adapter last. Introduce config and prepared objects \emph{behind} the existing \texttt{args} structure; the adapter translates. \texttt{args} remains the safety net for pin-equivalent intermediate commits. OP-8 (legacy-\texttt{args} removal) is the final work package, not the first. Seam~4 (RNG ownership) is already settled by pre-3f Commit~B (\texttt{6ba2194}) and is a constraint the new objects must honor.

\paragraph{Enforcement rule (D-35).}
Every freeze ships with its enforcement in the same commit. A config object that does not validate its own invariants at construction is not done. \texttt{NumericalConfig} carrying \texttt{panel\_h\_m} and \texttt{gl\_order} must assert the Commit~C measured-budget policy (\texttt{PRODUCTION\_TAU\_ABS}${}={}10^{-5}$, \texttt{BUDGET\_REFERENCE\_GL\_ORDER}${}={}32$) at construction, together with $\sigma$-bound coherence and support-mode compatibility --- not leave those checks solely to downstream call sites.

\paragraph{Gate profile (every 3f commit).}
\supsd{A-25}{Full suite}\ \amdnew{A-25} \texttt{pytest tests/}, enforced by \texttt{testpaths = ["tests"]} in \texttt{pyproject.toml} since \texttt{82e9c09}. The supersession is enforcement-first: WP1.4c made the configuration name the suite, and this profile now states the invocation that configuration already enforces, rather than the informal ``full suite'' the profile carried while bare \texttt{pytest} still swept the \texttt{refactor-patches/} archive. Lane~B covering array; four-config pins (\texttt{PIN\_DIFFS 0 MATCH}); ruff on touch; \texttt{jax\_enable\_x64} unchanged. A pin diff during a behavior-preserving commit stops the phase for human decision.
\amdnew{A-25} \emph{Gate-run provenance (amends this paragraph).} Every captured gate run records \texttt{git status --porcelain} alongside its result, in the same capture file. This applies to \emph{all} gate runs, not only documentation-only ones. The reason: a gate figure produced in a tree containing files absent from the commit is unverifiable, and in WP1.4c one such figure entered the record as a false statement about the repository's own structure --- a collection count measured over three untracked SBC smoke files was recorded as evidence that the archive under \texttt{refactor-patches/} contained tests the repository does not contain (A-25; \texttt{findings\_ledger.md}, ``Suite invocation change''). An empty \texttt{porcelain} result makes the provenance explicit; a non-empty one enumerates exactly what was present that is not in the commit.
\amdnew{A-26} \emph{Extension (A-26).} Every ad-hoc probe capture also records \texttt{bstpp.\_\_file\_\_}. A stale \texttt{bstpp} installed in \texttt{site-packages} shadows the repository for any script whose directory is not the repository root --- \texttt{pytest} is unaffected because it inserts the rootdir first, ad-hoc probes are not. This is the same failure class as the untracked test files that put a false figure into the WP1.4c ledger: the capture looks correct and describes a different object. It was hit during A-26's own Step~1 probe, whose output is cited in this register. One line makes the shadow visible instead of silent.
\amdnew{A-24} \emph{Documentation-only profile (amends this paragraph).} A commit qualifies as documentation-only when its diff touches no file that any gate reads. The rule is stated by what reads a file, not by directory: e.g.\ \texttt{refactor-patches/pre-3f-stabilization/covering\_array\_rows.json} is read by \texttt{tests/test\_lane\_b\_config\_matrix.py}, and \texttt{refactor-patches/pin\_check\_v2.py} with \texttt{baselines-2026-07/pins.json} are the pin harness --- those are not documentation-only paths; an inert document beside them (e.g.\ \texttt{traceability\_matrix.md}) may be. On a documentation-only commit, pins and suite are recorded as \emph{not run} with the reason and the file list; the discriminating gates are the document build (when the toolchain permits), the structural \texttt{\textbackslash hypertarget} check, and any content checks that catch category errors the suite cannot see. Anything that touches a gate-read file runs the full profile above. Declaration and first use land together (this A-24 paragraph; first use in the same commit that repairs the §12.2 transposed row).


\hypertarget{a-23}{%
\subsection{A-23 --- Config objects as frozen dataclasses (deviation from A-21 Pydantic)}\label{a-23}}

\textbf{Opened} 2026-08-04. \textbf{Class} DOC / DEF. \textbf{Amends} A-21 configuration-boundary paragraph (``strict, frozen Pydantic models''). \textbf{Decided by} Terhi.

\paragraph{Deviation.}
\supsd{A-21}{The five user-authored configurations will be strict, frozen Pydantic models.}
Phase~3f implements the five named objects --- \texttt{ModelConfig}, \texttt{PriorConfig}, \texttt{PartitionDecoderConfig}, \texttt{NumericalConfig}, \texttt{InferenceRunConfig} --- as \texttt{@dataclass(frozen=True)} with validation in \texttt{\_\_post\_init\_\_}, constructed through a single factory per object. No new runtime dependency is added.

\paragraph{Reasoning (WP1.0).}
\begin{enumerate}
\tightlist
\item Pydantic v2 ships a compiled Rust core; adopting it perturbs the declared environment during the one phase whose central claim is bit-identical trace equivalence \emph{within} that environment, and it touches the runtime pin set guarded by \texttt{test\_wheel\_requires\_dist\_contains\_critical\_runtime\_pins}.
\item These objects are constructed internally behind the adapter from arguments that already cleared data contracts. Pydantic's value is parsing untrusted external input; every validation that matters here is a custom validator either way.
\item Silent type coercion is an active hazard where float32-vs-float64 is load-bearing and JAX and NumPy arrays are not interchangeable.
\end{enumerate}

\paragraph{Reversibility constraints.}
All validation remains in \texttt{\_\_post\_init\_\_} behind a single factory --- a later migration to Pydantic should then be mechanical. Do not mix idioms: if Pydantic is revisited at G2 (schema versioning / validated deserialization), all five objects migrate together or none do. Even at G2 the configs will hold NumPy arrays and mass tables that Pydantic does not serialize natively, so custom serializers are required regardless.


\hypertarget{a-24}{%
\subsection{A-24 --- Closeout decisions adopted as D-36--D-39}\label{a-24}}

\textbf{Opened} 2026-08-04. \textbf{Class} DOC. \textbf{Establishes} D-36--D-39; opens OP-14--OP-17. \textbf{Adopts} the decisions proposed in \texttt{refactor-patches/pre-3f-stabilization/phase3e\_closeout\_and\_3f\_readiness.tex}, renumbered. \textbf{Amends} \amdnew{A-25} A-22 (gate-profile paragraph, documentation-only profile); §8, §12, §17.

\paragraph{Renumbering.}
The closeout drafted these as D-35--D-38. D-35 was allocated concurrently by A-22 to the freeze-ships-with-enforcement rule. They land here as D-36--D-39. Mapping for a reader holding the closeout: closeout D-35 $\rightarrow$ register D-36; closeout D-36 $\rightarrow$ register D-37; closeout D-37 $\rightarrow$ register D-38; closeout D-38 $\rightarrow$ register D-39.

\paragraph{Retroactivity.}
D-36 and D-37 were pre-registered in \texttt{refactor-patches/pre-3f-stabilization/pre3f\_stabilization\_audit\_protocol.md} (blocker rule §5; finite readiness / iteration cap §9) and applied at tip \texttt{fe44de4} when A-22 opened Phase~3f; they are adopted into this register on 2026-08-04. Stating the retroactivity is required: a rule that appears in the register after the event it authorized, without that sentence, reads as post-hoc justification --- the failure mode the pre-3f series was convened to eliminate.

\paragraph{R1--R9 readiness instance (3f; not part of D-37).}
The Phase~3f opening instance of D-37 is the closed readiness rule of closeout §5 (R1--R9), evaluated on one frozen candidate tip and recorded in \texttt{refactor-patches/pre-3f-stabilization/readiness\_report.md} (headline \texttt{READY FOR 3F}; candidate tip \texttt{359c031}; §8 battery tip \texttt{c5e4871}; iteration 3 of 3). Cross-walk, citing the closeout text and the artifacts that discharged each condition:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item \textbf{R1} (every known blocker under the blocker test fixed or reachability-recorded) --- closeout §5 R1 / protocol §5; readiness report: B1--B6 closed; ledger in \texttt{findings\_ledger.md}.
\item \textbf{R2} (focused RED-before-GREEN regression per fixed blocker) --- closeout §5 R2; readiness report: Commit~A RED at \texttt{143d219}; B/C CF; E test.
\item \textbf{R3} (compatibility matrix passes) --- closeout §5 R3; discharged by the executable Lane~B gate (see §6 resolution below), not by the unimplemented closeout deliverable \texttt{tests/test\_config\_matrix.py}.
\item \textbf{R4} (standing gates on the candidate tip) --- closeout §5 R4; readiness §8 battery: suite, pins, Lane~B, polygon I11, ruff, \texttt{jax\_enable\_x64}.
\item \textbf{R5} (register-to-test traceability matrix exists) --- closeout §5 R5 / closeout §7; artifact \texttt{refactor-patches/pre-3f-stabilization/traceability\_matrix.md}.
\item \textbf{R6} (audit coverage map exists; no 3f-seam module \texttt{unaudited}) --- closeout §5 R6 / closeout §8; artifact \texttt{refactor-patches/pre-3f-stabilization/audit\_coverage\_map.md}.
\item \textbf{R7} (OP-3, OP-4, OP-7, OP-8, OP-13 dispositions recorded for 3f) --- closeout §5 R7; A-21/A-22/A-23 and the readiness residual-gap table.
\item \textbf{R8} (one final bounded audit finds no new blocker) --- closeout §5 R8; termination rule closeout §9 / protocol §9.
\item \textbf{R9} (every remaining non-blocking finding in the ledger with owner) --- closeout §5 R9; readiness residual gaps G1--G3, G6--G8.
\end{itemize}

\paragraph{Three-iteration cap.}
Recorded alongside the readiness instance: the final bounded audit is capped at three iterations (closeout §9; protocol §9: ``Cap: three iterations''). Iteration~$k{+}1$ must differ from iteration~$k$ in auditor or method. The 3f opening used iteration 3 of 3.

\paragraph{§6 resolution (why D-38 names files, not section numbers).}
D-38 cites the executable Lane~B gate by filename --- \texttt{tests/test\_lane\_b\_config\_matrix.py} and \texttt{refactor-patches/pre-3f-stabilization/covering\_array\_rows.json} --- not by any document's section number. Two reasons, both load-bearing:

\begin{enumerate}
\tightlist
\item Closeout §6's proposed deliverable \texttt{tests/test\_config\_matrix.py} is not implemented. D-35 requires a freeze to ship with its enforcement in the same commit; binding §12 to a gate with no code would manufacture the register/gate gap this series exists to close, at the moment of adoption.
\item The bridge ``Lane~B is the implementation of closeout §6'' is not verified true: the axis sets differ (closeout five configuration axes against a nine-axis covering array) and the assertion families are not the covering array's rows. An unverified equivalence in the governing record is worse than no claim.
\end{enumerate}

Section numbers collide across the two committed documents (closeout §6 = matrix design; protocol §6 = audit lanes); filenames do not.

\paragraph{Coverage delta (closeout §6 design origin $\rightarrow$ Lane~B).}
Closeout §6 remains the design origin of Lane~B and must not evaporate into a filename. It specifies five configuration axes (C1--C5) and five assertion families (P1--P5). Mapping below was read from \texttt{tests/test\_lane\_b\_config\_matrix.py} against that design; statuses are \emph{asserts} / \emph{partially asserts} / \emph{does not assert}. Every partial or missing item is a recorded unaudited state under D-39.

\subparagraph{Assertion families.}
\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item \textbf{P1 Admissibility} --- \supsd{A-26}{\textbf{partially asserts.}} \amdnew{A-26} \textbf{asserts}, from A-26 / WP1.4e-1: both bare-\texttt{raises} sites now carry \texttt{match=}, OP-17 is closed, and no test in the affected set uses a bare \texttt{pytest.raises} for any of these invariants. The grade below is the state as recorded at A-24 and is retained because the reasoning that produced it is what D-40 was written from. Closeout P1 requires every rejection in the matrix to assert error type \emph{and} a message substring, not bare \texttt{pytest.raises(Exception)}. Forced reject rows and most covering-array rejects do so; two matrix rejections do not --- \texttt{test\_lane\_b\_rejected\_set\_window\_rolls\_back\_whole\_state} (parametrized \texttt{bad\_call}) and the stale-table install in \texttt{test\_lane\_b\_polygon\_incompatible\_spatial\_change\_rolls\_back} use bare \texttt{pytest.raises(ValueError)} with no \texttt{match=}. Both sit inside P4 rollback tests where the state assertion is the point; that does not change the P1 grade. \textbf{Why it matters:} \texttt{NumericalConfigError} subclasses \texttt{ValueError}, so a bare \texttt{pytest.raises(ValueError)} cannot distinguish the constructor's error from \texttt{set\_window}'s. A fully enforced P1 would have caught the entry-path error asymmetry at the moment WP1.4b created it --- the unasserted assertion family and the defect that slipped past it are the same gap, the concrete instance of D-39's claim. Recorded as OP-17; discharged by the D-40 unification work, not by writing \texttt{match=} against the current split (that would freeze the asymmetry into the suite).
\item \textbf{P2 Leg consistency} --- \textbf{partially asserts.} Lane~B checks \texttt{excitation\_support is args['excitation\_support']}, mass-table install identity, and \texttt{cutoff\_provenance is not None}. It does \emph{not} assert at every covered success that the event term, compensator, and simulator consume the same support/cutoff atoms (closeout P2). Recorded as OP-14.
\item \textbf{P3 Provenance} --- \textbf{partially asserts.} Success paths require provenance presence; \texttt{save\_rslts} round-trip is present as a \emph{strict xfail} (ledger G2 / A-22 declared API change). Covering-array success does not assert bit-identical untouched-axis provenance after an accepted \texttt{set\_window}. Recorded as OP-15.
\item \textbf{P4 Transactionality} --- \textbf{asserts.} Rejected \texttt{set\_window} paths compare whole observable state to a pre-call snapshot (\texttt{\_assert\_window\_state\_unchanged}).
\item \textbf{P5 Numerical budget} --- \textbf{asserts.} \texttt{test\_lane\_b\_polygon\_shipped\_defaults\_meet\_panel\_budget} requires \texttt{measured\_max\_abs\_residual} $\le$ \texttt{PRODUCTION\_TAU\_ABS} at shipped defaults, with \texttt{BUDGET\_REFERENCE\_GL\_ORDER} recorded in install provenance (production measures against the elevated-GL reference in host float64; \texttt{BUDGET\_REFERENCE\_GL\_ORDER}${}={}32$, oracle bound $10^{-6}$). \textbf{Finding:} contrary to the expectation that P5 would remain unimplemented behind the closeout's former \texttt{[[FILL]]} anchors, the measured residual gate is live in production and asserted by Lane~B. The panel-ratio check in the same test is an additional prefilter, not a substitute for the residual.
\end{itemize}

\subparagraph{Configuration axes.}
\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item \textbf{C1 Model family} --- covered by Lane~B axis \texttt{model\_family} \{\texttt{hawkes}, \texttt{cox\_hawkes}, \texttt{lgcp}\}.
\item \textbf{C2 Trigger specification} --- covered by remapping, not by identity: closeout C2 is one axis with levels \{built-in Gaussian$\otimes$exponential, power-law temporal, custom\}; Lane~B factors this into \texttt{temporal\_trigger} $\times$ \texttt{spatial\_trigger}. Not an unaudited gap; the factorization differs from the closeout design.
\item \textbf{C3 Excitation support mode} --- covered by Lane~B axis \texttt{support}.
\item \textbf{C4 Cutoff input} --- covered by Lane~B axis \texttt{cutoff\_input}.
\item \textbf{C5 State path} --- \textbf{partially asserts.} Closeout levels are \{\texttt{constructor} only, \texttt{set\_window} success, \texttt{set\_window} rejected\}; Lane~B \texttt{entry\_path} is \{\texttt{constructor}, \texttt{set\_window}\}. Reject outcomes are separate forced/rollback tests, not pairwise-crossed covering-array levels. Recorded as OP-16.
\end{itemize}

\subparagraph{Extensions beyond closeout C1--C5.}
A map that lists only shortfalls would imply that everything unlisted matches the design exactly, which is false in both directions. Levels below are taken from \texttt{refactor-patches/pre-3f-stabilization/covering\_array\_rows.json} (17 rows). No OP: these are coverage the design did not ask for, recorded so the declaration is symmetric under D-39.
\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item \texttt{builder\_numerics} --- \{\texttt{default\_panel\_gl}, \texttt{guided\_small\_panel}, \texttt{nondefault\_gl\_order}\}.
\item \texttt{standardization} --- \{\texttt{bool\_rejected}, \texttt{domain\_area}, \texttt{none}\}.
\item \texttt{sigma\_bounds} --- \{\texttt{both}, \texttt{custom\_spatial\_rejects}, \texttt{neither\_rect}, \texttt{polygon\_min\_required}\}.
\end{itemize}

\paragraph{Decisions as adopted (register text).}
See Part~I §8 rows D-36--D-39 (tagged \amdnew{A-24}). Material departures from the closeout draft, beyond renumbering: D-36 generalizes ``the surface 3f will freeze into typed configuration objects'' to ``the surface the next phase will freeze''; D-37 is the readiness \emph{principle}, with R1--R9 kept in this entry as the 3f instance; D-38 names the executable Lane~B filenames per the ruling above; D-39 adds same-commit traceability-row enforcement.

\paragraph{§12.2 transposed-row correction.}
The D-38 gate was first recorded (commit \texttt{beb0482}) as a row in the §12.2 matrix. The matrix's row axis is Phase, so placing ``Lane~B matrix (D-38)'' in column~1 was a category error: the thirteen fields then landed in gate-family columns by position, with the substantive marks falling in Unit/property tests (column~4) and Stage~3 SBC (column~8). Column~8 contradicted D-38's own non-replacement clause one page earlier. The row was syntactically valid at thirteen fields, so it compiled and the structural \texttt{\textbackslash hypertarget} check passed --- neither existing gate could see this class of error. The correction relocates D-38 to a separate standing-gates table immediately after §12.2 (additional to the matrix; replaces nothing in it). A row that compiled, passed the structural check, and contradicted the decision it implements is a useful instance of what D-39 is about.

\paragraph{Documentation-only gate profile (declaration).}
A-22's gate-profile paragraph is amended in the same commit as this correction (see the \amdnew{A-24} clause under A-22): a commit is documentation-only when its diff touches no file that any gate reads; pins and suite are then recorded as not run with the reason and the file list; the discriminating gates are the document build, the structural check, and content checks such as the §12.2 column-1 phase-name check. Declaration and first use land together --- relaxing a declared profile without recording the relaxation is the process form of the gap D-35 exists to close.

The failed attempt is not evidence of a defect at the commit; it is evidence that the capture set contains runs from trees that are not the commit, which is the same class of problem the provenance requirement below exists to make visible.

\paragraph{Suite figures --- re-measured, and the correction.}
\textbf{Commit messages are immutable, and this entry is where the record is corrected.} The figures below are \texttt{tests/} collection at each commit, re-measured for this entry in a clean detached \texttt{git worktree}; every capture carries its own \texttt{git status --porcelain}, empty in all eleven runs (\texttt{results/\allowbreak{}\_a25\_collect\_*.txt}). The bare column is \texttt{pytest --collect-only -q} with no path, shown because the difference between the two columns \emph{is} the archive that WP1.4c excluded.
