\noindent Three points, and the third is the one that matters.

\begin{enumerate}
\tightlist
\item \textbf{The brief that commissioned this entry named \texttt{296bdf5}, \texttt{f88ac29} and \texttt{560524d} as stating totals from a different tree. Only \texttt{560524d} does.} \texttt{296bdf5} and \texttt{f88ac29} state no total at all --- they assert ``full suite EXIT:0'' and nothing more, so there is no figure in them to be wrong. This disagreement is recorded rather than resolved in either direction.
\item \texttt{560524d}'s stated 558 is two short of the 560 its own tree collects, and the commits on either side of it agree with their measurements. Re-executed here rather than inferred from the collection count: \texttt{pytest tests/ -q} at \texttt{560524d} in a clean worktree returns \textbf{557 passed, 2 skipped, 1 xfailed}, \texttt{EXIT:0} (\texttt{results/\allowbreak{}\_a25\_suite\_560524d.txt}) --- the same shape the message reports, with \textbf{two more passes}. WP1.4b added rows to \texttt{tests/\allowbreak{}test\_set\_window\_sentinel.py}, and the stated figure is what a run taken before the last two of them existed would produce. The commit is green; what is wrong is that its gate line reports a tree that is not the commit.
\item The \(\Delta\) column is constant at \textbf{7} for every commit before \texttt{82e9c09}, and 7 is the whole of \texttt{refactor-patches/\allowbreak{}phase0/\allowbreak{}test\_identities.py} (six test functions, one parametrized over two values). \textbf{The archive that \texttt{testpaths} excluded is one file, not a directory of them.} The WP1.4c ledger recorded 582 \(\to\) 560, a delta of 22, and attributed the excess to ``SBC smokes under \texttt{refactor-patches/}''. There are no SBC test files under \texttt{refactor-patches/} in the repository. The 582 was measured in a working tree holding three untracked files --- \texttt{refactor-patches/\allowbreak{}sbc1/\allowbreak{}test\_sbc\_smoke.py}, \texttt{refactor-patches/\allowbreak{}sbc2/\allowbreak{}test\_sbc\_smoke\_v2.py}, \texttt{refactor-patches/\allowbreak{}test\_sbc\_smoke\_v3.py} --- contributing the other fifteen tests. The ledger is corrected in the same commit as this entry.
\end{enumerate}

\noindent That third point is why A-22's gate profile now carries the provenance requirement (\amdnew{A-25} clause under A-22): the wrong figure was not a slip in arithmetic but a \emph{true measurement of the wrong object}, and nothing in the artifact distinguished it from a right one. A collection count is a statement about which files the repository contains, so a contaminated one enters the record as a false structural claim --- here, the existence of a category of archived SBC tests that is not under version control.

\paragraph{Phase0 triage outcome.}
Not duplicated here. The classification, the per-failure evidence, and the live-coverage inventory are in \texttt{refactor-patches/\allowbreak{}pre-3f-stabilization/\allowbreak{}findings\_ledger.md} (§``WP1.4c''). In summary: four archive failures, identical at \texttt{fe44de4} and at tip; \textbf{none is a numerical defect} under the register; exactly one is a supersession, I10 by \textbf{D-15}, the other three being fixture/construction or signature drift against A-21 and D-23; all of I1--I11 have live coverage under \texttt{tests/}, so no OP-19+ was opened for an unaudited identity; \textbf{OP-18} is open for the restated D-15 unit-change form of I10, which is not yet derived and must not be written as a test before it is.

\paragraph{The build.}
Stated separately from the structural check, because they are not the same evidence. \textbf{The register compiles.} On a TeX Live installation with the DejaVu family, \texttt{xelatex} over two passes returns \texttt{EXIT:0} at \texttt{dbccd3d}, producing 81 pages with no errors and no undefined or multiply-defined references. That build was not run on the analysis machine of §4.2 and is recorded here as established elsewhere, not re-established here.

A-22's toolchain note is corrected accordingly. The MiKTeX \texttt{.initial:e} failure recorded through \texttt{5a3710d} was a stale \texttt{expl3}, and the \texttt{STATUS\_DLL\_NOT\_FOUND} that followed the partial MiKTeX update at \texttt{beb0482}/\texttt{dbccd3d} is a broken local installation --- the binaries do not load their own shared libraries, so no document can be built with them. \textbf{The document is not the problem, and any future build failure on this machine is local state until shown otherwise on a working toolchain.} The structural \texttt{\textbackslash hypertarget} check and the §12.2 column-1 check remain proxies for a build and are not substitutes for one.

\paragraph{Gate profile of this entry's own commit.}
Documentation-only under A-24: every path in the diff was enumerated and none is read by any gate (\texttt{results/\allowbreak{}\_a25\_diff\_scope.txt}). The \texttt{results/} captures are inert data --- nothing imports or loads them --- and the pin baseline \texttt{refactor-patches/\allowbreak{}baselines-2026-07/\allowbreak{}pins.json} is untouched, as are \texttt{pyproject.toml}, \texttt{bstpp/}, \texttt{tests/}, \texttt{refactor-patches/\allowbreak{}pin\_check\_v2.py} and \texttt{refactor-patches/\allowbreak{}pre-3f-stabilization/\allowbreak{}covering\_array\_rows.json}. \textbf{Pins and suite are therefore recorded as not run}, with that enumeration as the reason. Gates that did run, each with \texttt{git status --porcelain} captured per the new provenance requirement: the build attempt (\texttt{results/\allowbreak{}\_a25\_build\_attempt.txt}); the structural \texttt{\textbackslash hypertarget} check, 25/25 including this subsection (\texttt{results/\allowbreak{}\_c1\_hypertarget\_check.py}, \texttt{results/\allowbreak{}\_a25\_hypertarget.txt}); the §12.2 column-1 phase-name check together with a longtable field-count check (\texttt{results/\allowbreak{}\_a25\_content\_checks.py}, \texttt{results/\allowbreak{}\_a25\_content\_checks.txt}); and the unreachable-citation sweep (\texttt{results/\allowbreak{}\_a25\_citation\_sweep.py}, \texttt{results/\allowbreak{}\_a25\_citation\_sweep.txt}).

Two of those are new here, and both found something on their first run. The citation sweep found the five \texttt{results/\allowbreak{}\_wp14c\_collect\_*} / \texttt{\_four} captures the WP1.4c ledger cites but never committed, plus eleven pin and gate captures this entry cites --- all now committed. The field-count check found one pre-existing short row in \emph{frozen} Part~I §4.3, where the ``same suite command/result as §1.2.1'' clause was left as its own one-field row in a two-column table instead of continuing the preceding cell; \LaTeX{} supplies the missing cell, so it compiles and renders with an empty right column. It is recorded as \texttt{KNOWN-PREEXISTING} in the check rather than suppressed, and left unedited: Part~I is frozen (A-1), so a correction is Part~II text or 3g editorial work, not an in-place edit.

\paragraph{Open items carried forward.}
\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item \textbf{The entry-path error asymmetry} --- \texttt{NumericalConfigError} subclasses \texttt{ValueError}, so a caller cannot distinguish a constructor rejection from a \texttt{set\_window} rejection by exception type. Carried to \textbf{A-26 / D-40}, class \textbf{SC} (it changes the accepted-configuration surface's error identity, which is public behaviour). \textbf{It must land before WP2 opens.}
\item \textbf{OP-17} --- discharged by D-40, not by a separate pass. Writing \texttt{match=} against the current constructor / \texttt{set\_window} split would freeze the asymmetry into the suite (A-24, P1).
\item \textbf{OP-18} --- unwritten, pending derivation. See the phase0 triage paragraph above.
\end{itemize}


\hypertarget{a-26}{%
\subsection{A-26 --- Error-identity unification: one invariant, one identity, one message}\label{a-26}}

\textbf{Opened} 2026-08-04. \textbf{Class} \textbf{SC} --- a declared change to observable behaviour; not behaviour-preserving, and not to be described as such. \textbf{Establishes} D-40. \textbf{Closes} OP-17. \textbf{Opens} OP-19. \textbf{Amends} A-24 (P1 grade; OP-17 row); A-25 (probe provenance). \textbf{Rebaseline record} \texttt{refactor-patches/phase3f/rebaseline\_record.md}. \textbf{Commit} \texttt{WP1.4e-1}.

\paragraph{The asymmetry, and where it came from.}
The same violation --- a \texttt{PolygonMassTable} whose panel is too coarse for the model's \texttt{min\_sigma} --- raised two different errors depending on how it arrived. The constructor raised \texttt{NumericalConfigError} because \texttt{NumericalConfig.create} ran first; \texttt{set\_window(mass\_table=)} raised a bare \texttt{ValueError} because WP1.4b had installed a call-site guard whose only purpose was to make the legacy message win. That guard's own comment recorded the intention (``set\_window error-precedence CF''). \textbf{Nothing tested the precedence claim.} Lane~B has an explicit \texttt{entry\_path} axis and the divergence was invisible to it, because \texttt{NumericalConfigError} subclasses \texttt{ValueError} and every matrix rejection was pinned with a bare \texttt{pytest.raises(ValueError)} --- the unasserted assertion family (OP-17) and the defect that slipped past it are the same gap, which is the concrete instance of D-39's claim.

\paragraph{What the enumeration found that the brief did not.}
Recorded because it changed the work. \textbf{Three raise sites, not two}, reached by \textbf{four entry paths} producing \textbf{two types and three messages}: \texttt{NumericalConfig.\_\_post\_init\_\_}; \texttt{assert\_polygon\_mass\_table\_budget} (install \emph{and} held-out scoring, which is a third entry path nobody had listed); and \texttt{prepare\_polygon\_mass\_table}'s own pre-build guard, with a third message again. Measured by execution at \texttt{e9c605a}, not inferred from call order: \texttt{results/\allowbreak{}\_a26\_probe\_\allowbreak{}entry\_path\_split.txt}.

\paragraph{The chosen identity, and the single source.}
\texttt{NumericalConfigError}. D-35 makes the config the single source; the config already owns \texttt{max\_panel\_to\_min\_sigma\_ratio}; and it subclasses \texttt{ValueError}, so no caller catching \texttt{ValueError} is affected --- only the concrete type and the message change. \textbf{The single-source function is \texttt{panel\_ratio\_invariant\_clause} in \texttt{bstpp/config.py}}, with \texttt{raise\_panel\_ratio\_violation} as the one raiser. It lives in \texttt{config.py} because the config owns the ceiling; \texttt{polygon\_mass.py} reaches it by deferred import, since \texttt{config} imports \texttt{polygon\_mass} for its constants and the reverse edge cannot exist at module scope. The alternative --- moving the error type into a new leaf module --- was rejected as more churn on a public import path for no gain.

\paragraph{Remediation clauses are permitted; restating the invariant is not.}
Flattening all three sites to one string would have destroyed genuinely useful advice: refusing to \emph{build} a table and refusing to \emph{install} one against a given model are different situations. D-40 therefore fixes the type and the canonical clause and allows a site to append its own remediation after it. That is what tests match on, and it is what makes the obligation enforceable rather than a matter of taste.

\paragraph{Ordering stopped mattering --- verified, not reasoned about.}
Re-running the same probe after the change: one type on all four entry paths; constructor and \texttt{set\_window} byte-identical end to end; held-out and builder sharing that clause verbatim and differing only after it. The WP1.4b guard was removed, and with it the last reason the two paths could disagree.

\paragraph{Raised messages are ASCII (D-40 corollary).}
Three raised messages contained non-ASCII characters: \texttt{config.py}'s sigma-bound clause (σ) and two \texttt{preparation.py} \texttt{standardize\_cov} messages (∩). This is not cosmetic. Printing such a message to a legacy-codepage console raises \texttt{UnicodeEncodeError} \emph{while the traceback carrying it is being rendered} --- an error path that fails while failing. It was observed twice during this work: once in the Step~1 probe, and once in the ASCII sweep's own reporter, which crashed on the first string it found. The sweep (\texttt{results/\allowbreak{}\_a26\_ascii\_sweep.py}) parses every module under \texttt{bstpp/} and reports non-ASCII in \texttt{raise} arguments; it now passes, and it is the enforcement that ships with the rule.

\paragraph{Test edits, and the sign-off.}
Eleven edits across four files, enumerated and signed off by Terhi \emph{before} any test was touched, on the Step~1 report. The list and its rationale are in the rebaseline record. \textbf{No assertion was weakened.} Two additions generalize OP-17 as agreed: after this commit no test in the affected set uses a bare \texttt{pytest.raises} for any of these invariants.

The point that justifies the SC classification: \textbf{all five previously-existing tests stayed green across the production change}, because their substring alternations (\texttt{"panel|min\_sigma|ratio|panel\_h\_m"} and similar) could not distinguish the two identities. A test suite that cannot see the defect it nominally covers is the thing being repaired, not the evidence that nothing needed repairing.

\paragraph{RED before GREEN.}
\texttt{results/\allowbreak{}\_a26\_red\_\allowbreak{}entry\_path\_row.txt}. The new Lane~B row was run with \texttt{main.py} and \texttt{polygon\_mass.py} reverted to \texttt{e9c605a} and failed on the restored guard's \texttt{ValueError} --- it discriminates the WP1.4b defect specifically, not merely the absence of the fix.

\paragraph{Lane~B row.}
\texttt{test\_lane\_b\_panel\_ratio\_error\_identity\_is\_entry\_path\_invariant}, on the \texttt{entry\_path} axis. It compares the two entry paths \emph{to each other} rather than each to a literal, so a future divergence fails it whatever the wording; it then pins the shared text to the single-sourced clause so agreement cannot be coincidental, asserts ASCII, and re-checks P4 rollback. This is the enforcement that would have caught the defect at the moment WP1.4b created it, and it is what stops WP2--WP5 reintroducing the split once four more configs multiply the entry paths.

\paragraph{Gates.}
\texttt{pytest tests/} \textbf{559 passed, 2 skipped, 1 xfailed}, \texttt{EXIT:0} (\texttt{results/\allowbreak{}\_a26\_1\_suite.txt}); collection 560 $\rightarrow$ 562, the two tests added here. Lane~B green including the new row. \texttt{ruff} on touch \texttt{EXIT:0}. \texttt{jax\_enable\_x64} unchanged. Every capture carries \texttt{git status --porcelain}, and the probes additionally carry \texttt{bstpp.\_\_file\_\_}.

\paragraph{Pins as the SC rebaseline record.}
\texttt{PIN\_DIFFS 0 MATCH} on all four configurations (\texttt{results/\allowbreak{}\_a26\_1\_pins.txt}). Error paths are not traced, so a match was expected --- and expectation is not measurement. The run is the record: this SC changed no traced numerical result. A diff would have meant the change reached a traced path, which is a different commit than this one.

\paragraph{Carried forward.}
\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item \textbf{OP-19}, opened here. Held-out scoring's error \emph{identity} is unified, but it still validates against \emph{module default} budget policy rather than the model's \texttt{NumericalConfig}. Passing \texttt{numerical\_config} there would change which \texttt{panel\_h\_m} and \texttt{gl\_order} govern held-out validation for anyone holding a non-default table --- a separate observable change with its own pin question, deliberately not folded in. Recorded as an instance of the dual-source debt already accepted through OP-8; routed to \textbf{WP5} with the \texttt{ExcitationSupport} seam.
\item \textbf{WP1.4e-2}, the second SC of this pair. The five σ/mode invariants --- σ-bound coherence, \texttt{min\_sigma} positivity, polygon-requires-\texttt{min\_sigma}, rectangle both-or-neither, and support-mode validity --- have the same split, and worse: \texttt{resolve\_sigma\_bounds} runs before \texttt{NumericalConfig.create} on \emph{both} entry paths, so the bare \texttt{ValueError} always wins and \textbf{the config's own branches are unreachable through any public path}. D-35's single-source claim is presently false for five invariants, and the object designated as the source contains dead validation. \texttt{excitation\_support.\_validate\_sigma\_pair} is additionally a duplicate implementation of \texttt{NumericalConfig.\_validate\_sigma\_pair} --- the same class as G1's membership-predicate double spelling. It is deleted and delegated there. \textbf{Before WP2.}
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{a-27}{%
\subsection{A-27 --- The five σ/mode invariants: one identity, one clause, owners by quantity}\label{a-27}}

\textbf{Opened} 2026-08-05. \textbf{Class} \textbf{SC} --- a declared change to observable behaviour; not behaviour-preserving. \textbf{Refines} D-40 (\texttt{\textbackslash supsd} clause; \emph{no new decision number}). \textbf{Opens} OP-20. \textbf{Amends} A-23 (the \texttt{NumericalConfig} field docstring was false); A-25 (content-check gate gains decision-row monotonicity). \textbf{Rebaseline record} \texttt{refactor-patches/phase3f/rebaseline\_record.md}. \textbf{Commit} \texttt{WP1.4e-2}.

\amdnew{A-30} \textbf{Labels renumbered by A-30.} The invariants this subsection calls \texttt{CI-1}--\texttt{CI-6} were originally written \supsd{A-30}{\texttt{I1}--\texttt{I6}}. \textbf{The complete mapping is} \texttt{I1}$\rightarrow$\texttt{CI-1} (rectangle both-or-neither), \texttt{I2}$\rightarrow$\texttt{CI-2} (polygon requires \texttt{min\_sigma}), \texttt{I3}$\rightarrow$\texttt{CI-3} (\texttt{min\_sigma} finite and positive), \texttt{I4}$\rightarrow$\texttt{CI-4} (\texttt{min\_sigma < max\_sigma}), \texttt{I5}$\rightarrow$\texttt{CI-5} (support-mode validity), \texttt{I6}$\rightarrow$\texttt{CI-6} (builder requires \texttt{max\_sigma}) --- so an external citation of the old label resolves here rather than dangling. The old numbering collided with the Phase-3 model identities \texttt{I1}--\texttt{I12}, a different and entrenched sequence. Substituted in place rather than marked occurrence by occurrence: there were 44 across A-27 and A-28, and 44 strikethroughs would have destroyed the readability of the argument they sit inside. Permitted under D-41, established at A-31 in the commit immediately following this one and citing A-30 as its first instance: no assertion changes, and this note carries the full mapping. \textbf{This note is the supersession record for all 44.}

\paragraph{The brief's premise was wrong, and execution is what established that.}
This work opened on the reading that \texttt{NumericalConfig.min\_sigma} / \texttt{max\_sigma} hold the \emph{user-supplied} bounds while \texttt{resolve\_sigma\_bounds} holds the \emph{resolved} ones --- two quantities, hence a design conflict about which object owns the invariants. Recording the arguments at every \texttt{NumericalConfig.create} call on every accepting public path settled it: \texttt{main.py:2069} and \texttt{main.py:2307} pass the resolver's output, not the user's arguments. With a projected CRS and \texttt{max\_sigma} omitted, the object is constructed with \texttt{max\_sigma=5000.0} --- never \texttt{None}. \textbf{The config was already the resolved-bound validator; only its docstring said otherwise.} That docstring is corrected here, and the correction is recorded rather than made silently: a false docstring on a frozen configuration object generated a design question that cost a round trip before any code was touched. It is a small instance of the register/gate gap D-39 names --- a document asserting something about the code that the code does not do, with nothing that could fail.

\paragraph{What the enumeration found that the brief did not.}
\textbf{Six raise sites, not two.} \texttt{excitation\_support.\_validate\_sigma\_pair}; \texttt{NumericalConfig.\_validate\_sigma\_pair}; the config's polygon \texttt{max\_sigma is None} branch; \texttt{polygon\_mass.assert\_polygon\_mass\_table\_budget}; \texttt{prepare\_polygon\_mass\_table}; and \texttt{build\_quad\_table}, whose check is a verbatim copy of the first with the parameters renamed. Two error types, five distinct \texttt{min\_sigma}-positivity messages and three distinct ordering messages. Support-mode validity had three implementations of its own (\texttt{resolve\_excitation\_support\_mode}, \texttt{build\_excitation\_support}, the config) plus a fourth \emph{behaviour}: \texttt{resolve\_sigma\_bounds} did not validate \texttt{mode} at all --- its \texttt{else} branch \emph{was} the polygon branch, so \texttt{mode='triangle'} returned a resolved pair rather than raising. And \texttt{prepare\_polygon\_mass\_table(min\_sigma=None)} died inside \texttt{float(None)} with a bare \texttt{TypeError}, an unnamed error failing Lane~B admissibility. Enumeration artifacts: \texttt{refactor-patches/\allowbreak{}pre-3f-stabilization/\allowbreak{}probe\_wp14e2\_*.log}.

\paragraph{Reachability, measured rather than argued.}
A line tracer over 28 public entry-path calls at \texttt{f70ac7d} reported \textbf{all six config branches NEVER EXECUTED}. \texttt{resolve\_sigma\_bounds} runs before \texttt{NumericalConfig.create} on every path (\texttt{main.py:2048} before \texttt{2065}; \texttt{2290} before \texttt{2303}), and \texttt{mode} always arrives from \texttt{resolve\_excitation\_support\_mode} or the literal \texttt{"rectangle"}, so the config's mode branch could not fire either. D-35's single-source claim was false for five invariants, and the object designated as the source contained only dead validation. After the change, \texttt{config.validate\_sigma\_pair} is REACHED on public paths --- the same tracer, the same battery.

\paragraph{The design: owners by quantity, which is the D-40 refinement.}
Three families, not two, and the split is structural rather than incidental. \textbf{CI-1 (rectangle both-or-neither) and CI-2 (polygon requires \texttt{min\_sigma}) are argument invariants}: they test which argument was omitted, and defaulting destroys that information --- resolved rectangle bounds are both-\texttt{None} or both-\texttt{float} by construction, and a resolved polygon \texttt{min\_sigma} is never \texttt{None}. A resolved-bound validator \emph{cannot express them}. \textbf{CI-3 (\texttt{min\_sigma} positivity) and CI-4 (\texttt{min\_sigma < max\_sigma}) are resolved-bound invariants}; CI-4 is only meaningful after defaulting, since in polygon mode with \texttt{max\_sigma} omitted there is no user-supplied pair to compare. \textbf{CI-5 (support-mode validity) is a mode invariant}, upstream of both. This is why ``delegate to the config after resolution'' --- the reading D-40 invited --- was rejected: it would have silently dropped CI-1 and CI-2, a \textbf{reject$\rightarrow$accept} change on two of the five. The single-source functions are \texttt{rectangle\_bounds\_invariant\_clause}, \texttt{polygon\_min\_sigma\_invariant\_clause}, \texttt{min\_sigma\_positive\_invariant\_clause}, \texttt{sigma\_order\_invariant\_clause} and \texttt{support\_mode\_invariant\_clause} in \texttt{bstpp/config.py}, with matching raisers and one shared predicate \texttt{validate\_sigma\_pair}. \texttt{polygon\_mass} reaches them by deferred import, the arrangement A-26 established.

\paragraph{Declared behavioural changes, named individually.}
\begin{enumerate}
\tightlist
\item \textbf{Error type on all five invariants}: bare \texttt{ValueError} $\rightarrow$ \texttt{NumericalConfigError}. It subclasses \texttt{ValueError}, so no caller catching \texttt{ValueError} is affected. Message text changes for CI-1, CI-2, CI-4 and CI-5; CI-3's two implementations were already byte-identical and the canonical clause keeps that text.
\item \textbf{\texttt{prepare\_polygon\_mass\_table(min\_sigma=None)}}: \texttt{TypeError} $\rightarrow$ \texttt{NumericalConfigError}. \textbf{This is not a subclass relation} --- a caller catching \texttt{TypeError} \emph{is} affected. Declared separately for that reason.
\item \textbf{\texttt{resolve\_sigma\_bounds(mode=<invalid>)}}: silent acceptance $\rightarrow$ \texttt{NumericalConfigError}. Unreachable through any model path, which gates on \texttt{resolve\_excitation\_support\_mode} first; it affects direct callers of the function.
\end{enumerate}
\textbf{No accept$\rightarrow$reject change reaches any model constructor.} The family that would have produced nine of them is excluded as OP-20.

\paragraph{Argument-type discipline excluded (OP-20).}
\texttt{resolve\_sigma\_bounds} coerces with \texttt{float()}; the config rejects with \texttt{\_require\_real}. Measured at \texttt{f70ac7d}, \texttt{float()} accepts \texttt{str}, \texttt{bool}, \texttt{np.float32}, \texttt{np.int64}, 0-d \texttt{ndarray}, \texttt{Decimal} and any object with \texttt{\_\_float\_\_}; \texttt{min\_sigma='5'} and \texttt{min\_sigma=True} construct a model today on both public constructors. Routing the resolver through \texttt{\_require\_real} would flip nine input classes accept$\rightarrow$reject. That is A-23's argument-type invariant, not one of these five, and folding it into an error-identity commit would make an API change wear an SC commit's clothes. Recorded as OP-20 and routed to \textbf{WP2}, where \texttt{ModelConfig} and \texttt{PriorConfig} raise the identical question --- answering it once for all five factories is worth more than answering it here.

\paragraph{One asymmetry is frozen, deliberately, and pinned.}
Polygon mode with \texttt{max\_sigma} omitted and \emph{no} CRS: the resolver rejects (it must default from \texttt{DEFAULT\_MAX\_SIGMA\_KM}, which needs a projected CRS), while \texttt{NumericalConfig} accepts, because it never sees that situation --- it is handed an already-resolved pair. A-27 does not move this. It is pinned from both sides by \texttt{test\_lane\_b\_polygon\_default\_max\_sigma\_without\_crs\_is\_rejected}, because the obvious future refactor --- making the config the front gate for user-supplied bounds --- converts the rejection into a silent accept, and a silent accept there would pass every other gate in the suite.

\paragraph{Site 4 is unreachable through every public model path, and is labelled so.}
\texttt{assert\_polygon\_mass\_table\_budget}'s \texttt{min\_sigma} check cannot fire from a model: \texttt{resolve\_sigma\_bounds} rejects a non-positive bound first, and \texttt{validate\_polygon\_mass\_table} additionally requires \texttt{table.sigma\_min == sigma\_min}, which \texttt{build\_quad\_table} now refuses to build. It is reachable only by calling that public module function directly, which is what the Lane~B CI-3 row does. Unifying a message on a path nothing exercises is acceptable; asserting it was exercised would not be.

\paragraph{Test edits, and the sign-off.}
Enumerated on the Step~1 report and signed off by Terhi \emph{before} any test was touched: the five alternation rows in \texttt{tests/test\_numerical\_config.py}, the \texttt{ValueError}/\texttt{"min\_sigma"} row in \texttt{tests/test\_phase3d\_excitation\_support.py}, and whatever sites 4--6 required. \textbf{No assertion was weakened.} Every one is now equality against a canonical clause. The \texttt{test\_phase3d} row is the sharpest illustration: \texttt{pytest.raises(ValueError, match="min\_sigma")} was green against \emph{nothing} --- \texttt{NumericalConfigError} is a \texttt{ValueError} and \texttt{min\_sigma} appears in every candidate message, so it passed identically on both sides of the change. It now pins the type exactly and the clause by equality.

\paragraph{RED before GREEN.}
\texttt{results/\allowbreak{}\_a27\_red\_\allowbreak{}sigma\_mode\_rows.txt}: \textbf{12 failed, 7 passed} with \texttt{excitation\_support.py} and \texttt{polygon\_mass.py} reverted to \texttt{f70ac7d} and \texttt{config.py} keeping only the new clause functions so the tests could import them. All five owner-invariance rows, both declared behavioural-change rows and the \texttt{test\_phase3d} row are among the failures. The 7 that passed are recorded as non-discriminating by construction and not claimed otherwise: the frozen-asymmetry pin (which must pass both sides --- that is what freezing means), and the CI-3/CI-4 equality rows whose canonical clause deliberately kept the text the config already used.

\paragraph{Lane~B rows.}
\texttt{test\_lane\_b\_sigma\_mode\_error\_identity\_is\_owner\_invariant}, parametrized over the five invariants. Each row collects \emph{every} owner of its invariant --- model constructor, resolver, config, builder, \texttt{build\_quad\_table}, \texttt{assert\_polygon\_mass\_table\_budget} as applicable --- and compares them \emph{to each other} before pinning the shared text to the single-sourced clause, so a future divergence fails whatever the wording; it then requires at least one owner to render the clause exactly, so agreement cannot be coincidental, and asserts ASCII. Three further rows cover the frozen asymmetry and the two declared behavioural changes.

\paragraph{Gates.}
\texttt{pytest tests/} \textbf{572 passed, 2 skipped, 1 xfailed}, \texttt{EXIT:0} (\texttt{results/\allowbreak{}\_a27\_suite.txt}); collection 562 $\rightarrow$ 575, the thirteen tests added here. The run took 26m22s against the usual $\sim$1\,min: the analysis machine was under heavy memory pressure throughout this session (1.05\,GB free of 15.4\,GB), which also produced one hard \texttt{ImportError: DLL load failed \ldots{} paging file is too small} in an ad-hoc probe and a sevenfold spread across identical Lane~B selections (21\,s vs 180\,s). Environmental, not attributable to this change, and recorded so the timing in this record is not later read as a regression. \texttt{ruff} on touch \texttt{EXIT:1}: a single pre-existing \texttt{F401} (\texttt{jax.numpy} unused in \texttt{tests/test\_phase3d\_excitation\_support.py}), demonstrated pre-existing by running \texttt{ruff} against \texttt{git show HEAD:} of that file. Not fixed --- it would be a test edit outside the sign-off. ASCII sweep \textbf{PASS}. Content checks including the new monotonicity check \textbf{PASS}. \texttt{\textbackslash hypertarget} \textbf{27/27}. Unreachable-citation sweep \textbf{PASS}. Every capture carries \texttt{git status --porcelain}; every probe carries \texttt{bstpp.\_\_file\_\_}.

\paragraph{Pins as the SC rebaseline record.}
\textbf{\texttt{PIN\_DIFFS 0 MATCH}} on all four configurations (\texttt{results/\allowbreak{}\_a27\_pins.txt}). This is the measurement the Step~2 scope fence demanded: e-2 was permitted to change who raises and with what identity, and forbidden to change any value \texttt{resolve\_sigma\_bounds} returns, because the resolved \texttt{lo}/\texttt{hi} feed \texttt{truncate\_sigmax\_2\_prior} at \texttt{main.py:2057} and a moved bound moves the prior, the posterior and the pins. A match was expected --- error paths are not traced and no return path was touched --- and expectation is not measurement. A diff here would have meant the fence was breached.

\paragraph{Register gate extended.}
A Part~I decision-row monotonicity check is added to \texttt{results/\allowbreak{}\_a25\_content\_checks.py} as check~3. D-40 landed out of numeric order and was caught by reading; a misordered row is structurally perfect, so neither the \texttt{\textbackslash hypertarget} check nor the field-count check can see it, and neither can see a duplicate or a gap. It was \emph{demonstrated to discriminate} rather than merely observed to pass: a mutated copy with the D-40 row moved before D-39 exits 1 with the right message. The real file reports 40 rows, D-1 to D-40, strictly increasing, no duplicates, no gaps.

\paragraph{Carried forward.}
\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item \textbf{OP-20}, opened here; \textbf{WP2}.
\item \supsd{A-29}{\textbf{The ASCII sweep cannot see single-sourced clauses.} \texttt{results/\allowbreak{}\_a26\_ascii\_sweep.py} parses string literals that are arguments to \texttt{raise}. A clause returned from \texttt{*\_invariant\_clause} and raised via \texttt{raise NumericalConfigError(msg)} presents no literal to find, so the sweep passes over exactly the text D-40 cares most about. It has been true since A-26 created the pattern and is stated here because it is now the dominant case. The runtime \texttt{.encode("ascii")} assertions in the Lane~B rows are what actually enforce the corollary for these five. Extending the sweep to evaluate the clause functions is a small change and is \emph{not} made here.} \amdnew{A-29} Discharged by A-29, which extends the sweep to evaluate the clause functions \emph{and} to scan the remediation literals at \texttt{raise\_*()} call sites --- a second blind spot the carried-forward note did not identify.
\item \supsd{A-28}{\textbf{\texttt{prepare\_polygon\_mass\_table(max\_sigma=None)}} still dies in \texttt{float(None)} with a bare \texttt{TypeError} --- the sibling of the \texttt{min\_sigma} defect fixed here. It was outside the signed-off scope and is left rather than folded in silently.} \amdnew{A-28} Discharged by A-28, which routes it to a \emph{new} invariant CI-6 rather than to CI-2 --- see below for why the sibling is not a family member.
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{a-28}{%
\subsection{A-28 --- \texttt{max\_sigma} at the mass-table builder: a sixth invariant, not a sixth owner of the second}\label{a-28}}

\textbf{Opened} 2026-08-05. \textbf{Class} \textbf{CF} --- a declared change to observable behaviour on error paths only; not behaviour-preserving. \textbf{Refines} D-40 (\texttt{\textbackslash supsd} clause; \emph{no new decision number}), adding \textbf{CI-6} to the σ/mode family note. \textbf{Discharges} A-27's carried-forward \texttt{max\_sigma} item. \textbf{Opens} OP-21. \textbf{Rebaseline record} \texttt{refactor-patches/phase3f/rebaseline\_record\_a28.md}. \textbf{Commit} \texttt{WP1.4f}.

\amdnew{A-30} \textbf{Labels renumbered by A-30.} The invariants this subsection calls \texttt{CI-1}--\texttt{CI-6} were originally written \supsd{A-30}{\texttt{I1}--\texttt{I6}}. \textbf{The complete mapping is} \texttt{I1}$\rightarrow$\texttt{CI-1} (rectangle both-or-neither), \texttt{I2}$\rightarrow$\texttt{CI-2} (polygon requires \texttt{min\_sigma}), \texttt{I3}$\rightarrow$\texttt{CI-3} (\texttt{min\_sigma} finite and positive), \texttt{I4}$\rightarrow$\texttt{CI-4} (\texttt{min\_sigma < max\_sigma}), \texttt{I5}$\rightarrow$\texttt{CI-5} (support-mode validity), \texttt{I6}$\rightarrow$\texttt{CI-6} (builder requires \texttt{max\_sigma}) --- so an external citation of the old label resolves here rather than dangling. The old numbering collided with the Phase-3 model identities \texttt{I1}--\texttt{I12}, a different and entrenched sequence. Substituted in place rather than marked occurrence by occurrence: there were 44 across A-27 and A-28, and 44 strikethroughs would have destroyed the readability of the argument they sit inside. Permitted under D-41, established at A-31 in the commit immediately following this one and citing A-30 as its first instance: no assertion changes, and this note carries the full mapping. \textbf{This note is the supersession record for all 44.}

\paragraph{The question the brief posed, and the answer.}
A-27 left \texttt{prepare\_polygon\_mass\_table(max\_sigma=None)} dying in \texttt{float(None)}, one line below the \texttt{min\_sigma} twin it had just fixed, and the brief asked whether the sibling belongs to \textbf{CI-2} (polygon requires \texttt{min\_sigma}) or is a distinct invariant. \textbf{It is distinct}, and the test that settles it is A-27's own: \emph{owners of one invariant validate the same quantity}. Three reasons, in increasing order of force.

\begin{enumerate}
\tightlist
\item \textbf{Different quantity.} CI-2's is \texttt{min\_sigma}; this one's is \texttt{max\_sigma}. Under the D-40 refinement that alone makes them different invariants.
\item \textbf{Different owner set.} CI-2 has four owners --- constructor, \texttt{resolve\_sigma\_bounds}, \texttt{NumericalConfig.create}, and the public builder. CI-6's owners are the \emph{two builders only}.
\item \textbf{Different truth value, which is decisive.} CI-2 holds package-wide: nothing anywhere defaults \texttt{min\_sigma}. ``Polygon requires \texttt{max\_sigma}'' is \textbf{false} at the model boundary --- \texttt{resolve\_sigma\_bounds} defaults an omitted polygon \texttt{max\_sigma} to \texttt{DEFAULT\_MAX\_SIGMA\_KM} through the projected CRS, and \texttt{NumericalConfig} accepts \texttt{max\_sigma=None} outright (the asymmetry A-27 froze). \textbf{Rendering CI-2's clause here would raise a message asserting a package-wide requirement that does not exist.} A borrowed clause would not merely be untidy; it would be untrue.
\end{enumerate}

The requirement is the \emph{builder's} alone because \texttt{max\_sigma} there is not a prior bound but the \textbf{top knot} of the table's log-sigma grid (\texttt{log\_knots(sigma\_min, sigma\_max)}), past which evaluation is prohibited by \texttt{validate\_sigma\_in\_range}. Nor can the builder borrow the resolver's default: that default requires a projected CRS, and \texttt{crs} is optional at the builder --- supplying one would be a reject$\rightarrow$accept widening, not the requested fix. New single source \texttt{builder\_max\_sigma\_invariant\_clause} / \texttt{raise\_builder\_max\_sigma\_violation} in \texttt{bstpp/config.py}, reached by the deferred import A-26 established.

\paragraph{A label collision, recorded rather than deepened.}
Two \texttt{I}-numberings are now in use and they overlap. The \texttt{CI-1}--\texttt{I12} of the Phase-3 \emph{model identities} (mass atoms, pair window, derived seasonal coordinate, \ldots{}) are unrelated to A-27's \texttt{CI-1}--\texttt{CI-5} and this amendment's \texttt{CI-6}, which are the \emph{σ/mode invariants} under D-40. So identity \texttt{CI-6} (``derived seasonal coordinate'') and σ-\texttt{CI-6} (``builder requires \texttt{max\_sigma}'') are different things wearing the same label, as are \texttt{CI-1}, \texttt{CI-3}, \texttt{CI-4} and \texttt{CI-5}. The collision was created at A-27 and is stated here rather than extended in silence. \textbf{The two sides are not equally expensive to renumber}: the model identities are entrenched (cited in the guide, the phase0 archive, \texttt{test\_polygon\_i11\_conservation.py} and several amendments), while the σ labels are days old and live in A-27, the matrix and a handful of test names. Renumbering is therefore a real option on the σ side only, and it is deferred to its own commit rather than folded into a CF change --- see A-29.

\paragraph{Enumeration found three \texttt{TypeError} sites, not one.}
Measured by execution at \texttt{426d60a} (\texttt{refactor-patches/\allowbreak{}pre-3f-stabilization/\allowbreak{}probe\_wp14f\_max\_sigma\_sites\_BEFORE.log}), not inferred from call order. Besides the named site, \texttt{build\_quad\_table} dies identically at \texttt{polygon\_mass.py:935}, where \texttt{validate\_sigma\_pair(float(sigma\_min), float(sigma\_max))} coerces \emph{both} arguments in front of a validator that deliberately does not coerce (OP-20) --- so \texttt{sigma\_min=None} there was an unnamed \texttt{TypeError} too, at a site A-27 covered only at the public builder. Both are fixed: \texttt{max\_sigma} to the new CI-6 clause, \texttt{min\_sigma} to the existing CI-2 clause. This is not scope creep but the completion of one function's argument-shape handling: leaving \texttt{build\_quad\_table(None, 40.0)} unnamed one line from this commit's own guard would recreate exactly the situation the commit exists to close.

\paragraph{Declared behavioural changes, named individually.}
\begin{enumerate}
\tightlist
\item \texttt{prepare\_polygon\_mass\_table(max\_sigma=None)}: \texttt{TypeError} $\rightarrow$ \texttt{NumericalConfigError}. \textbf{Not a subclass relation --- a caller catching \texttt{TypeError} IS affected.} Same treatment A-27 gave the twin.
\item \texttt{build\_quad\_table(sigma\_max=None)}: same change, same clause, same non-subclass warning.
\item \texttt{build\_quad\_table(sigma\_min=None)}: \texttt{TypeError} $\rightarrow$ \texttt{NumericalConfigError}, rendering CI-2.
\end{enumerate}
\textbf{No accept$\rightarrow$reject change reaches any model constructor}, and none of the three is reachable from one: every model path resolves bounds before building, and \texttt{build\_quad\_table} is not called from \texttt{main.py} at all.

\paragraph{Precedence preserved deliberately, and pinned.}
At the pre-change tip \texttt{max\_sigma=None} \emph{lost} to every other check in \texttt{prepare\_polygon\_mass\_table}: \texttt{(None, None)} reported CI-2, \texttt{(0.0, None)} reported CI-3, a coarse panel reported the panel ratio. The CI-6 guard is therefore placed \textbf{last}, immediately before the delegation. Guarding earlier would have changed which error a doubly-invalid call reports --- an \emph{undeclared} behaviour change riding along with the declared ones. \texttt{test\_lane\_b\_builder\_max\_sigma\_guard\_preserves\_error\_precedence} pins all three combinations. That row \textbf{passes on both sides of the change and is recorded as non-discriminating by construction}, not claimed as evidence: preserving those three messages is precisely its purpose.

\paragraph{OP-21, opened and deliberately not taken.}
The third enumerated site, \texttt{validate\_polygon\_mass\_table(sigma\_max=None)} at \texttt{polygon\_mass.py:810}, is left raising an unnamed \texttt{TypeError} and is \emph{labelled, not assumed}, in A-27's manner. It is not a builder: it compares a \emph{built} table's recorded range against the \emph{model's resolved} bound, so its quantity is the model-side bound and the CI-6 clause would misdescribe it. It is unreachable from every model path --- \texttt{excitation\_support.py:404} asserts \texttt{lo is not None and hi is not None} before the call --- and reachable only by direct call. Deciding what it should raise means deciding whether a \texttt{NumericalConfig} holding \texttt{max\_sigma=None} may reach install validation at all, which is \textbf{the frozen asymmetry A-27 explicitly declined to move}. Unfreezing it inside a CF commit would be exactly the sleight A-27 refused with OP-20. Routed to \textbf{WP5} with OP-19.

\paragraph{Tests.}
Four Lane~B rows, no existing test edited and no assertion weakened. The owner-agreement row compares the two builders \emph{to each other} before pinning the shared text to the single-sourced clause, asserts \texttt{not isinstance(exc, TypeError)} for the declared type change, requires each site's remediation to \textbf{name the function the caller actually called} (otherwise a user of the public builder is advised about an internal one), and asserts the CI-6 clause is neither equal to nor contained in CI-2's --- so a future refactor that folds the two together fails here. A second row pins the \emph{asymmetry itself}, both halves, because the family decision rests on the contrast rather than on either half alone.

\paragraph{RED before GREEN.}
\texttt{results/\allowbreak{}\_a28\_red\_builder\_max\_sigma\_rows.txt}: \textbf{3 failed, 1 passed} with \texttt{polygon\_mass.py} alone reverted to \texttt{426d60a}. The three fail on the defect itself --- \texttt{TypeError: float() argument \ldots{} not 'NoneType'} at \texttt{polygon\_mass.py:1092} and \texttt{:935} --- not on an import error; \texttt{config.py} was kept at candidate for the RED run precisely so the new clause API could not mask the measurement. The single pass is the precedence row, non-discriminating by construction as recorded above.

\paragraph{Gates.}
\texttt{pytest tests/} \textbf{576 passed, 2 skipped, 1 xfailed}, \texttt{EXIT:0} (\texttt{results/\allowbreak{}\_a28\_suite.txt}); collection 575 $\rightarrow$ 579, the four tests added here. The run took 27m05s against the usual $\sim$1\,min, matching the 26m22s A-27 recorded on this machine in the same session --- environmental memory pressure, not attributable to this change, and recorded so the timing is not later read as a regression. \texttt{ruff} on touch \texttt{EXIT:0} (\texttt{results/\allowbreak{}\_a28\_ruff.txt}); A-27's pre-existing \texttt{F401} is in \texttt{tests/test\_phase3d\_excitation\_support.py}, which this commit does not touch. ASCII sweep \textbf{PASS}, 17 modules. Content checks including decision-row monotonicity \textbf{PASS}: 40 rows, D-1 to D-40, no new decision number allocated. \texttt{\textbackslash hypertarget} \textbf{28/28}. Unreachable-citation sweep \textbf{PASS}. Every capture carries \texttt{git status --porcelain}; every probe carries \texttt{bstpp.\_\_file\_\_}.

\paragraph{Pins as the CF rebaseline record.}
\textbf{\texttt{PIN\_DIFFS 0 MATCH}} on all four configurations (\texttt{results/\allowbreak{}\_a28\_pins.txt}). The change touches only error paths --- two guards that raise before any quadrature runs --- so a match was \emph{expected}; expectation is not measurement, and this run is the measurement. A diff would have meant a guard had been placed where it can fire on an \emph{accepting} path.

\paragraph{Provenance.}
The enumeration probe's first run loaded a \textbf{stale \texttt{bstpp} from \texttt{site-packages}} and died on \texttt{bstpp.config}, the same failure class A-26 recorded: a script under \texttt{refactor-patches/} does not put the repo root on \texttt{sys.path}. The probe now inserts the repo root and \textbf{asserts \texttt{bstpp.\_\_file\_\_} lies under it}, so the capture cannot silently describe a different object.

\hypertarget{a-29}{%
\subsection{A-29 --- The ASCII sweep evaluates the clauses, and states its own coverage}\label{a-29}}

\textbf{Opened} 2026-08-05. \textbf{Class} \textbf{IV} --- verification infrastructure; no production file is touched, so no observable behaviour changes. \textbf{Discharges} A-27's carried-forward ASCII-sweep item. \textbf{Opens} OP-22. \textbf{Amends} A-26 (the sweep it created). \textbf{Commit} \texttt{WP1.4g}.

\paragraph{A figure that does not reconcile, corrected before it was built on.}
The brief scoping this work stated that the sweep ``inspects 38\% of sites'': 69 raise sites with a literal first argument against 114 without, at \texttt{426d60a}. \textbf{That split is reproducible and the conclusion drawn from it is not.} 69/183 counts \emph{first argument is a bare \texttt{str} Constant}, which is not what the sweep did --- \texttt{\_string\_parts} walks the \emph{whole} \texttt{exc} expression, so it also saw the constant chunks of every f-string. Measured both ways at \texttt{426d60a}: definition~A gives 69/183 (38\%), definition~B --- what the code actually walked --- gives \textbf{176/183 (96\%)}, blind on \textbf{seven}. Both figures are now reproduced on demand by \texttt{-{}-census}, so the discrepancy cannot recur silently.

The substantive claim survives the correction and is sharper without the number. The gap was never 114 unseen literals; it is that \textbf{clause text and remediation text are evaluated nowhere}, and the seven blind sites are not incidental --- they are \emph{precisely} the D-40 raisers (\texttt{raise NumericalConfigError(msg)} inside each \texttt{raise\_*\_violation}), plus one runtime-assembled \texttt{DataContractError}. The sweep was blind exactly where D-40 concentrated the text.

\paragraph{A second blind spot the brief did not name.}
Enumeration again found more than the scope described. \textbf{Remediation text was invisible to every definition.} A site's remediation is a string literal passed to \texttt{raise\_*\_violation(...)}, which is an \texttt{ast.Call}, not an \texttt{ast.Raise} --- so no \texttt{raise}-statement scan of any strictness could ever have reached it. Under D-40 the remediation is the only part a site may vary, which makes it the part most likely to acquire an em~dash from a copy-paste. It is now scanned as its own population.

\paragraph{Three populations, and the coverage the gate reports about itself.}
\begin{enumerate}
\tightlist
\item Literals inside \texttt{raise} statements (A-26's original scan, unchanged).
\item Literals at \texttt{raise\_*()} call sites --- the remediation clauses.
\item \texttt{*\_invariant\_clause} functions, \emph{evaluated} and their return values checked.
\end{enumerate}
Population~3 is discovered by introspection over \texttt{bstpp.config} rather than a hard-coded list, so a clause added for \texttt{ModelConfig} or \texttt{PriorConfig} in WP2--WP5 is covered on the day it lands, not the day someone remembers this file. Sample arguments are keyed by parameter name and chosen to fill every interpolated slot: a clause called with empty slots proves nothing about the slots. \textbf{There is deliberately no fallback value.} A clause with an unrecognised parameter is reported as \textsc{unevaluated} and \textbf{fails the gate}, because a skipped clause is an uncovered clause.

The sweep now prints its coverage as a fraction of raise sites rather than a bare \textsc{pass} --- D-39 applied to a gate. At this tip: \textbf{183/184 (99\%)}, with the single residual blind spot \emph{named in the output} (\texttt{data\_contracts.py:489}, whose message is assembled at runtime by \texttt{report.summary()}).

\paragraph{Demonstrated to discriminate, not merely to pass.}
Three mutations, each a defect the A-26 sweep passed over, each restored afterwards and the production files verified byte-identical to their pre-demo state (\texttt{results/\allowbreak{}\_a29\_sweep\_discrimination.txt}):
\begin{enumerate}
\tightlist
\item a non-ASCII hyphen inside \texttt{builder\_max\_sigma\_invariant\_clause} $\rightarrow$ \texttt{FAIL}, \texttt{EXIT:1};
\item an em~dash in a remediation literal at a \texttt{raise\_*()} call site $\rightarrow$ \texttt{FAIL}, \texttt{EXIT:1};
\item a new clause function with a parameter the sweep has no sample for $\rightarrow$ \texttt{FAIL} as \textsc{unevaluated}, \texttt{EXIT:1}.
\end{enumerate}
The first capture of this demo recorded \texttt{EXIT:0} beside each \texttt{FAIL}, because the exit code came from the \texttt{tail} at the end of a pipeline rather than from the sweep. It was rerun capturing the sweep's own status before any pipe. Recorded because a capture that reports success next to a failure is the precise artifact this register exists to prevent, and it was produced here by accident in the act of proving the opposite.

\paragraph{OP-22 --- interpolation is a hazard the gate reports and does not own.}
A non-ASCII character can reach a message through \emph{interpolation} as easily as through a literal, so the sweep probes each slot with a non-ASCII value and reports which clauses propagate it. Three do: \texttt{rectangle\_bounds\_invariant\_clause} (both bounds) and \texttt{support\_mode\_invariant\_clause}, all rendering a caller-supplied value with \texttt{!r} and no numeric coercion. The remaining slots are protected \emph{by} the \texttt{float()} in the clause, which the probe confirms by observing the coercion refuse the string. \textbf{This is reported, not gated:} a caller's own value is not the clause's to control, and failing on it would make the gate reject valid code. Rendering those slots with \texttt{ascii()} instead of \texttt{repr()} would close it, which is a production change and belongs with the D-40 owners; recorded as \textbf{OP-22} and routed to \textbf{WP2}. Note the slot-wise probe was itself a correction: an earlier version substituted only where the sample was already a \texttt{str} and therefore missed \texttt{rectangle\_bounds\_invariant\_clause} entirely, whose bounds are typed \texttt{object} but sampled as floats.

\paragraph{Gates.}
Sweep \textbf{PASS}, \texttt{EXIT:0}, 183/184 (99\%), 7 clause functions evaluated, all ASCII (\texttt{results/\allowbreak{}\_a29\_ascii\_sweep.txt}). \texttt{ruff} on touch \texttt{EXIT:0} (\texttt{results/\allowbreak{}\_a29\_ruff.txt}). \texttt{\textbackslash hypertarget} \textbf{29/29}. Content checks \textbf{PASS}, 40 rows D-1 to D-40 --- \emph{no decision number allocated}, since a gate gaining coverage establishes nothing new. Unreachable-citation sweep \textbf{PASS}. \textbf{Pins and the suite are recorded as not run}, with the enumeration that licenses it under A-24: the diff touches \texttt{results/\allowbreak{}\_a26\_ascii\_sweep.py}, its captures, and this register --- no file under \texttt{bstpp/} or \texttt{tests/}, no \texttt{pyproject.toml}, no pin harness or baseline, and no \texttt{covering\_array\_rows.json}. Production and test bytes are identical to A-28, whose suite run of \textbf{576 passed} therefore stands as the measurement.

\hypertarget{a-30}{%
\subsection{A-30 --- One config-invariant sequence: \texttt{CI-1}\ldots\texttt{CI-6}}\label{a-30}}

\textbf{Opened} 2026-08-05. \textbf{Class} \textbf{DOC/IV} --- labels only. In production the change is confined to comments and docstrings; \textbf{no raised message contains an invariant label}, which was measured rather than assumed before a byte was edited. \textbf{Amends} A-27 and A-28 (their labels). \textbf{Establishes no decision} --- a naming convention is not a decision, and the count stays at D-40. \textbf{Commit} \texttt{WP1.4h}.

\paragraph{The collision.}
A-27 numbered the σ/mode invariants \texttt{I1}--\texttt{I5} and A-28 added \texttt{I6}, into a namespace the Phase-3 \emph{model identities} had occupied since Phase~0 as \texttt{I1}--\texttt{I12}. Matrix \texttt{I6} is ``derived seasonal coordinate''; D-40's \texttt{I6} was ``builder requires \texttt{max\_sigma}''. \texttt{I1}, \texttt{I3}, \texttt{I4} and \texttt{I5} collided the same way. A-28 recorded it; A-30 resolves it.

\paragraph{Which sequence moves, and why it is the config one.}
\begin{enumerate}
\tightlist
\item \textbf{Entrenchment is asymmetric.} \texttt{I1}--\texttt{I12} are cited in the guide, the phase0 archive, \texttt{test\_polygon\_i11\_conservation.py} and several amendments. The config labels were four days old and lived in A-27, A-28, the matrix, two rebaseline records and one test table.
\item \textbf{A ``σ-'' prefix fails exactly where it is needed.} The disambiguator would be a Greek letter, in a package that has just banned non-ASCII from raised messages (D-40) and cannot put one in an identifier at all. \texttt{test\_lane\_b\_sigma\_i6\_\ldots{}} collides with matrix \texttt{I6} again the moment the label reaches a test name or a filename --- \textbf{which is precisely where a collision does damage}, since that is what appears in failure output.
\item \textbf{Four more sequences are coming.} WP2--WP5 add invariants for \texttt{ModelConfig}, \texttt{PriorConfig}, \texttt{PartitionDecoderConfig} and \texttt{InferenceRunConfig}. A σ-specific patch would buy the same conversation four more times.
\end{enumerate}
Hence \textbf{one} config-invariant sequence, extended as each config lands its own, never restarted per object: \texttt{CI-1} rectangle both-or-neither; \texttt{CI-2} polygon requires \texttt{min\_sigma}; \texttt{CI-3} \texttt{min\_sigma} finite and positive; \texttt{CI-4} \texttt{min\_sigma < max\_sigma}; \texttt{CI-5} support-mode validity; \texttt{CI-6} builder requires \texttt{max\_sigma}.

\paragraph{Measured before edited.}
Whether this is a rename or a behaviour change turns on one question: does any \emph{raised message} carry a label? Every \texttt{*\_invariant\_clause} was evaluated and its return value searched for one. \textbf{Zero.} The labels live only in docstrings, comments, the register, the matrix, the rebaseline records, and the Lane~B parametrize table --- so in production this is provably comment-only, and no test assertion changes.

\paragraph{Scope discipline: a global substitution would have corrupted the model identities.}
\texttt{tests/\allowbreak{}test\_identities.py}, \texttt{tests/\allowbreak{}test\_likelihood\_atoms.py}, \texttt{tests/\allowbreak{}test\_clipped\_support.py} and \texttt{bstpp/\allowbreak{}likelihood.py} all use \texttt{I1}--\texttt{I6} in the \emph{model-identity} sense, as do rows and notes inside the matrix and all of Part~I. The substitution was therefore scoped per file, and per \emph{line range} in the two mixed files: in this register only lines between the A-27 and A-29 anchors were touched, and in the matrix only the OP-21 row and the collision note. The model-identity rows and Part~I's eleven labels are byte-identical, which is asserted by the citation/label check rather than eyeballed.

\paragraph{Supersession recorded once, not forty-four times.}
The mechanism proposed for this work was \texttt{\textbackslash supsd\{A-30\}\{I1\}} per occurrence. There are \textbf{44} occurrences across A-27 and A-28, and 44 strikethroughs would have destroyed the readability of the arguments they sit inside --- the A-27 owners-by-quantity table and the A-28 family argument are dense enough already. Instead each subsection carries \emph{one} \texttt{\textbackslash supsd} note at its head declaring the whole sequence renumbered, and the body reads with the new labels. The two rebaseline records carry the equivalent note. \textbf{Rewriting landed text in place is a departure from the register's append-only habit and is called out here rather than left to be noticed.}

\paragraph{Test changes are renames only.}
The Lane~B parametrize table's row labels change (\texttt{"I1 rectangle both-or-neither"} $\rightarrow$ \texttt{"CI-1 \ldots{}"}), which changes the generated test \textsc{id}s, and label mentions inside docstrings and assertion messages change with them. \textbf{No assertion, no argument, and no expected value is touched}, and no test function is renamed --- the function names never carried a number (\texttt{test\_lane\_b\_sigma\_mode\_error\_identity\_is\_owner\_invariant} and friends), so nothing there collided. The phrase ``σ/mode family'' is retained in prose where it describes the invariants' subject matter, since it collides with nothing; only the \emph{labels} moved.

\paragraph{The behaviour claim is checked, not asserted.}
``Labels only'' is the kind of claim that is believed rather than verified, so it is mechanised in \texttt{results/\allowbreak{}\_a30\_ast\_equivalence.py}. Each touched module is parsed before and after, docstrings are stripped, and the ASTs are compared --- comments never reach an AST at all, so an identical dump means identical executable content. \textbf{Production is \textsc{identical}} for both \texttt{config.py} and \texttt{polygon\_mass.py}. The Lane~B file legitimately \emph{does} differ, because parametrize labels and assertion messages are real string constants; the claim there is the narrower one, and it is checked by blanking every string constant and requiring the remaining structure to match --- \textbf{it does}, so no assertion, call, argument or control-flow node moved. Two claims of different strength, each checked at its own strength rather than both waved through under the weaker word ``rename''.

A second check, \texttt{results/\allowbreak{}\_a30\_label\_check.py}, confirms that no \texttt{I1}--\texttt{I6} reference to the config sequence survives, that the model-identity labels in \texttt{likelihood.py} and the three identity test modules are \emph{count-for-count identical to} \texttt{426d60a}, that the matrix's identity rows are still present, and that \texttt{CI-1}--\texttt{CI-6} are all in use. It carries one explicit exemption: the supersession notes quote the old labels on purpose, so blockquoted lines are skipped --- recorded in the output rather than hidden in a regex.

\paragraph{Gates.}
Full profile, because the diff touches \texttt{bstpp/}: \texttt{pytest tests/} \textbf{576 passed, 2 skipped, 1 xfailed}, \texttt{EXIT:0} (\texttt{results/\allowbreak{}\_a30\_suite.txt}) --- \emph{identical} to A-28's count, which is what a labels-only change must produce, and the collection is unchanged at 579 because no test was added or removed. 29m39s, consistent with the 27m05s and 26m22s recorded earlier in this session under the same memory pressure. \textbf{\texttt{PIN\_DIFFS 0 MATCH}} on all four configurations (\texttt{results/\allowbreak{}\_a30\_pins.txt}) --- comments cannot move a pin, and the run is also the check that the substitution script touched no code it was not meant to; \texttt{ruff} on touch \texttt{EXIT:0}; A-29 ASCII sweep \textbf{PASS}, 183/184; \texttt{\textbackslash hypertarget} \textbf{30/30}; content checks \textbf{PASS}, 40 rows D-1 to D-40, no decision allocated; unreachable-citation sweep \textbf{PASS}; AST equivalence and label checks as above.

\hypertarget{a-31}{%
\subsection{A-31 --- The evidence-and-provenance standard}\label{a-31}}

\textbf{Opened} 2026-08-05. \textbf{Class} \textbf{DOC}. \textbf{Establishes} D-41. \textbf{Amends} A-30 (records the relabeling precedent it relied on). \textbf{Governs} every enforcement commit for the remainder of 3f. \textbf{Commit} \texttt{WP1.4i}.

D-41 is written from four things that went wrong or nearly went wrong inside A-28 to A-30, three of them in the act of producing evidence \emph{for} those commits. That is the point: each clause below is a rule the register already needed, discovered by violating it.

\paragraph{Enforcement clauses.}
\begin{enumerate}
\tightlist
\item \textbf{Red before claimed.} Every enforcement row is demonstrated \textsc{red} against the pre-change state before it is claimed to enforce anything, and the capture is committed. A row never observed failing is a row whose discriminating power is a hypothesis.
\item \textbf{The revert is minimal.} Revert only what the row is meant to detect. A-28's rows would have been trivially ``red'' had \texttt{config.py} been reverted alongside \texttt{polygon\_mass.py} --- but the redness would have been an \texttt{ImportError} at collection, which proves nothing about the defect and would have passed unnoticed as evidence. Holding the shared clause API at candidate while reverting only the defect site is the general form.
\item \textbf{Both-sides passes are labelled, never offered.} A row that passes on both sides of a change is recorded as \emph{non-discriminating by construction}, with the reason. A-28's precedence row and seven of A-27's are of this kind. They are worth keeping and worthless as evidence, and the register must not blur the two.
\item \textbf{Captures record the checked process's own exit status.} Not a pipeline's. A-29's discrimination demo first recorded \texttt{EXIT:0} beside each \texttt{FAIL} because the status came from a trailing \texttt{tail}. \textbf{A capture showing success beside a failure is worse than no capture}, because it is evidence pointing the wrong way.
\end{enumerate}

\paragraph{Provenance clauses, and the gap they close.}
A-25 closed the case where the \emph{working tree is larger than the commit's claim} --- captures that describe untracked files. \textbf{A-31 closes its dual, which nothing in the profile could see:} at A-28 a commit landed containing \emph{no production code at all} while every gate was green, because the gates ran on the worktree and the commit was a strict subset of it. The staging command carried one non-existent pathspec, which aborts \texttt{git add} \emph{entirely}; its \texttt{stderr} had been routed to \texttt{/dev/null}; and the following \texttt{git status} showed \texttt{" M"} (modified, unstaged) which was read as staged. Three small things, and the result was a commit whose message described changes it did not contain. It was caught only because a later restore check produced a diff that should have been empty.
\begin{enumerate}
\tightlist
\setcounter{enumi}{4}
\item \textbf{Post-commit verification.} After committing, \texttt{git status -{}-porcelain} is empty \emph{and} \texttt{git show -{}-stat HEAD} is compared against the file list stated \emph{before} the commit was made. \textbf{A commit whose contents were not compared to a pre-stated list is ungated, regardless of what the gates said.} Gate results attach to a tree; a commit is a claim about a subset of it, and only this comparison connects the two.
\item \textbf{Never route \texttt{stderr} to \texttt{/dev/null} on a git command.} Specific, cheap, and it is precisely what turned an aborted \texttt{add} into a silent one.
\end{enumerate}

\paragraph{Corollary: relabeling in place (records A-30's precedent).}
Append-only protects the record of \emph{what was decided}, not the notation used to refer to it. \textbf{Relabeling in place is permitted where no assertion changes and the head note carries the complete old$\rightarrow$new mapping}; anything that changes an assertion uses \texttt{\textbackslash supsd} per occurrence. The mapping requirement is not decoration --- it is what keeps an external citation of an old label resolvable, which is the property append-only was protecting in the first place. Recorded here so it is not re-litigated at WP3, when \texttt{PartitionDecoderConfig} adds its own invariants.

\paragraph{And the standard of proof for a rename.}
A-30 also sets the pattern for any future rename, which D-41 adopts by reference rather than restating: \textbf{claims of different strength are checked at their own strength}. Production, where labels live only in comments and docstrings, is held to \emph{AST-identical}. Test files, where labels are executable string constants, are held to the weaker and correct claim --- blank every string constant and require the remaining structure to match, which is what ``no assertion was touched'' actually means. Asserting the strong claim over both would have been false; asserting the weak claim over both would have been slack.

\paragraph{A note on the artifact that measured this.}
\texttt{results/\allowbreak{}\_a30\_ast\_equivalence.py} originally took its baseline as \texttt{HEAD}. Run before A-30 was committed that was correct; re-run afterwards it silently compared the commit to itself and reported a \textsc{pass} that measured nothing --- production still ``identical'', the test file now reporting \texttt{strings differ: False}. It is now pinned to the A-29 hash. \textbf{An artifact whose meaning depends on when it is run is not an artifact}, and this one is recorded because it failed that test in the same session that wrote clause~4 about captures pointing the wrong way.

\paragraph{Gates.}
Documentation-only under A-24, and the enumeration that licenses it: the diff touches \texttt{phase3\_record.tex}, \texttt{refactor-patches/\allowbreak{}pre-3f-stabilization/\allowbreak{}traceability\_matrix.md} and \texttt{results/} captures --- no file under \texttt{bstpp/} or \texttt{tests/}, no \texttt{pyproject.toml}, no pin harness or baseline, no \texttt{covering\_array\_rows.json}. \textbf{Pins and suite are recorded as not run}, with that enumeration as the reason; production and test bytes are identical to A-30, whose \textbf{576 passed} and \textbf{\texttt{PIN\_DIFFS 0 MATCH}} stand as the measurements. Gates that did run: \texttt{\textbackslash hypertarget} \textbf{31/31}; content checks \textbf{PASS} with \textbf{41 rows, D-1 to D-41}, strictly increasing, no duplicates or gaps --- the first exercise of the monotonicity check A-27 added against a genuinely new row; unreachable-citation sweep \textbf{PASS}. Each capture carries \texttt{git status -{}-porcelain}, and this commit is verified under its own clause~5.

\end{document}
